# Assumptions

What the contact-first program assumes but could challenge. Each entry says what is assumed, why it is held without proof, what
rests on it and how it could be challenged, with its status and a dated history. Epics can be dedicated to challenging an entry,
and their outcomes are recorded here.

The register was started on 2026-11-07 at the owner's request: "These are things that we're assuming, but that can be challenged.
We can have epics dedicated to challenging assumptions. This list will help us keep track of what we're assuming." It adds no
evidence: results and their status come from the records each entry links.

## The rules

- **What counts.** A decision taken by default rather than proved: a reading adopted where another was available, a scope or
  measure chosen without comparing the alternatives, a premise that a line of work builds on before it is proved, or a question
  the owner records as an assumption instead of deciding it. A theorem is not an assumption, but the readings it is proved under
  can be.
- **Changes.** Any line of work may add an entry or change a status, and reports the change to the owner. An assumption that is
  part of the canonical knot test or the foundational thesis changes only through a canonical correction request to the owner. IDs
  are never reused, and entries are never deleted.
- **Challenges.** An epic dedicated to an assumption marks it Under challenge when it starts. When the epic closes, the entry
  becomes Confirmed or Overturned, or returns to Assumed, with a history line naming the epic.
- **Overturned.** Every published claim that rested on an overturned assumption is corrected, and the correction is recorded.

## Reading an entry

- **The status line** gives the status, the date it was set, and the kind.
- **Statement.** What is assumed, precisely enough to be tested.
- **Why.** Why it is held without proof: who adopted it, when, and on what evidence.
- **Rests on it.** The results, readings and lines of work that would change if it fell.
- **Challenge.** The alternative, the cheapest test that could tell them apart, and what would confirm or overturn it. For a
  settled entry, the challenge that settled it.
- **History.** Dated lines, newest first: added, challenged, confirmed, overturned, reworded.

## Statuses

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| **Assumed** | In force: work proceeds as if it were true, and no epic is testing it. |
| **Under challenge** | An epic dedicated to testing it is open, and the history names it. Work may still rely on it. |
| **Confirmed** | A challenge ended without overturning it: the claim was proved, or the alternative was mapped and the assumption kept for stated reasons. It can be reopened. |
| **Overturned** | A challenge showed it false or replaced it. The entry says what replaced it. |

## Kinds

| Kind | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| **premise** | Part of the program's thesis or its canonical test: the theory under test. |
| **reading** | One way of reading a definition where another was available. |
| **scope** | What the program counts or studies, and what it sets aside. |
| **measure** | How the program measures a quantity it reports. |
| **working premise** | A claim that a line of work built on before it was proved. |
| **interpretation** | A philosophical reading that no proof or experiment can settle. |
| **scaffold** | A reading, weighting, start or rule supplied by a study. Compare an alternative before attributing a result to the model. |

## Entries

### A1 · A native knot on an infinite system is also natively bound

**Assumed** · 2026-11-07 · reading

- **Statement.** A knot S of an infinite system is natively bound at a solution x when S is also a knot of the closure of x's
  homoclinic class: the solutions that differ from x in finitely many places. A knot that is native in every variable (A9) is
  assumed to be natively bound as well. Each knot claim on an infinite system records whether the knot is natively bound, beside
  its K1–K3 verdict and the source of each distinction, and claims of distance, locality or spacing there use natively bound knots.
- **Why.** Native variables do not say where a knot's binding comes from. In the law with Ledrappier's rule on x, z free and
  y = x + z, every variable is flipped by a finite move, yet for every k a six-variable knot joins points 2^k apart, bound through
  the hidden field x, whose openness comes only from the ends
  ([proof](../proofs/LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001A/LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001A-P001_rulers_without_ends/proof.md), Proposition 5).
  Requiring native binding makes every native knot of a parity law chained, so bond distance is a ruler in every parity law
  (Proposition 2), and it adds no primitive: it reuses the finite moves that already define nativity. The logic-axis epic
  recommended it, and the owner chose to record it as an assumption rather than decide it
  ([request](../research/LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001/canonical_correction_request.md)).
- **Rests on it.** Every reading of native knots on infinite systems as local, and every claim of distance, locality or spacing
  there (question 9 of [the knot intuition](KNOT_INTUITION.md#open-questions)). Earlier verdicts keep their labels: Tseitin's
  law and the gauge law on the square grid have no ends, so their native knots are natively bound, and Ledrappier's law has no
  native knot either way. The long knots of the law above are native in every variable but not natively bound.
- **Challenge.** The alternative keeps nativity per variable and has each claim about distance or locality state only whether
  the law has ends. Whether natively bound knots are always chained is open beyond parity laws. A non-linear law whose natively
  bound knots join far points without a chain would remove the reason for the assumption, and so would a law in which the label
  discards knots that every other test treats as emergence.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-07 · Recorded as an assumption at the owner's request, instead of a decision on the request. The canonical text of
    the source condition is unchanged.
  - 2026-11-07 · Raised as a decision request by `LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001`, which recommended it.

### A2 · First emergence is a logical knot

**Assumed** · 2026-07-24 · premise

- **Statement.** The first structure that logic forms without presupposing space is a knot: loops that each leave a distinction
  open, bound through one contact so that the whole is constrained and every part is free (K1–K3). One knot is the first atomic
  logical topology and the minimal contact-distance. Paths, networks and extended distance are built from many.
- **Why.** It is the program's working theory, protected by the
  [foundational thesis](FOUNDATIONAL_THESIS.md#emergence-atomic-topology-and-distance). It is not a theorem: the theorems say
  which relations are knots and which constraint languages define them (A16), not that knots are where emergence begins.
- **Rests on it.** The program's reading of its results: a result about knots is read as a result about emergence, space or
  hiddenness because of this premise.
- **Challenge.** Look for structure the program would count as emergent that no knot carries. Known places to look: competing
  pairs make knots among their least-violation states (A8), pairs alone make a knot on three-valued distinctions (A10), and
  trends at infinity are invisible to the knot test
  ([holes at infinity](../research/HOLES-AT-INFINITY-20261020-001/holes_at_infinity.md)). A successful challenge goes to the
  owner as a canonical correction request.
- **History.**
  - 2026-10-19 · Made precise by the canonical knot test K1–K3, adopted by the owner.
  - 2026-07-24 · Stated in the workspace's first commit.

### A3 · A verdict depends only on the allowed states

**Assumed** · 2026-10-19 · premise

- **Statement.** A system of constraints is read as the set of joint states it allows, with witness variables quantified away.
  Whether it contains a knot depends only on that set, never on how the constraints are written or the order in which they are
  checked, and every map that preserves the allowed states preserves the verdict.
- **Why.** Adopted with the [canonical knot test](FOUNDATIONAL_THESIS.md#canonical-knot-test) after an earlier proof's knot
  turned out to depend on its executor: cross-wired two-clause guards act like a knot only if each loop's clauses are checked
  only while that loop runs.
- **Rests on it.** Every K1–K3 verdict, the knot laboratory, the census of 34 two-loop knots, and the theorem that no 2-CNF
  defines a knot (A16).
- **Challenge.** On infinite systems the source-aware reading (A9) already lets verdicts depend on more than the projected
  relation: on how the system's solutions are joined by finite changes. A challenge would ask whether something the projection
  forgets, such as a growth order (A14) or the way solutions connect, ever carries emergence that the allowed states miss.
- **History.**
  - 2026-10-19 · Adopted with K1–K3 as the canonical test.

### A4 · Nothing outside the constraints supplies an input

**Assumed** · 2026-10-19 · premise

- **Statement.** No executor, scheduler or checker may supply an input to a system. A constraint that should hold only while a
  loop runs carries the loop's presence as a literal, and formation order alone is not physical time.
- **Why.** Adopted with the [canonical knot test](FOUNDATIONAL_THESIS.md#canonical-knot-test). An earlier proof's executor
  checked a loop's clauses only while the loop ran, which supplied each loop's presence as a hidden third input and made a common
  hub of two-clause guards pass for a knot.
- **Rests on it.** The negative result for 2-CNF, which no evaluation rule can evade, and every growth result: a growth result
  belongs to the law only if it holds in every growth order (A14).
- **Challenge.** Question 4 of [the knot intuition](KNOT_INTUITION.md#open-questions) asks whether loops are better modelled as
  processes. Write a growth process as one law on space and time, and ask whether it has knots that no static law has and
  whether its order can be a variable of the law rather than an input.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-07 · Its growth clause tested by `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001A`. A growth order written as variables of the law never
    knots with the world's states, and existence enters as the presence literal this premise prescribes. A rule that reads a
    state does knot the order to the states, which is the input it forbids. The status is unchanged, since this is a premise.
  - 2026-10-19 · Adopted with K1–K3 as the canonical test.

### A5 · A loop is anything that leaves a distinction open

**Assumed** · 2026-10-19 · premise

- **Statement.** The thesis asks for two independently witnessed native recurrences. The knot test reads a recurrence as
  whatever leaves a distinction open: a variable, or a named pattern such as a parity, that the structure leaves free. A loop's
  presence is not a variable.
- **Why.** Settled in form with the canonical test: K1–K3 applies to open distinctions, and which structures count as native
  loops is interpretive (question 1 of [the knot intuition](KNOT_INTUITION.md#open-questions)).
- **Rests on it.** Every knot claim, since K1–K3 is checked on distinctions, not on loops.
- **Challenge.** Question 1 asks which structures should count as native loops, and it is open. Infinite systems showed that
  the source can matter: an end can open a distinction that no loop opens, and the knot test cannot tell the two apart, which
  led to A9. A challenge would look for open distinctions of different kinds in finite systems and ask whether the test should
  separate them.
- **History.**
  - 2026-10-20 · Refined for infinite systems by the source-aware reading (A9).
  - 2026-10-19 · Settled in form with the canonical test; the question of where presence comes from was retired.

### A6 · Erasure is forgetting

**Assumed** · 2026-10-19 · reading

- **Statement.** Erasing a loop forgets its distinction, so that nothing constrains it any more. A knot is then constrained as a
  whole and unconstrained in every part: forgetting any one variable, a loop or the contact, leaves the rest unconstrained (K2).
- **Why.** The owner chose it on 2026-10-19
  ([decision](../research/KNOT-FOUNDATION-20261019-001/canonical_correction_request.md)). Erasure entered the agents' gate in the
  workspace's first commit as a test of decomposability, which is the forgetting reading, and the program began from parity
  patterns, which are knots only under forgetting.
- **Rests on it.** Parity as the knot that hides the most, the 34 two-loop knots in five classes, and every result about parity's
  knots since 2026-10-19.
- **Challenge.** The absence reading (A17) keeps only the atom. Every knot it accepts is a knot under forgetting, and both
  readings exclude the common hub and every 2-CNF pattern, so a challenge needs a question whose answer changes between them.
  The knot laboratory keeps both readings.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-07 · Used by `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001A`. With the variables of absent sites and pairs forgotten, no knot joins a
    growth order to the world's states. Setting them to 0 instead makes order bits carry existence, and they then knot with the
    states.
  - 2026-10-19 · Chosen by the owner, replacing the absence reading adopted earlier the same day (A17).

### A7 · Witnesses are existential

**Assumed** · 2026-10-19 · reading

- **Statement.** A state is allowed when some assignment of the witnesses satisfies every constraint. Witnesses carry no signs,
  so they cannot cancel.
- **Why.** The canonical reading since the test was adopted. On 2026-10-29 the signed reading, in which a state is allowed when
  its signed witness sum is nonzero, was adopted beside it under the owner's rule for forks between readings, and later results
  record both verdicts ([adoption](../research/INTERFERENCE-20261029-001/canonical_correction_request.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The two/three boundary as stated (A16): with signed witnesses every three-bit knot is one cancelling witness
  away from pairs, so the boundary becomes a boundary of positivity. Also "only parity survives native patterns of patterns"
  (`GEOMETRY-LAW-20261029-001`), which the signed reading changes: native gadgets then rebuild 14 of the 16 knot classes.
- **Challenge.** Both readings are mapped side by side. A challenge would ask which one the program's questions need, for
  example whether cancelling witnesses build a space that no positive law builds. Interference reproduces knots on finitely
  ramified, tree-like space but not on genuine two-dimensional space (`SIGNED-SUBDIVISION-20261029-001`).
- **History.**
  - 2026-10-29 · The signed reading adopted beside it, reopenable (`INTERFERENCE-20261029-001`).
  - 2026-10-19 · Adopted with the canonical test.

### A8 · Allowed means every constraint holds

**Assumed** · 2026-10-19 · reading

- **Statement.** A state is allowed only when it satisfies every constraint (the strict reading). When the constraints cannot
  all hold, the states that break the fewest are not allowed for that reason.
- **Why.** The strict reading keeps verdicts independent of presentation and gives pairs no knots. The competition reading
  needs a declared measure of violation. Both were mapped on 2026-10-19 and the choice was left open
  ([competing pairs](../research/KNOT-COMPETITION-20261019-001/competition.md); question 10 of
  [the knot intuition](KNOT_INTUITION.md#open-questions)).
- **Rests on it.** The theorem that pairs never make a knot, in its strict form (A16).
- **Challenge.** Under the competition reading, frustrated pairs make exactly one kind of knot, the not-all-equal triangle, and
  competition turns a triangulated layout into a chain of knots but does not make the layout. A challenge would adopt it as a
  second reading, as signed witnesses were, and record both verdicts.
- **History.**
  - 2026-10-19 · Both readings mapped (`KNOT-COMPETITION-20261019-001`); the strict reading kept and the choice left open.

### A9 · Native knots are opened by finite changes

**Assumed** · 2026-10-20 · reading

- **Statement.** On an infinite system, a knot is native at a solution only when each of its variables is flipped there by some
  change of finitely many variables. Every knot claim on an infinite system records its K1–K3 verdict and the source of each
  distinction: loop-supplied, end-supplied or fixed. Finite systems are unaffected.
- **Why.** An infinite system's ends can open distinctions that no loop opens, and K1–K3 cannot tell the two apart: the
  3-regular tree has a knot at every vertex and no loop at all
  ([holes at infinity](../research/HOLES-AT-INFINITY-20261020-001/holes_at_infinity.md)). Asked which reading gives more
  interesting results, the owner adopted the source-aware one, reopenable. Under it emergence depends on the law and on the
  solution ([frozen worlds](../research/FROZEN-WORLDS-20261020-001/frozen_worlds.md);
  [adoption](../research/HOLES-AT-INFINITY-20261020-001/canonical_correction_request.md)).
- **Rests on it.** Frozen and flexible worlds: a linear law is native everywhere or nowhere, colourings have frozen worlds
  without native knots, and Ledrappier's law has knots but no native knot. A1 adds a requirement to it.
- **Challenge.** The source-blind reading counts end-supplied distinctions too. Both verdicts are recorded for every claim, so
  reopening needs no new derivation. A challenge would look for a result that the source-blind reading makes more interesting.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-07 · Strengthened by A1 (natively bound).
  - 2026-10-20 · Adopted by the owner, reopenable.

### A10 · The base distinction is a bit

**Assumed** · 2026-10-21 · scope

- **Statement.** A distinction has two values. The thesis is the boundary between what clauses of two and of three literals can
  express, clauses are over bits, and laws over larger domains are a different question, not a test of the thesis.
- **Why.** The owner's statement of the thesis on 2026-10-21. The base-three epic found that the threshold is three somewhere:
  three places on bits, or three values on pairs. The two/three boundary is fundamental exactly when the base distinction is a
  bit ([base three](../research/BASE-THREE-20261021-001/base_three.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The two/three boundary as the boundary of emergence (A16), and every census of knots on bits.
- **Challenge.** On three-valued distinctions pairs alone make a knot: a hidden vertex adjacent to three visible ones, under
  colouring constraints, makes "not all different" ([Gallai knots](../research/GALLAI-KNOTS-20261020-001/gallai_knots.md)). A
  challenge would ask whether the bit is forced, for example whether a three-valued distinction written natively on bits needs a
  three-place knot, which would move the boundary back to three places. The base-three epic proposed mapping all 512 binary
  relations on three values; that was deferred.
- **History.**
  - 2026-10-21 · Stated by the owner after the base-three epic.

### A11 · Hubs are a declared substrate, never contact

**Assumed** · 2026-10-29 · scope

- **Statement.** Native contact shares each distinction between exactly two knots. A distinction shared by three or more knots
  is a hub: equality fan-out, which is pairwise. Hub laws are in scope as a declared substrate, but a hub is never counted as
  contact or emergence.
- **Why.** The canonical common hub, two attachments A-k and B-k, fails K2, and a distinction in three or more knots ties its
  copies by equality. On 2026-10-29 the owner put hub laws in scope
  ([hub survivors](../research/HUB-SURVIVORS-20261029-001/hub_survivors.md)).
- **Rests on it.** Surfaces glued natively, with every shared distinction in exactly two knots (`SURFACE-GLUING-20261024-001`),
  and every result that compares a law with and without hubs.
- **Challenge.** With hubs, emergence beyond parity persists across scales (`HUB-SURVIVORS-20261029-001`). A challenge would
  ask whether some sharing among three or more knots is native after all, for example whether a law can form its own fan-out
  without an equality.
- **History.**
  - 2026-10-29 · Hub laws put in scope by the owner, as a declared substrate.

### A12 · Bond distance is a law's own spacing

**Overturned** · 2026-11-08 · measure

- **Statement.** The spacing a law sets between two distinctions is their bond distance: the size of the least knot through
  both, minus one, read from the allowed states alone. Dimension is read from how fast bond balls grow, and a circle is the
  large-scale outline of a bond ball.
- **Why.** It uses nothing but knots and allowed states, so no metric, coordinate or drawing is put in. On an endless chain of atoms it
  counts the loops between two distinctions, one unit per loop, which matches the owner's reading of the integers' spacing as space
  ([logic axis](../research/LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001/logic_axis.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The results on space from a law since 2026-11-07: the endless chain (the literature's number line), the taxicab plane, the circles of
  periodic and pinwheel laws, and dimension read from ball growth (question 9).
- **Challenge.** Other measures can be read from allowed states, such as the shortest chain of knots joining two distinctions,
  each sharing a distinction with the next. On knot graphs that are trees bond distance is the number of knots on the path plus
  one, so the two agree there, but they need not agree elsewhere. A challenge would compare them on the laws already measured
  and ask whether any dimension or circle changes.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Overturned as a universal statement by the test `BOND-CHAIN-20261108-001` froze before running: in the NAND
    thread, clauses not(s(t−1) ∧ a(t) ∧ s(t)), chain distance reads a number line and bond distance none, since no knot crosses a
    gate (a value of a shared distinction that satisfies both knots). It holds wherever knots compose: on every parity law chains
    counted in knots collapse to one step and counted in distinctions equal bond distance (proved), and every law measured for
    space so far composes, so nothing under Rests on it changes. Replaced by A39
    ([bond and chain distance](../research/BOND-CHAIN-20261108-001/bond_chain.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Under challenge by `BOND-CHAIN-20261108-001`: chain distance counted in knots and in distinctions, against bond
    distance, on parity laws and on non-matroidal laws.
  - 2026-11-07 · Used as the measure of space from a law (`LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001`).
  - 2026-10-21 · Defined from the allowed states of a declared substrate (`NATIVE-NETWORK-20261021-001`).

### A13 · Witness variables are part of a law, not a drawing

**Assumed** · 2026-11-07 · reading

- **Statement.** A uniform local law may use hidden witness variables, quantified away, and a space it builds with them is still
  built with nothing drawn. The number of hidden bits is reported with the result.
- **Why.** The canonical setting quantifies witnesses away (A3), so hidden variables belong to a law's constraints, unlike
  coordinates or positions written in advance.
- **Rests on it.** "With one witness bit per incidence, quantified away, a uniform local law lays out its own round sphere"
  ([sphere growth](../research/SPHERE-GROWTH-20261107-001/sphere_growth.md)), the eleven-label pinwheel rule
  (`PINWHEEL-NATIVE-20261107-001`), and A14.
- **Challenge.** Decorated matching rules force essentially every substitution tiling (Mozes 1989; Goodman-Strauss 1998), so
  with enough labels a law can carry almost any self-similar layout. A challenge would ask which spaces need no hidden bits, how
  the number of bits needed grows with the size, and whether labels that grow with the size are a drawing in disguise.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-07 · Added when the register was started. Witness bits were first used to lay out a space in
    `SPHERE-GROWTH-20261107-001`, and how many bits roundness costs is open.

### A14 · A growing law's history is a hidden witness

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-07 · reading

- **Statement.** A growth result belongs to the law only if it holds in every growth order. Information that a growth process
  keeps from earlier steps, such as a bit carried for one lap, is a hidden witness of the grown pattern and is counted as one.
- **Why.** It follows from A4: a process may not supply an input, so a kept bit must be written into the system as a variable,
  and a variable that is quantified away is a witness. The grow-coin epic applied it: one bit kept for one lap ties a sphere's
  four equator corners together, so growth lays out a new round sphere only with a witness, just as a finished sphere is checked
  round only with one ([grow coin](../research/GROW-COIN-20261107-001/grow_coin.md)).
- **Rests on it.** "Growth does not remove the witness" (`GROW-COIN-20261107-001`), and with it the reading that a law lays out
  a new round sphere only with hidden information; "a beginning is a knot" and the reading of what growth adds
  ([growth as a law](../research/GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001/growth_law.md)).
- **Challenge.** Settled by `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001`, which wrote growth histories into the law as variables and read them
  under K1–K3 ([proof](../proofs/GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001A/GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001A-P001_a_beginning_is_a_knot/proof.md)).
  - **The order adds no knot.** It never knots with the grown pattern's states. Existence enters only as a presence literal.
  - **The history's parts.** Its local part (which neighbour came first) is pairwise and holds no knot. Its global part, time
    without a loop, is a knot on every cycle of the grown pattern, so a beginning is a knot.
  - **What is left to carry.** What a kept history adds beyond bounded local information is only whether a grown pattern has a
    beginning.
  - **The plane.** There label-free growth by the pinwheel's own law left the pinwheel at once
    ([pinwheel growth](../artifacts/GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001B/GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001B-E001_pinwheel_grown_outward/run/interpretation.md)).
  - **For whom.** Confirmed for rules that read only the law, with absent things forgotten. A rule that reads a state is not a
    law in this sense, and fixing absent variables instead of forgetting them lets order bits carry existence.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-07 · Confirmed by `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001` (tickets A to C), for the reasons above.
  - 2026-11-07 · Under challenge: backlog B1, growth as a law, taken up as `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001`.
  - 2026-11-07 · Added when the register was started; first applied by `GROW-COIN-20261107-001`.

### A15 · Every coherent alternative is co-real

**Assumed** · 2026-10-18 · interpretation

- **Statement.** Every internally coherent admissible base, and every world branch it coherently admits, is treated as co-real,
  and nothing selects one of them as uniquely real. Co-reality is not simultaneity, and no contact is inferred across bases.
- **Why.** It is the program's
  [protected interpretation](FOUNDATIONAL_THESIS.md#protected-modal-plurality-interpretation).
- **Rests on it.** How results are worded: a law that leaves alternatives open is not said to fail to choose, and a construction
  is described by what it puts in, not by what it selects.
- **Challenge.** None by proof or experiment. A model-existence or nonselection theorem fits it without proving it, and an
  obstruction to one infinite carrier does not refute it. The entry keeps the assumption visible, so that no result is read as
  evidence for or against it.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Applied to meeting patterns by the owner, status unchanged. On 2026-11-11 (Pacific) he said: "there is no choice.
    I feel like we keep going back to this being a choice. It isn't. It's just random", and of ours: "It is indifferent. It's is
    purely coincidental." So a meeting pattern the record writes down is worded as one sample, under a declared weighting (A71), of
    meeting patterns none of which is chosen, never as a defect; the questions about it are which are typical and which rare.
    Whether a law forces its own meeting pattern stays a question of its own (A62)
    ([Intuitions, lesson 7.3](../INTUITIONS.md#73-every-meeting-pattern-none-chosen)).
  - 2026-10-18 · In the foundational thesis as a protected interpretation, in its earliest committed version.

### A16 · Two-place rules cannot make a knot; a three-place rule can

**Confirmed** · 2026-10-19 · premise

- **Statement.** No system of two-literal clauses, with any number of witnesses, defines a knot, and three-literal clauses with
  witnesses define every knot.
- **Why.** It was the protected arity thesis, first supported by a proof whose executor supplied an input (A4). Under K1–K3 it
  became two theorems about definability ([thesis](FOUNDATIONAL_THESIS.md#protected-cnf-arity-thesis)).
- **Rests on it.** The program's central contrast: two-place relations are logic, and three-place ones carry topology.
- **Challenge.** Confirmed on 2026-10-19: proved in Lean under both readings of erasure
  ([proof](../proofs/KNOT-FOUNDATION-20261019-001E/KNOT-FOUNDATION-20261019-001E-P001_forgetting_reading/)). It holds as stated
  under existential witnesses (A7), the strict reading (A8) and bits (A10): signed witnesses turn it into a boundary of
  positivity, competing pairs make the not-all-equal knot, and pairs on three values make "not all different".
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-26 · Retitled at the owner's word, together with lesson 3.2 of the guide. The title was "Pairs cannot make a knot; three places can", which read as if two loops could not make a knot. The statement is unchanged.
  - 2026-10-29 · Restated under signed witnesses as a boundary of positivity (`INTERFERENCE-20261029-001`).
  - 2026-10-19 · Confirmed: proved under K1–K3, in Lean.
  - 2026-07-24 · Stated in the workspace's first commit as the protected arity thesis.

### A17 · Erasure is absence

**Overturned** · 2026-10-19 · reading

- **Statement.** Each loop's variable is its presence, and erasing a loop sets it to absent. A knot then constrains the contact
  when every loop is present and constrains nothing when any loop is absent.
- **Why.** It came from the first formalization of the knot test on 2026-10-19, not from the original gate, and was adopted that
  morning.
- **Rests on it.** Under it only the atom, the single clause "not A or not B or c", is a knot, parity is not, and the doctrine
  said that a parity pattern is never a knot.
- **Challenge.** Overturned the same day, when the owner chose forgetting (A6): the original gate used erasure as a test of
  decomposability, and the program began from parity patterns. Every knot of the absence reading is a knot under forgetting. The
  reading stays available in the knot laboratory and in `KNOT-FOUNDATION-20261019-001A-P001`.
- **History.**
  - 2026-10-19 · Overturned: replaced by forgetting (A6).
  - 2026-10-19 · Adopted with the first formalization of the knot test.

### A18 · The Fano pattern is the knot's only three-regular contradiction

**Overturned** · 2026-10-30 · working premise

- **Statement.** The only gluing in which every shared distinction joins three copies of the knot and on which the not-all-equal
  knot reproduces itself is the Fano pattern (the literature's Fano plane).
- **Why.** Of about 25,000 random connected 3-regular triple systems with 7 to 15 points, the only ones that could not be
  2-coloured were Fano planes ([space selection](../research/SPACE-SELECTION-20261030-001/which_spaces_the_harmony_builds.md)),
  and the next epic was approved with uniqueness as its premise.
- **Rests on it.** The approved form of the three-fold-spaces epic.
- **Challenge.** Overturned by exhaustive search and SAT in that epic: Fano planes splice together without limit (7, 13, 19, …
  triangles), odd cycles of Fano blocks give more, and two twisted 14-point systems give a 27-triangle space
  ([three-fold spaces](../research/THREEFOLD-SPACES-20261030-001/the_knots_three_fold_spaces.md)). The Fano plane is the
  smallest three-fold space, not the only one. The lesson: a random probe is no evidence of uniqueness.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "The Fano plane is the knot's only three-fold space" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-10-30 · Overturned (`THREEFOLD-SPACES-20261030-001`).
  - 2026-10-30 · Adopted as the premise of the three-fold-spaces epic, from the probe of `SPACE-SELECTION-20261030-001`.

### A19 · A gadget's verdict does not depend on how its distinctions are listed

**Overturned** · 2026-11-07 · working premise

- **Statement.** When a far side is glued to a gluing knot, the creation test's verdict does not depend on the order in which
  the far side's distinctions are written.
- **Why.** Three of the four far sides the test used, K4^(3), the Fano plane and census 58, have the symmetry that makes the
  order irrelevant, and the test never varied it.
- **Rests on it.** The split step of the four-step rule that read Fano atoms (`FAR-READ-20261106-001-D002`), and the sentence of
  `FAR-READ-20261106-001-D001` about census 6.
- **Challenge.** Overturned by `GENUS-READ-20261107-001`: census 6 has three inequivalent gluings, and its verdict changed with
  the order in 8,046 of 11,552 sampled pairs. Counted over its gluings a Fano atom is homogeneous, so the split step was not
  native, and both records carry pending qualifications
  ([genus read](../research/GENUS-READ-20261107-001/genus_read.md)). The lesson: a listing order is a choice.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "A gadget's verdict does not depend on how its points are listed" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-07 · Overturned (`GENUS-READ-20261107-001`).
  - 2026-11-06 · Used by `FAR-READ-20261106-001`.

### A20 · A learning point is a localized dissonance

**Assumed** · 2026-11-07 · reading

- **Statement.** In the owner's picture of learning, a state is a point with a strength that moves through a network of knots.
  The program reads the point as a localized dissonance, a violated knot that local moves carry but cannot remove, rather than
  the whole joint state, and its strength as an index that local moves conserve, rather than a persistence time.
- **Why.** These readings fit what the record already has. The knot sphere's contradiction is a vortex charge that can be placed
  through any point and steps locally (`FLAT-STATES-20261107-001`), and violated knots minus agreeing faces count the same in
  every state, so no move removes the forced dissonance (`COUNT-SURFACES-20261107-001`, whose record reads the count as a
  discrete Poincaré–Hopf theorem). They were written down when the owner described the picture, and the direction that would
  test them was scoped and parked.
- **Rests on it.** Only the framing of that parked direction, steering a dissonance by re-signing knots. No result yet.
- **Challenge.** The alternatives read the point as the whole state, or its strength as how long a pattern persists under an
  update law. The parked direction's first step decides between them: on the octahedral knot spheres, change one sign at a time
  and check whether the forced dissonance moves as its index predicts.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-09 · On a torus whose Kasteleyn law is in a class no state satisfies, `LEARN-HANDLES-20261109-001` found the point to be
    a pair of opposite charges, one violated line and one face whose three corners agree, which learning removes only by winding
    around a handle ([learning in handles](../research/LEARN-HANDLES-20261109-001/learn_handles.md)). The point stays localized. Kept as Assumed.
  - 2026-11-07 · The first step ran in `RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001`. The forced dissonance moves exactly as its index predicts:
    one sign change at the violated line splits its index 2 into two even faces of index 1, every such law is satisfiable, and
    the halves rejoin into a violated line anywhere ([steering by re-signing](../research/RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001/resign_steer.md)).
    The point stays localized, a line or two faces. The persistence reading was not tested, and the count of states consistent
    with the law turned out to rank the halves' positions, a second, entropic notion of the strength the owner's words name. Kept
    as Assumed.
  - 2026-11-07 · Added from the readings recorded with the owner's learning picture; none is a result.

### A21 · Learning changes the law, not the state

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-07 · reading

- **Statement.** In the learning picture, orienting the logic means choosing signs, a change of the law rather than of the state.
  Rationalization removes dissonance by changing the law, with a sign or an added witness, rather than the state.
- **Why.** On the knot's surfaces, changing one sign toggles the parity of the two faces at the ends of its side, so the forced
  amount of dissonance changes by at most one, and dissonance would leave the states only by entering the law
  (`COUNT-SURFACES-20261107-001`; a working hypothesis, not yet checked).
- **Rests on it.** The framing of the parked steering direction.
- **Challenge.** The alternative calls a change of state learning, as when a network's connections stay fixed and its activity
  settles. The steering direction's first check, whether one re-signing moves the forced violated knot out of the states once
  two even faces appear, tests the premise of this reading.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-09 · The boundary off the sphere, drawn by `LEARN-HANDLES-20261109-001`: on a torus a law's homology class is what
    learning carries beyond its even faces, and on a class-forced torus every process that removes the dissonance, whatever the
    state does, changes the law around a handle; no change of state does it ([learning in handles](../research/LEARN-HANDLES-20261109-001/learn_handles.md)). Still Confirmed.
  - 2026-11-07 · Confirmed on the sphere, with its boundary drawn, by `RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001`. The premise is proved: on
    every simplicial knot sphere one re-signing at the violated line leaves a satisfiable law with two even faces. The boundary:
    on the sphere a re-signing that keeps the even faces where they are is a change of state in disguise (a gauge), and a closed
    walk of re-signings equals a block change of state. So the part of learning that is not a change of state is exactly where
    the law's even faces sit ([steering by re-signing](../research/RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001/resign_steer.md)). Off the sphere,
    signings with the same even faces also differ by a homology class, which no change of state reaches; that is not yet
    studied.
  - 2026-11-07 · Added from the readings recorded with the owner's learning picture.

### A22 · Many points at once means no scheduler

**Assumed** · 2026-11-07 · reading

- **Statement.** The owner asked for a parallel version of the learning picture. The program reads it as several points updated
  with no scheduler choosing an order, either together or by a rule whose result holds in every order, rather than as a faster
  program.
- **Why.** The canonical test already asks this of every process (A4, A14). The other reading, running the computations in
  parallel, is an implementation choice that changes no result.
- **Rests on it.** The framing of the parked stretch direction, many points at once.
- **Challenge.** If the owner meant parallel computation, the direction reduces to an implementation choice. Otherwise its first
  step places two dissonances on one drawn knot pattern, updates both in every order, and compares the outcomes: points might meet,
  cancel or bind, since violated knots and over-agreeing faces carry opposite indices.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-07 · Added from the readings recorded with the owner's learning picture.

### A23 · A law's local view is its rooted code

**Assumed** · 2026-11-07 · measure

- **Statement.** What a local law sees around a dart u → v within radius r is the dart's rooted code: a breadth-first traversal
  from u, starting at v and turning counterclockwise, that records each discovered vertex's degree and its neighbours' discovery
  numbers out to graph distance r. No labels, coordinates or angles enter it.
- **Why.** It is the code with which `PINWHEEL-NATIVE-20261107-001B` read the pinwheel's labels from its unlabelled map, and the
  atlas epic kept it so that the counts compare
  ([atlas proof](../proofs/PINWHEEL-ATLAS-20261107-001A/PINWHEEL-ATLAS-20261107-001A-P001_the_complete_radius_2_atlas/proof.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The counts of `PINWHEEL-ATLAS-20261107-001`, tickets A, B and D: the 1,168 radius-2 forms, the 198 radius-1
  forms of which 9 carry two labels, the least radius that reads every label, the 96,474 periodic configurations that fail at
  radius 1, and the 3,227 codes of the growing patch.
- **Challenge.** Another local view, such as one built from faces or one that hides the outer face, could change the counts and
  the least radius that reads every label. A challenge would recompute the atlas under a second view and compare.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-07 · Added at the pinwheel line's request; the code was defined in `PINWHEEL-NATIVE-20261107-001B`.

### A24 · The pinwheel law's allowed states fix its planar map

**Assumed** · 2026-11-07 · working premise

- **Statement.** The pinwheel law's allowed states determine its planar map, rotation order included, up to one mirror image of
  the whole, in infinite tilings as in finite patches.
- **Why.** By Whitney's theorems a 3-connected planar graph is determined by its cycle matroid and has only one planar
  embedding. The 3-connectivity was measured only on finite patches at levels 2 to 5, each with an added outside vertex joined to
  its boundary (`PINWHEEL-NATIVE-20261107-001B`), and the atlas proof says it is not re-proved for infinite tilings.
- **Rests on it.** Reading the radius-2 atlas as the law's own rather than the drawing's: the rooted code takes its rotation order
  from the drawing (A23), so the atlas belongs to the law only through this step.
- **Challenge.** Prove that the embedding is unique for infinite pinwheel maps, for example by proving every pinwheel tiling's
  graph 3-connected. A failure would mean that the law fixes the map only patch by patch.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-07 · Added at the pinwheel line's request; first used in `PINWHEEL-NATIVE-20261107-001B`.

### A25 · A local law without hidden states cannot force roundness

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-08 · working premise

- **Statement.** A uniform local law with no hidden states cannot force its spaces to be round: whatever such a law allows on a
  round space, it also allows on some space that is not round.
- **Why.** It is proved for closed spheres: a law without hidden states cannot tell the octahedral knot spheres from long capped
  tubes, whose balls all occur in the sphere ([sphere growth](../research/SPHERE-GROWTH-20261107-001/sphere_growth.md)). The
  sphere line's readings carried it over to space in general.
- **Rests on it.** The reading that roundness costs hidden information, one witness bit per incidence on the spheres, and with
  A13 the reading that a round space is laid out only with hidden bits.
- **Challenge.** The pinwheel tested it in the plane, and it held. The pinwheel's own radius-2 law is a finite list of 1,168 forms
  that reads every label from the unlabelled map, and none of 96,474 periodic configurations obeys it uncut
  ([pinwheel atlas](../research/PINWHEEL-ATLAS-20261107-001/pinwheel_atlas.md)). But a crystal cut by the pinwheel's own
  subdivision does: the rhombus lattice cut once obeys the collar law, and for every radius a further cut obeys the pinwheel's
  undecorated radius-r law, so no such law forces roundness
  ([collar plane](../research/COLLAR-PLANE-20261108-001/collar_plane.md)). It would be reopened by a uniform local law without
  hidden states, in the plane or on a surface, whose allowed spaces are all round.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Bears on it: `ROUND-PRICE-20261108-001`. Labels read from a bounded window of the pinwheel never force it either,
    and one hidden bit per tile, set by a decorated substitution, lets a crystal through in 1,008 of 1,024 cases.
  - 2026-11-08 · Confirmed in the plane for the pinwheel by `COLLAR-PLANE-20261108-001`: a periodic plane obeys its undecorated
    local law at every radius. The atlas epic's reading that the plane might need no hidden bit is corrected
    (`docs/CORRECTIONS.md`).
  - 2026-11-07 · Bears on it: `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001B`. The pinwheel's collar law, one tile around each tile with no hidden
    states, admits collar-legal patches of 500 tiles that are not pinwheel patches, built by forced-first growth. They are finite
    and break the radius-2 law, so this is left open.
  - 2026-11-07 · Challenged in the plane by `PINWHEEL-ATLAS-20261107-001`: no tested periodic plane obeys the pinwheel's law;
    left open.
  - 2026-11-07 · Proved for closed spheres by `SPHERE-GROWTH-20261107-001`.

### A26 · A learner acts only at dissonance while the state holds still

**Overturned** · 2026-11-10 · scope

- **Statement.** In the steering results a learning step changes one sign, and only on a side of a violated knot or on a side
  with an end at an even face, where dissonance can be seen. The state does not change while the law is learned. A target, when
  there is one, is supplied from outside as a graph distance on the triangulation.
- **Why.** `RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001` took it by default, to separate what changing the law can do from what changing the state
  can do. It is the cleanest reading of "learning orients the logic", and it matches the owner's word for the process,
  rationalization: the belief changes and the behaviour does not.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's theorem that re-signing alone carries the dissonance from any knot to any knot with the state fixed,
  and its local rule, which reached 2,399 of 2,400 targets
  ([steering by re-signing](../research/RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001/resign_steer.md)).
- **Challenge.** The alternatives let the state relax by its own local moves between learning steps, or let the learner change any
  sign. The cheapest test runs the same local rule while the state takes one improving step after each sign change, and asks
  whether every target is still reached or whether the state's own moves pull the point elsewhere. A native target signal, such
  as the count of consistent states (A27) or bond distance (A12), in place of the outside distance, is a second test.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Overturned as a limit on what learning reaches by its challenge, `MOVING-STATE-20261110-001`, under the rule frozen before it
    ran: a state that flips one point between learning steps without raising the dissonance freed a stuck learner in 23 of 50 one-atom
    runs and brought 28 of 50 two-atom runs to the floor, where the fixed-state learner made no step; a unit settled at the genus-2 floor
    still wanders only inside its own atom. The results proved or computed with the state held still stand as statements about such a
    learner; the program's reading of the learning picture is now A50 ([let the state move](../research/MOVING-STATE-20261110-001/moving_state.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Put under challenge by `MOVING-STATE-20261110-001` (backlog B71), a frozen test: the state flips one point between learning
    steps when the flip does not raise the dissonance weight, on the atom spaces where the fixed-state learner was stuck or frozen.
  - 2026-11-10 · `ATOM-LAYERS-20261110-001` found that A26 is what freezes the genus-2 floor unit (an over-agreeing face offers no learning
    step); its next-steps report selects a challenge of A26, backlog B71, letting the state move ([layers through atoms](../research/ATOM-LAYERS-20261110-001/atom_layers.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · `CHOOSE-RES-20261110-001` supplied a target class from outside, as A26 allows a target, and kept the learner's steps
    at the dissonance with the state fixed ([choosing the resolution](../research/CHOOSE-RES-20261110-001/choose_res.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Relied on again by `GROW-HANDLE-20261110-001` for learning after growth; growth itself is a separate move, chosen by the
    experimenter there (A48) ([learning that grows a handle](../research/GROW-HANDLE-20261110-001/grow_handle.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · The outside target is not needed on the class-forced tori: `NATIVE-TARGET-20261110-001` steered the same learner with
    native signals, its sight of its own moves and the count of states sharing the state's dissonance, resolving every run and beating
    random moves (computed). The target part of A26 is no longer relied on there; the rest is kept ([a native target for learning](../research/NATIVE-TARGET-20261110-001/native_target.md)).
  - 2026-11-09 · Relied on again by `LEARN-HANDLES-20261109-001` for its walks and its local rule, which used an outside distance to
    the over-agreeing face. Its theorem that every resolution of a class-forced pair winds does not rest on A26: it holds for any
    process, with the state changing or not ([learning in handles](../research/LEARN-HANDLES-20261109-001/learn_handles.md)). Backlog B61 would replace the outside distance with a native signal.
  - 2026-11-07 · Added by `RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001`.

### A27 · A law is weighed by how many states it allows

**Assumed** · 2026-11-07 · measure

- **Statement.** When laws are compared, as in the force between the two halves of a rationalized dissonance or in a learner that
  keeps the most possibilities open, each law is weighed by the number of states that satisfy it, every state counting once.
- **Why.** `RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001` took it by default. It is the uniform measure on allowed states, the one the canonical
  reading of allowed states (A3, A8) suggests, and it makes the weights exact counts of perfect matchings.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's finding that the halves repel, the corners attract, and a learner that keeps the most states parks
  the halves on corners ([steering by re-signing](../research/RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001/resign_steer.md)).
- **Challenge.** The alternatives weigh states by their dissonance at a temperature, or count only the states reachable by local
  moves. The cheapest test recomputes the force with each state weighted by a power of its number of over-agreeing faces, or with
  the states of one local-move component only, and checks whether the halves still repel and the corners still attract.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-10 · Weighed by the state's own dissonance, the count steers: `NATIVE-TARGET-20261110-001` found it is 2 far from the
    over-agreeing face and grows near it, so a learner keeping the most such states resolves every class-forced pair tested, faster
    than random moves; one keeping the fewest does not ([a native target for learning](../research/NATIVE-TARGET-20261110-001/native_target.md)).
  - 2026-11-07 · Added by `RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001`.

### A28 · Outward growth is judged by its law's local consistency

**Assumed** · 2026-11-07 · scope

- **Statement.** A growth move is legal when the tiles it adds overlap no placed tile and leave every tile they touch with a
  collar that still extends to one of the law's complete collars, without overlap. This is one step of lookahead. A dead end is a
  tile with no legal move. Growth results for the pinwheel are stated under this rule, or under it plus the radius-2 atlas.
- **Why.** `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001` took it by default. It is the weakest rule that a local process can apply and that never
  rejects a genuine continuation: growth that follows a genuine pinwheel patch is never rejected
  ([pinwheel growth](../artifacts/GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001B/GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001B-E001_pinwheel_grown_outward/run/interpretation.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The dead-end counts and sizes of `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001B` and `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001C`, and the reading
  that label-free growth leaves the pinwheel at once.
- **Challenge.** A deeper lookahead could get stuck later or less often. Ticket C's stronger arm adds the radius-2 atlas. Dworkin
  and Shieh (1995) prove that every aperiodic protoset of the plane has deceptions of all orders, but the pinwheel's collar and
  radius-2 laws are not aperiodic: a periodic plane obeys both (`COLLAR-PLANE-20261108-001`). So that theorem does not settle
  whether some growth order of these laws fails. The cheapest test is two moves of lookahead on ticket B's seeds.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Challenge reworded after `COLLAR-PLANE-20261108-001`: it had said that by Dworkin and Shieh a stronger rule can
    change when growth fails but not whether some order fails, which needs an aperiodic law (`docs/CORRECTIONS.md`).
  - 2026-11-07 · Added by `GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001`.

### A29 · Where a Fano piece sits within its switching class does not matter

**Overturned** · 2026-11-08 · scope

- **Statement.** A plain edge-piece system is studied through one placement of its Fano pieces: a Fano piece on each signed
  incidence of one minimal signing of its glue. Results computed on that placement are taken to hold for every placement in the same switching
  class.
- **Why.** HANDLE-COUNT A's generator places the atoms on the minimal signing it finds, and the lines that used it, HANDLE-COUNT B
  and TRISUM-READ, took whichever placement it returned without comparing placements. Which one it returns depends on Python's hash
  seed, since its Pfaffian signing walks sets.
- **Rests on it.** The fresh trisum spaces of `HANDLE-COUNT-20261106-001B` (three to six of its 257 per-space rows change under
  other hash seeds; its stated totals do not), and the eighteen spaces of `TRISUM-READ-20261107-001`, each reproduced at its seed
  only under a recorded hash seed.
- **Challenge.** Overturned by `SWITCH-READ-20261108-001`. For five glues with two placements in one switching class, the native
  maps agree exactly between glue lines, but removing a line of an atom creates differently at glue lines, so the whole maps are never
  isomorphic: for native creation each placement is its own space. What replaced it: creation between glue lines depends only on the
  switching class (computed, five pairs), and the results that rested on it stand, since the junction epic's seven statements hold
  on all five other placements and HANDLE-COUNT B's stated totals reproduce
  ([switch read](../research/SWITCH-READ-20261108-001/switch_read.md)).
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "Where an atom sits within its switching class does not matter" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Overturned (`SWITCH-READ-20261108-001`); the results resting on it rechecked and standing.
  - 2026-11-07 · Added by `TRISUM-READ-20261107-001`.

### A30 · A process's memory is read from its two-time knots

**Assumed** · 2026-11-08 · scope

- **Statement.** What a process remembers of its past is read from its two-time knots: the knots of its space-time law that join one
  cell's value after T steps to cells of the first row, which are the minimally decisive sets of the T-step map. Knots through
  intermediate rows, or with several future cells, are set aside, except where they are counted for the linear rules.
- **Why.** `TIME-KNOTS-20261107-001` took it by default. These knots are characterized exactly (Theorem 1 of its ticket A), each is
  a knot of the whole space-time law, and they are cheap to count, which made the census of the 88 elementary automata possible
  ([knots across time](../research/TIME-KNOTS-20261107-001/time_knots.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The census classes of `TIME-KNOTS-20261107-001B`, the reading that pairwise memory is exactly the single ANDs and
  ORs, and the first measure of the next epic, knots between processes.
- **Challenge.** The alternative reads memory from every knot of the space-time law that meets two times, and it finds more.
  Ledrappier's law has smaller knots through intermediate rows (2 + w(T) places against 2^popcount(T) + 1), and its least knot at
  T = 7 leaves the light cone through a second future cell. The cheapest test, the census's 15 knot-free rules read on their whole
  law in small windows, was run the day this entry was added. Rules 128, 136 and 160 knot two times and rule 32 knots one later row,
  wherever the image forbids a pattern, while the other eleven showed no knot. So the readings differ, and every claim made under
  this one names the first row. It would be overturned if a question the program asks of processes, such as whether a contact binds
  two of them, got a different answer under the whole-law reading. The next epic reads both.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `TIME-KNOTS-20261107-001`. The same day its cheapest test showed that the two readings differ, and the
    epic's claims were narrowed to the first row
    ([correction](../research/TIME-KNOTS-20261107-001/time_knots.md#correction-2026-11-08)).

### A31 · A shared contact combines its two rings' proposals

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-08 · scope

- **Statement.** When the contact is a cell of both rings, its update is a two-input law applied to the two values that the rings'
  own rules would give it: c(t + 1) = g(f_A(a_{m−1}, c, a_1), f_B(b_{n−1}, c, b_1)). Results on shared contacts are stated for this
  family.
- **Why.** `PROCESS-CONTACT-20261108-001` took it by default. It is the smallest family in which the contact is a cell of each ring
  under that ring's own rule, and it has only 16 laws, so every one could be run on every rule
  ([knots between processes](../research/PROCESS-CONTACT-20261108-001/process_contact.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The census of `PROCESS-CONTACT-20261108-001B` (44 of 128,128 configurations bind at every lag) and the reading
  that a contact shared by identity rarely persists.
- **Challenge.** At lag 1 this family fails exactly when a rule is canalizing at its centre, because the contact's own value enters
  both proposals. A contact that combines its four ring neighbours without reading itself is still a cell of both rings, since both
  rings read it. The cheapest test is the same census with the laws u(a_{m−1}, a_1) + v(b_{n−1}, b_1). If they persist much more
  often, the rarity belongs to this family and not to shared identity; if not, the reading stands.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Confirmed by `SHARED-CONTACT-20261108-001`. Self-blind parities persist only a few points more often than proposal
    parities on the same rings (6.4% against 4.5%), with the same growth in ring size, so the reading stands. The rarity itself was
    largely a small-ring effect: on rings of 8 and 9 cells a fifth to a quarter of both families persist
    ([which shared contacts persist](../research/SHARED-CONTACT-20261108-001/shared_contact.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `PROCESS-CONTACT-20261108-001`, and put under challenge the same day by `SHARED-CONTACT-20261108-001`.

### A32 · Two-sided essentiality across time is binding

**Assumed** · 2026-11-08 · reading

- **Statement.** A contact between two processes is two-sided essential at lag T when every minimally decisive set of its value
  c(T) over the joint first row meets both processes' cells. It persists when this holds at every lag.
- **Why.** `PROCESS-CONTACT-20261108-001` took it by default. Its ticket A proves that this is exactly the loss of the contact's
  knots when either process is forgotten, so two items of the contact gate become one property across time
  ([which contacts bind](../proofs/PROCESS-CONTACT-20261108-001A/PROCESS-CONTACT-20261108-001A-P001_which_contacts_bind_two_processes/proof.md)).
- **Rests on it.** Every binding and persistence count of `PROCESS-CONTACT-20261108-001` and its successors.
- **Challenge.** Weaker readings would ask only that some knot through c(T) meet both processes, or would read binding from the
  whole space-time law (A30's alternative), where the contact's history can join the rings as a witness. The cheapest test reruns
  the census of ticket B with "some knot meets both rings" and compares the classes.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `PROCESS-CONTACT-20261108-001`.

### A33 · A local law is read up to congruence

**Assumed** · 2026-11-08 · reading

- **Statement.** A local law of tiles lists its allowed neighbourhoods up to congruence: a neighbourhood is written in its central
  tile's own frame, so rotations and reflections are free, and no absolute direction belongs to the law. The pinwheel's collar law
  (108 collars) and its radius-r laws are read this way, and the theorem that no undecorated local law of the pinwheel forces
  aperiodicity is stated for such laws.
- **Why.** The program's laws are not written on a space in advance, so they carry no frame. `PINWHEEL-ATLAS-20261107-001` took it
  by default for its collars (its rooted codes, A23, are congruence-free anyway), and `COLLAR-PLANE-20261108-001` stated its
  theorem under it ([collar plane](../research/COLLAR-PLANE-20261108-001/collar_plane.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The atlas's finite counts (193 collars, 108 complete, 1,168 forms), the growth engine's law, and the reading that
  the plane needs hidden information for roundness.
- **Challenge.** A law that also records directions against a fixed frame sees the pinwheel's tiles in infinitely many directions,
  so no finite law of that kind holds the pinwheel at all, and the question changes rather than its answer. A milder alternative
  records handedness only: in the pinwheel every complete collar occurs with both handednesses, so the cheapest test is to recount
  the census with handedness kept and check that nothing changes.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `COLLAR-PLANE-20261108-001`.

### A34 · A chain composes when its far contact binds its ends at every lag

**Overturned** · 2026-11-08 · reading

- **Statement.** Two contacts between processes, c1 joining rings A and B and c2 joining rings B and C, compose when c2 binds the
  end rings A and C at every lag from the first lag at which ring A can reach it, d + 1 for contacts d cells apart on ring B. That
  is, every minimally decisive set of c2's value meets both end rings' first-row cells.
- **Why.** `PROCESS-CHAIN-20261108-001` took it by default. It reads the contact-network gate's composition as persistence of the
  composed binding, matching the persistence asked of each contact
  ([two contacts across time](../research/PROCESS-CHAIN-20261108-001/process_chain.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The epic's finding that no chain with both contacts persistent composes, one size past included.
- **Challenge.** The alternative reads composition at the recurring lags the system itself fixes: the far contact binds the end
  rings at some lag of its eventual cycle, and so at lags recurring with its period. In linear chains and paths composition is
  arrival, so those lags are fixed by the dynamics, not by an observer. `RECUR-PATH-20261108-001` tests the alternative by
  transport, pre-registered on 2026-11-08 before it ran. It uses the growth families of `PATH-GROWTH-20261108-001`: one linear rule
  per ring and every law, four rings growing from 3 to 9 cells (or 4 to 9, contacts two apart), or paths from 4 to 9 rings.
  - **Overturned**, if some family composes at recurring lags with every contact persistent at every size of its schedule. The
    recurring reading then replaces this one, as a new entry, since under this reading no family composes at two sizes.
  - **Confirmed**, kept for the gate, if such families exist only with contacts that bind at the composing lags but not at every
    lag. The recurring reading would then transport only by giving up the persistence the contact gate asks of each contact.
  - **Confirmed**, also, if no family composes at recurring lags at every size. Composition across time is then tied to sizes under
    both readings.
  - **Back to Assumed**, if the best candidate families meet a recurring reading at every decided size but some sizes stay
    undecided.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Counts corrected: the census listed each two-apart family four times. The distinct counts are 64 two-apart
    families and 260 in all, and `RECUR-PROOF-20261108-001` proved the 64 at every ring size and length. The outcome stands.
  - 2026-11-08 · Overturned by `RECUR-PATH-20261108-001`, by the rule pre-registered above. 260 families of paths (first counted as
    452) compose at recurring lags with every contact persistent at every size of their schedule: 64 with contacts two apart on rings
    of 4 to 9 cells, every ring on rule 60 or 102, and 196 growing from 4 to 9 rings. Under this reading no family composes at two
    sizes. All are verified by an independent computation at every size and on their full state spaces at the two smallest. Replaced by
    A37. Of what rested on it, `PROCESS-CHAIN-20261108-001`'s finding stands under both readings, since its uniform chains never
    composed at recurring lags with both contacts persistent either
    ([composition at recurring lags](../research/RECUR-PATH-20261108-001/recur_path.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Challenge pre-registered by `RECUR-PATH-20261108-001` before its census ran: the outcomes and what each means for
    this entry are under Challenge above. The entry stays under challenge until the epic closes.
  - 2026-11-08 · Put under challenge by `PATH-GROWTH-20261108-001`, as its stop rule set out. Under this reading no family of paths
    with one linear rule per ring composes at more than one size: native paths sit at isolated shapes, and none survives one more
    cell or one more ring. Composition at recurring lags, with the phase fixed by the chain, held at every tested size for three-ring
    chains (`INTERMITTENT-NET-20261108-001`). The test is whether that reading gives paths that compose at every size; if none does
    either, this reading is kept and composition across time is tied to sizes under both
    ([native paths that survive growth](../research/PATH-GROWTH-20261108-001/path_growth.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Bears on it: `PATH-HYBRID-20261108-001`. Read for a path's last contact, native paths exist at particular sizes and
    none survives growth one size past. If binding at every lag proves tied to particular sizes, the intermittent reading returns
    as the alternative ([paths through mixed middle rings](../research/PATH-HYBRID-20261108-001/path_hybrid.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Confirmed by `INTERMITTENT-NET-20261108-001`. Composition at recurring lags is persistent composition of a strobed
    chain, whose phase only the chain could fix. The chain fixes it in 92 of 244 intermittently composing chains, every one with an
    affine rule, and never with a nonlinear rule. The contact gate asks for persistence, which intermittent composition fails, so
    this reading is kept for the gate. The native intermittent network on affine substrates is recorded beside it
    ([intermittent networks](../research/INTERMITTENT-NET-20261108-001/intermittent_net.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `PROCESS-CHAIN-20261108-001`.

### A35 · Hidden information is counted as labels a decorated substitution sets

**Assumed** · 2026-11-08 · measure

- **Statement.** The hidden information a local law of the pinwheel spends on roundness is counted as the number of labels per
  tile, with labels set by a decorated substitution: Conway's cut gives each child a label fixed by its parent's label and its child
  index, and the law lists the labelled collars of decorated supertiles. One bit per tile means two labels.
- **Why.** `ROUND-PRICE-20261108-001` took it by default. Every known way to force a substitution tiling (Mozes; Goodman-Strauss;
  Radin for the pinwheel) decorates the substitution this way, it makes the count finite and exhaustive for small alphabets, and
  the rule radius then does not matter ([price of roundness](../research/ROUND-PRICE-20261108-001/round_price.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The census (1,008 of 1,024 one-bit decorations admit a crystal), the sixteen open candidates, and the comparison
  with the sphere's one hidden bit per incidence.
- **Challenge.** Count bits on edges instead, as Radin's rules do, or let a child's label depend on its parent's neighbours too.
  The cheapest test runs the rhombus crystal against one-bit edge labellings of the pinwheel's collars; if some edge bit excludes
  every crystal that all one-bit tile decorations admit, the measure misplaces the price.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Under this count, one bit per tile never forces the pinwheel: all 1,024 one-bit decorations admit a crystal
    (`CRYSTAL-UP-20261108-001`). The challenge (bits on edges) keeps its interest: whether the count depends on where the bits sit.
  - 2026-11-08 · Bears on it: `ONE-BIT-20261108-001`. Under this measure one bit per tile fails in 1,022 of 1,024 cases, and one
    decoration, up to relabelling, is open.
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `ROUND-PRICE-20261108-001`.

### A36 · A law declared cell by cell is a native substrate

**Assumed** · 2026-11-08 · scope

- **Statement.** A ring whose cells run different elementary rules, a hybrid ring, is a declared substrate like any other. A path
  of such rings is judged by the same contact and network gates as a path of uniform rings.
- **Why.** `PATH-HYBRID-20261108-001` took it by default when it counted ticket B's hybrid paths as native. The canonical knot test
  reads any declared system of constraints, and a law fixed cell by cell is still fixed in advance, with no input supplied from
  outside ([paths through mixed middle rings](../research/PATH-HYBRID-20261108-001/path_hybrid.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The 1,120 native paths with hybrid middle rings and their characterisation. The existence of native paths across
  time does not rest on it: ticket A's 16 use one rule per ring.
- **Challenge.** Require one uniform law per ring, as question 9 asks of space, or require the cell types to arise from a uniform
  law, for instance as a second bit per cell that the dynamics holds fixed. The cheapest test
  builds the smallest hybrid path's ring C from a uniform law on two bits per cell. If no such emulation keeps the path native,
  the hybrid paths depend on drawing the law cell by cell.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `PATH-HYBRID-20261108-001`.

### A37 · A path composes at the recurring lags its dynamics fixes

**Assumed** · 2026-11-08 · reading

- **Statement.** A chain or path of processes composes when every contact binds its own two rings at every lag and the far contact
  binds the end rings at some lag of its eventual cycle, and so at the lags recurring with its period. In a linear path those lags are
  the lags at which both ends' influence arrives, fixed by the dynamics.
- **Why.** `RECUR-PATH-20261108-001` overturned A34 by its pre-registered rule, and this reading replaces it. Under it, composition
  across time survives every growth tested; under A34 it survives none
  ([composition at recurring lags](../research/RECUR-PATH-20261108-001/recur_path.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The 260 families of `RECUR-PATH-20261108-001` as native networks across time that transport, 64 of them proved at
  every ring size and length (`RECUR-PROOF-20261108-001`).
- **Challenge.** Ask more of the composing lags: that a knot of the path itself reads them, or that they make up a fixed share of
  every cycle at every size. The cheapest test proves or refutes, for the rule-60 family, that the composing share stays positive at
  every ring size.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Bears on it: `DISTANCE-20261108-001` proved that in any network of rule-60 rings a route isolated by freezing, with no
    arcs among its contacts but its hops, composes under this reading at every size: every route contact persists, and the far contact
    needs the near ring exactly when C(T − L − 1, s + m) is odd, s the route's own cells and m below the near corner's own cells
    ([distance across time](../research/DISTANCE-20261108-001/distance.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Bears on it: `RECUR-PROOF-20261108-001` proved that the two-apart paths with rule 60 or 102 compose under this reading
    at every ring size and length, the far contact needing the first ring exactly when one binomial coefficient is odd; their
    composing share is 2^(−w), positive at every size
    ([recurring composition at every ring size](../research/RECUR-PROOF-20261108-001/recur_proof.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `RECUR-PATH-20261108-001`, replacing A34.

### A38 · A contact is cut by freezing it

**Assumed** · 2026-11-08 · scope

- **Statement.** The intervention that tests native separation across time cuts a contact by freezing it: its law becomes c ← c, so
  it reads nothing and keeps its first value.
- **Why.** `SEPARATE-20261108-001` took it by default. Freezing is the simplest declared contact intervention that stops every route
  through the contact, and it leaves every other cell's law unchanged
  ([native separation across time](../research/SEPARATE-20261108-001/separate.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The epic's theorems that a cut separates a proved path and that a cycle of four rings needs one cut per route;
  the readings of separation, genus and distance across time built on them since (A41).
- **Challenge.** Cut instead by erasing the contact, forgetting it as the knot test erases a loop, or by letting it read only one of
  its rings. The cheapest test reruns the epic's checks with a one-sided cut; a route that survives it would show that what counts as
  a cut changes which sets are separated.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Bears on it: `DISTANCE-20261108-001` reads distance across time through freezing (A41): the earliest need any freeze
    allows and the most disjoint cuts are graph distances on every network
    ([distance across time](../research/DISTANCE-20261108-001/distance.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `SEPARATE-20261108-001`.

### A39 · Space is spaced by knots that compose

**Under challenge** · 2026-11-18 · reading

- **Statement.** The spacing a law sets between two distinctions is their bond distance, the size of the least knot through both,
  minus one: distinctions joined only by a chain of knots that meet at gates are not spaced at all. A gate is a shared distinction
  one value of which satisfies both knots it joins, so that it is a common hub rather than a touch.
- **Why.** `BOND-CHAIN-20261108-001` overturned A12 as a universal statement and this reading replaces it. Chains of knots and bond
  distance agree wherever knots compose across their meetings, including every parity law (proved), and part exactly at gates. The
  foundation's contact rule decides between them: "Two independent A-k and B-k relations make k a common hub, not one touch between
  A and B; shared variable identity alone is insufficient" (`AGENTS.md`). At a gate no knot crosses k, and bond distance follows
  that rule
  ([bond and chain distance](../research/BOND-CHAIN-20261108-001/bond_chain.md)).
- **Rests on it.** Everything that rested on A12: the number line, the taxicab plane, the circles of periodic and pinwheel laws, and
  dimension read from ball growth (question 9). All of it was measured where the two measures agree.
- **Challenge.** Find a law whose gate chains carry structure the program would want to call space that composing knots cannot
  carry, for example a hub chain in two dimensions whose chain balls are round. The cheapest test measures chain balls on a plane of
  gated knots against the grid it is drawn on.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-18 · `RIM-EXCESS-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/RIM-EXCESS-20261118-001/rim_excess.md); refereed, confirmed with fixes):
    the excess form. On weave patches without holes bond distance is steps apart + 1 plus an excess that is the least bond polygon's
    extra length over the round trip; to twelve knots it lives only on lone pairs of opposite classes facing each other; it is at most 2
    there, and on the facing pairs of every disc of thirteen and fourteen knots it is still at most 2 (the referee; no 3); growth open.
    Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `WEAVE-HEIGHTS-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/WEAVE-HEIGHTS-20261118-001/weave_heights.md); referee
    pending): on weave patches without holes bond distance is at least steps apart + 1, proved through the height function (with an
    exact criterion for constrained sets); its excess at the rim is open. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `DOUBLE-MEET-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/DOUBLE-MEET-20261118-001/double_meet.md); referee
    pending; refereed, one reading blocked): the weave form. On patches of the weave the double meetings never break the spacing;
    only a pair of lone distinctions of opposite classes facing each other does, unbonded or (from twelve knots) bonded too far, a
    property of the patch's rim. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `NAE-METRIC-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/NAE-METRIC-20261118-001/nae_metric.md); referee
    pending): the block form. On every not-all-equal or betweenness base, bond distance minus one adds across a joint, so a base is
    spaced by bond distance as soon as each of its blocks is; trees of knots and rings are (proved), lone double meetings are not.
    Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `BOND-METRIC-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/BOND-METRIC-20261118-001/bond_metric.md); refereed): a
    metric form. Bond distance satisfies the triangle inequality on every base whose knots compose like cycles (every parity base;
    proved, with bond distance minus one), and among the 34 three-place laws on every base of copies of one law exactly for parity;
    every other law has a failing base, two knots sharing two distinctions for six of the eight kinds (not-all-equal among them).
    This holds law by law, not base by base: not-all-equal trees and tori are metric. The results resting here were measured on
    weaves, trees and parity bases, where it is a metric. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `COUNT-BALLS-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/COUNT-BALLS-20261118-001/count_balls.md); referee
    pending): the outline depends on which reading spaces the law. On the six-regular weave under not-all-equal, bond distance
    gives the hexagon of steps apart (rational π), while the disagreement count, which recovers states for three-place not-all-equal rules on four or more distinctions, gives the
