    start's class a round outline (the amplitude the same along every repeat to 1e-4; computed exactly, classical in substance);
    chain grids give smooth outlines just off round. So the circles of periodic laws and dimension read from ball growth, which
    rest here, are statements about bond distance. Status Assumed → Under challenge.
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `BOND-CHAIN-20261108-001`, replacing A12.

### A40 · Counted length is a native magnitude

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-08 · measure

- **Statement.** The number of contacts along a route, equivalently the lags the substrate takes to carry influence along it, is a
  magnitude the substrate reads, and so may serve as a length across time. In a network of rule-60 rings whose law-(12, 12) contacts
  are g cells apart, a route of L contacts from a ring to a contact, isolated by freezing every contact off it (A38), makes the far
  contact first need the ring at lag g·L + 1, and these lags add when two routes are joined at a shared contact.
- **Why.** The owner ruled on 2026-11-08, answering the question the process line had put since the lattice epic, whether counted
  length can count as a native magnitude: "I think yes, length can count as magnitude. We should be willing to explore things like
  this without getting my approval first." Until then first lags were used only as lags
  ([lattice of rings](../research/LATTICE-NET-20261108-001/lattice_net.md),
  [genus by cuts](../research/GENUS-CUT-20261108-001/genus_cut.md)).
- **Rests on it.** `DISTANCE-20261108-001`, distance across time (G5): its time length (the earliest need any freeze allows) and its
  cut lengths, and whatever is later built on them, such as dimension or roundness read across time
  ([distance across time](../research/DISTANCE-20261108-001/distance.md)).
- **Challenge.** The alternative counts nothing: a count of lags or contacts is the observer's, and only orders, coincidences and
  separations are native. The frozen test of `DISTANCE-20261108-001A` was pre-registered on 2026-11-08 before it ran (sha256 of its
  contract c7818ce53ded426b, of run.py 7e109b3b76c426e2, of distance_lib.py ad7c26626e04edfe). It reads routes at the cell level on
  square tori of side 5, 6 and 7, triangular tori of side 4 and 5, honeycomb tori of side 3, 4 and 5, the genus-2 networks of GL(2,3),
  the genus-3 networks of PSL(2,7) (the Klein quartic map and its dual) and the five Platonic networks, with contacts 2, 3 and 4
  cells apart: three random shortest and three random chordless routes of each length L = 1 to 10 (seed 20261108), each from a ring
  holding only its first contact, with every contact off the route frozen.
  - **Confirmed**, if (a) every route's far contact first needs its ring at lag g·L + 1; (b) at every inner contact the first lags of
    the two halves, each isolated on its own, add up to the whole's ((t_P − 1) + (t_Q − 1) = t − 1); and (c) with consecutive
    contacts 2 to 5 cells apart at random, on the square torus of side 5 and the genus-2 network of 16 rings, the first lag is 1 plus
    the cells the route carries influence through.
  - **Overturned**, if (a) or (b) fails for any route: the lag would then not be a length the substrate reads.
  - **Confirmed for equally spaced networks only**, and reworded to say so, if only (c) fails.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-08 · Confirmed by `DISTANCE-20261108-001`'s frozen test, run once after its pre-registration in 510ff23fc: (a) all 2,250
    routes made the far contact first need the near ring at lag g·L + 1, (b) all 7,285 splits added, and (c) all 372 routes at unequal
    spacing gave 1 plus the cells carried. Its ticket B proves the law for every network of rule-60 rings with law-(12, 12) contacts.
    Confirmed for stated reasons: counted contacts and counted lags are one reading where contacts are equally spaced, and the
    substrate's own count is cells ([distance across time](../research/DISTANCE-20261108-001/distance.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Put under challenge by `DISTANCE-20261108-001`, whose test was frozen and pre-registered before it ran (under
    Challenge).
  - 2026-11-08 · Added at the owner's ruling that counted length can count as magnitude.

### A41 · Distance across time is read through freezes

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-08 · reading

- **Statement.** Distance across time is read with freezing (A38): as the cut length, the most pairwise disjoint freeze sets that
  leave two rings in different native pieces, and as the time length, the earliest lag at which any freeze makes a contact need a
  ring. It is not read as the plain first need of the unfrozen network.
- **Why.** `DISTANCE-20261108-001` took it by default. The freeze readings are graph distances on every network, by proof: the cut
  length is the ring graph's distance, and the time length is 1 plus the fewest cells of a route, a directed metric. The plain first
  need is the same light cone filtered by the parity of the number of shortest routes: late or absent where routes pair up, and it
  breaks the triangle inequality ([distance across time](../research/DISTANCE-20261108-001/distance.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The answer of `DISTANCE-20261108-001`, that distance across time is native and equals graph distance, and whatever
  is built on these lengths, such as balls, dimension or roundness read across time.
- **Challenge.** Read distance without intervening. On every Platonic, genus-2 and genus-3 network tested every contact needs every
  ring exactly at the time length, while on tori of side 5 and more it does not, and with unequal spacing the plain first need meets
  the cell length for 98% to 100% of pairs but contacts stop persisting. The cheapest test finds where the plain first need is on time
  for every pair among orientably regular maps from small groups. A family of persistent networks where it is, or a native
  observable that counts routes rather than their parity, would make freezing unnecessary there; finding none would confirm this
  reading.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-09 · Reason restated by `REROUTE-20261109-001`: the square torus's automaton reads its distance plainly when its cells are
    grouped into lines, so whether a network needs freezing depends on its declared rings (A45). On the square contact graph a grouping
    that reads plainly has at most 64n rings of linear size (proved), so the reason stands for networks of small rings
    ([large networks persistent without flips](../research/REROUTE-20261109-001/reroute.md)).
  - 2026-11-09 · Confirmation narrowed by `GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001` (a correction of the line below, `docs/CORRECTIONS.md`): the
    26 tori are the rotation-invariant ones. Thin tori of every size read distance without freezing too: every square torus whose
    lattice holds (2, 0), the ladders that are the band law's first stage (proved), and the square (2, 1) tubes of one chirality and the
    triangular √3 tubes (computed to index 100 and 80). The reason stands in its narrower form: freezing is needed wherever a network
    is two-dimensional and not tiny, and every stage of band growth from the second band on needs it
    ([distance in a grown network](../research/GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001/grown_distance.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Confirmed for stated reasons by `PLAIN-TIME-20261108-001`: the plain first need reads distance at every size only on
    the sphere (every loop of rings, pair of rings sharing contacts and Platonic network, proved). Flat and hyperbolic tilings hide it
    beyond a horizon set by ring and face sizes, exactly 26 tori read it (proved), and the smallest regular map that fails is a
    13-ring torus whose mirror image reads it. So freezing is needed wherever a network is two-dimensional and not tiny, and the two
    readings agree on the sphere ([distance without freezing](../research/PLAIN-TIME-20261108-001/plain_time.md)).
  - 2026-11-08 · Put under challenge by `PLAIN-TIME-20261108-001`, which took up this entry's cheapest test as its question.
  - 2026-11-08 · Added by `DISTANCE-20261108-001`.

### A42 · A grown network is read stage by stage

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-09 · scope

- **Statement.** A network grown by a law is read one stage at a time: each stage is a fixed network whose dynamics run from its own
  first row, and its lengths, balls and persistence are read on that network. Growth does not happen while the network runs.
- **Why.** `GROW-NET-20261108-001` and `BAND-SURFACE-20261108-001` judged growth by whether every stage is balanced, and
  `GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001` took the same reading by default, so that the readings of `DISTANCE-20261108-001` and
  `PLAIN-TIME-20261108-001` apply to each stage as proved.
- **Rests on it.** "Every stage balanced" in `GROW-NET-20261108-001` and `BAND-SURFACE-20261108-001`; the answer of
  `GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001`, that every stage reads both freeze lengths and that only the ladder stage reads distance without
  freezing ([distance in a grown network](../research/GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001/grown_distance.md)).
- **Challenge.** Let the band law act while the network runs: insert a band at a lag, its rings' cells new open distinctions from that
  lag, and read whether every contact keeps its witnesses across the insertion, when a contact first needs a ring on the far side of a
  band inserted between them, and how the balls of rings needed by a lag grow while bands keep arriving. The cheapest test inserts one
  band into a running 3 × m tube. Persistence lost at the insertion, or lengths that are not the stages' lengths, would overturn this
  reading for a law that grows in time.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-09 · Reason added by `REBASE-KNOTS-20261109-001`: every row after the first has even parity, since every cell is read by
    exactly two cells, so no later row is open and knots across time cannot be read natively from the last growth event; the stage
    reading works because each stage starts from an open first row
    ([knots read from the last growth event](../research/REBASE-KNOTS-20261109-001/rebase_knots.md)).
  - 2026-11-09 · Confirmed for stated reasons by `GROW-RUN-20261109-001`, which ran this entry's cheapest test: a band inserted while
    the tube runs leaves intact every ring it has not reached and binds its own rings (proved), but 68% of the bindings of the rings it
    has reached fail, and over every insertion lag of 13 tubes only lag 0 keeps every contact persistent in every case (computed). So
    the stage reading is the one under which band growth keeps persistence; "every stage balanced" is a property of growth between
    runs, and a law that grows in time keeps its knots across time only outside each insertion's reach
    ([growth while running](../research/GROW-RUN-20261109-001/grow_run.md)).
  - 2026-11-09 · Put under challenge by `GROW-RUN-20261109-001` (backlog B24).
  - 2026-11-09 · Added by `GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001`.

### A43 · Bands are added along the seed loop

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-09 · scope

- **Statement.** The band law adds bands of the seed loop's n rings, so its stages are the n × 2 ladder and then the n × m tori with n
  fixed and m growing. Bands of m rings across the other way, which the n × m torus admits as well, are not added.
- **Why.** `BAND-SURFACE-20261108-001` and `GROW-NET-20261108-001` stated the band step as n × m to n × (m + 1), and
  `GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001` kept it. The law itself does not say which way a band goes.
- **Rests on it.** The reading of `GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001` that band growth makes a tube: its balls grow as in the plane only out to
  half the loop and then by 2n rings per step, dimension 1 at large scale
  ([distance in a grown network](../research/GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001/grown_distance.md)).
- **Challenge.** Add bands both ways, or let the dynamics choose where the next band goes (backlog B23), and read the balls: a schedule
  that grows both sides reads dimension 2 out to the smaller side, and every n × m stage with both sides at least 3 except 3 × 3 needs
  freezing (`GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001A`, Theorem 3). A native rule that fixes the direction would replace this default.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-09 · Confirmed for stated reasons by `GROW-CHOOSE-20261109-001`, which let the dynamics choose: inserting a band only where
    it keeps every contact persistent, the network picks a direction after its first band and keeps it, so it grows a tube as this
    entry assumed, along whichever side the first band lengthened; only filling every allowed cut at once grew both ways, at lags 0 and
    1, and was not read further (exploratory census, exact per decision;
    [growth chosen by the dynamics](../research/GROW-CHOOSE-20261109-001/grow_choose.md)).
  - 2026-11-09 · Put under challenge by `GROW-CHOOSE-20261109-001` (backlog B23).
  - 2026-11-09 · Added by `GROWN-DISTANCE-20261109-001`.

### A44 · A band born while the network runs brings new open distinctions

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-09 · reading

- **Statement.** When a band is inserted into a network at a lag, its rings' cells and its contacts along the band are new open
  distinctions from that lag, born as the first row is born at lag 0, and a contact binds a new ring when it depends on those cells.
  The cut contact's value goes by one of four splits, all of which are read.
- **Why.** `GROW-RUN-20261109-001` took it by default: a beginning is a knot (`GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001`), and new cells that copied old
  values would hold no distinction of their own, so no contact could bind the new rings.
- **Rests on it.** The answer of `GROW-RUN-20261109-001`: the band's rings are always bound, the rings an insertion has not reached are
  intact, and most bindings of reached rings fail ([growth while running](../research/GROW-RUN-20261109-001/grow_run.md)).
- **Challenge.** Give the band values the network computes, for instance each new cell the value of the cell it is inserted beside, and
  read binding to the old rings through them. A birth rule under which inserting while running keeps every contact persistent would
  replace this default.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-09 · Reason narrowed by `ISOLATION-NEED-20261109-001`: the confirmation's "no rule read sustains balance with hearing" was
    read at g = 2; at g = 3 open births themselves, doubling every 2 or 4 lags, keep every stage balanced while rings hear each other to
    24 × 24, so open birth, this entry's default, is enough there (a correction in `docs/CORRECTIONS.md`).
  - 2026-11-09 · Confirmed for stated reasons by `BIRTH-RULE-20261109-001`: copies and zeros leave a new ring with no distinction of its
    own, so no contact binds it and no stage is balanced; open distinctions plus the parent's value keep every stage balanced while rings
    hear each other through 12 × 12 at P = 1, but fail at 24 × 24 at every P; no rule read sustains balance with hearing
    ([another birth rule](../research/BIRTH-RULE-20261109-001/birth_rule.md)).
  - 2026-11-09 · Put under challenge by `BIRTH-RULE-20261109-001` (backlog B51), a frozen test: under the doubling law every P = 1 to
    4 lags, the cells a doubling bears are given copies of the cells they split from, zeros, or open distinctions added to copies, and
    every stage's balance, the old rings' binding and hearing are read, with hypotheses frozen in its contract before any reading.
  - 2026-11-09 · Added by `GROW-RUN-20261109-001`.

### A45 · Rings are read as declared

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-10 · reading

- **Statement.** A network's rings are read as its declaration lists them. At a law-(12, 12) contact the automaton does not say which
  incoming corner a ring continues on: the contact reads its left neighbour on each ring and both rings read it, so every re-routing of
  a network is the same cellular automaton. Readings that group cells into rings (a contact needing a ring, the plain first need, freeze
  lengths, ring graphs and their balls, flips) are read for the declared rings; persistence and every contact-level reading do not
  depend on them.
- **Why.** Every network epic since `LOOP-NET-20261108-001` took it by default, building networks from a map's rings.
  `REROUTE-20261109-001` made it visible: re-routing leaves the automaton unchanged cell by cell, and the square torus's automaton is
  late read as small rings and on time read as lines ([large networks persistent without flips](../research/REROUTE-20261109-001/reroute.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The ring-level readings of `PLAIN-TIME-20261108-001`, `DISTANCE-20261108-001` (A41), `RESCUE-MAP-20261109-001`,
  `RESCUE-GENUS-20261109-001`, `GROW-PLAIN-20261109-001`, `PLANE-PLAIN-20261109-001`, `FLIP-RULE-20261109-001` and
  `REROUTE-20261109-001`, among them that a plane of rings needs freezing to read its distance.
- **Challenge.** Make the rings native: give contacts laws that read their two rings differently, so that the automaton fixes how each
  ring continues, and read whether persistence and the plain reading survive; or read ring-level quantities as invariants of the
  automaton, over all its groupings. The cheapest test reruns the square torus and its line network with a contact law that tells its
  rings apart. If persistence then picks out one grouping, the rings become native; if persistence needs laws that cannot tell them
  apart, ring-level readings stay declarations.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-10 · Confirmed by `RING-LAW-20261109-001`, which took up the challenge. A law is blind to re-routing exactly when it is affine
    and reads both arriving cells alike (32 of the 256 self-blind laws, (12, 12) among them), so the ring-level readings of the epics
    above are declarations, as stated. Rings become native under the 224 laws that see the routing; affine ones see only by cutting a
    ring. Under the eight symmetric nonlinear laws every network read needs both rings to lag 64 and reads its distance on time, so
    the plain reading survives native rings; but on no torus read does a law that sees the routing bind both rings (A32), and on the
    square line networks 32 such laws bind to lag 6. Native rings on a torus cost binding.
  - 2026-11-09 · Added by `REROUTE-20261109-001`.

### A46 · An over-agreeing face and an even face are dissonance

**Assumed** · 2026-11-09 · reading

- **Statement.** In the learning results a dissonance is a violated knot, a face where two or more corners agree, or an even face of
  the law. A law and a state are resolved only when none of the three is present. The count weighs them together: violated knots
  minus Σ⌊agree/2⌋ equals (χ − |D|) / 2 in every state.
- **Why.** `LEARN-HANDLES-20261109-001` took it by default, following the count of `COUNT-SURFACES-20261107-001` and the steering
  learner of `RESIGN-STEER-20261107-001`, which acts at violated knots and even faces. With it, over-agreement is the charge opposite a
  violated knot, and an even face is half a charge kept in the law.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's theorem that only surfaces of Euler characteristic 0 can be free of dissonance, and its reading of the
  class-forced torus's least dissonance as a pair that learning removes only by winding ([learning in handles](../research/LEARN-HANDLES-20261109-001/learn_handles.md)).
- **Challenge.** The alternative counts only violated knots as dissonance. Then one sign change at the violated knot resolves a
  class-forced torus at once, with no winding, and the sphere too, leaving two even faces; learning would then never need a handle. The
  owner's words, dissonance as what learning works to resolve, decide between the readings; a test would ask whether a learner that
  ignores even faces and over-agreement still has anything to steer.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-09 · Added by `LEARN-HANDLES-20261109-001`.

### A47 · A native signal comes from the law and the state alone

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · reading

- **Statement.** A signal that steers a learner is native when it is computed from the current law and the state alone: from their
  dissonance, from the states the law allows, or from the learner's own possible moves. It is outside when it uses a metric or
  coordinates of the triangulation, or a target the experimenter names. A native rule may recognise an over-agreeing face only by its
  agreement count, as dissonance it sees.
- **Why.** `NATIVE-TARGET-20261110-001` froze it before any rule ran, to replace A26's outside distance. It follows the guardrail that a
  distance supplied by a simulator is not native.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's reading that sight and the count of states sharing the state's dissonance steer learning natively
  ([a native target for learning](../research/NATIVE-TARGET-20261110-001/native_target.md)).
- **Challenge.** Two signals sit on the knot. The count is native but global: it weighs the whole law. Sight is local, but it reads the
  learner's moves, which follow the triangulation's incidences. A stricter reading admits only signals that a bounded neighbourhood of
  the hole determines; then only sight of bounded radius qualifies, and it already beats random moves.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `NATIVE-TARGET-20261110-001`.

### A48 · A grown handle is a Fano piece with the uniform law

**Overturned** · 2026-11-10 · scope

- **Statement.** When learning is paired with growth, the growth move splices one Fano piece (the Fano pattern less a distinction) at a knot,
  by one of the three bijections that keep both rotations; the piece brings the Fano pattern's uniform signs, the old signs are kept, and the
  three new sides take the signs that leave no even face. The experimenter chooses the knot.
- **Why.** `GROW-HANDLE-20261110-001` took it by default: a Fano piece is the knot's own uniform contradiction, and splicing one across a
  3-edge cut adds exactly one handle (`THREEFOLD-SURFACES-20261030-001`). The uniform signs make the grown law the one class that keeps the
  sphere's contradiction.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's reading that growth alone does not remove a sphere's dissonance and that learning then removes it through
  the grown handle ([learning that grows a handle](../research/GROW-HANDLE-20261110-001/grow_handle.md)). With the piece brought in another class, the piece would be transparent and growth alone would remove it.
- **Challenge.** Other pieces (a thin torus less a distinction, other three-fold blocks), the piece brought in the class growth chooses, or a
  growth site the learner chooses (backlog B68). The cheapest test grows a thin torus's piece instead and reads whether it too acts as the
  replaced knot in one class and is transparent in the others.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "A grown handle is a Fano atom with the uniform law" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Overturned as a limit by `OTHER-ATOMS-20261110-001`: the learned memory does not need the Fano plane. Every torus atom
    whose torus has one class no state satisfies and is free at its point acts as its line in exactly that class on any host and holds
    a unit there (proved by hand; freeness checked for every lattice torus to 30 points), which covers the Fano atom and the thin-torus
    atoms of even index to 40 points; three thin-torus atoms wrote and held 54 of 54 addresses on K_3. An atom whose torus satisfies every
    class is transparent in all four. Replaced by A52; what rests on A48 stands for every such atom
    ([other atoms](../research/OTHER-ATOMS-20261110-001/other_atoms.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Under challenge by `OTHER-ATOMS-20261110-001` (backlog B97): 28 other lattice-torus atoms grown at knot sphere lines, each
    tested for acting as its line in exactly one class, holding a unit, and writing and returning an address
    ([pre-registration](../research/OTHER-ATOMS-20261110-001/preregistration.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `GROW-HANDLE-20261110-001`.

### A49 · Dissonance is weighed as the count weighs it

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · measure

- **Statement.** The total dissonance of a law and a state is W = violated knots + E + |D| / 2: a violated knot counts 1, a face counts
  ⌊agree / 2⌋, and an even face counts 1/2, the weights of the count's identity.
- **Why.** `GROW-STUCK-20261110-001` took it by default for its learner, which steps only when W falls. With these weights the count gives
  the floor W ≥ |χ| / 2.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's learner and its reading that one grown handle is exactly enough ([grow where learning is stuck](../research/GROW-STUCK-20261110-001/grow_stuck.md)). The floor itself holds for any
  weights at least these.
- **Challenge.** Other weights (every dissonance counting 1, or over-agreement counted by agree − 1) change when a learner sees progress and
  so when it grows. The cheapest test reruns GROW-r with unit weights and reads whether it still grows once at the dissonance.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `GROW-STUCK-20261110-001`.

### A50 · The state moves without raising the dissonance while learning acts

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · scope

- **Statement.** In the learning picture, between learning steps (sign changes at the dissonance) the state may flip one distinction, chosen
  among the flips that do not raise the dissonance weight W (A49). The point moves; learning orients it.
- **Why.** It replaces A26 as a limit, after `MOVING-STATE-20261110-001` showed that holding the state still is what left a learner stuck
  and a unit frozen. It is the owner's picture most directly: a point that moves through a network that learning orients.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's finding that a moving point frees learning in about half the runs while a settled unit stays in its piece
  ([let the state move](../research/MOVING-STATE-20261110-001/moving_state.md)).
- **Challenge.** The alternatives let the state take only improving flips, or any flip with a temperature, or flip several distinctions at once.
  The cheapest test reruns that epic with improving flips only.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `MOVING-STATE-20261110-001`.
  - 2026-11-10 · Used both ways by `NATIVE-WRITE-20261110-001`: its learner tests its memory with this walk (a state flip only when W
    does not rise), and writes with the alternative, state flips at a temperature; status unchanged.

### A51 · Cells and their neighbours are given before the rules

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-10 · scope

- **Statement.** Every law measured for space so far has its cells, and which of them are neighbours, fixed before the law is
  read: a lattice Z^D, a declared network of rings (A45), a group's Cayley law, or a gadget's copies. The law's distinctions are
  the values on that board; the board itself is not a distinction, so no allowed state can change it.
- **Why.** A CNF names its variables, and naming them by positions on a grid is the usual way to write a uniform local law; Conway's
  Life is written that way (lesson 1.3 of the Intuitions guide). `LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001` took it by default and found that the
  dimension then comes with the board or with the gadget: no tested law selects its own dimension
  ([the axis built from logic](../research/LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001/logic_axis.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The dimensions read from bond-ball growth on Z^D, the taxicab plane, the circles of periodic and pinwheel laws,
  the grown planes of rings of the growth line (`GROW-ALL-20261109-001`, `GROW-STOP-20261110-001`, `CROSS-WHY-20261110-001`), and
  the reading that no tested law selects its own dimension. Only the number line of `OPEN-END-GROWTH-20261107-001` is grown with
  nothing drawn.
- **Challenge.** Make the board part of the allowed states, as the owner proposed on 2026-11-07 and 2026-11-10: give each pair of
  cells a distinction saying whether they are neighbours, or whether two names are one cell, in the same infinite CNF as the
  values, with one uniform clause pattern that names no degree, face size or relation of a group, and read which boards the rules
  allow or force (backlog B92). If every such law leaves the board free, the barrier belongs to uniform laws and not to drawing; if
  one forces a plane, dimension follows the rules. Such identity distinctions are read as distinctions of the law, never as
  contact: equality is not a binding.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "Places and their neighbours are given before the rules" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · The board line closes on it, with `GROW-FACE-20261110-001` ([every accretion law](../research/GROW-FACE-20261110-001/grow_face.md)):
    places and their neighbours need not be given before the rules, but a face must be named by them. Static laws, with letters
    or without, and laws that grow the board from a seed choose the plane only by naming a face (for growth by definition, A61).
    With a named face, growth from one seed gives the plane itself with coordinates mod 3, where a static law with those values
    allows its cylinders and tori: growth adds one world with no quotient, but no face. The line is parked.
  - 2026-11-10 · Extended by `BARE-NAMES-20261110-001` ([abstract names](../research/BARE-NAMES-20261110-001/bare_names.md)): with the neighbours
    themselves distinctions of the law, on names without letters, no finitary law forces two places to touch (Ramsey), every
    count law allows the tree, and a named square with aperiodic tiles gives planes only componentwise (proved). For finitary
    and count laws the barrier holds without the naming.
  - 2026-11-10 · Extended by `WEAK-APER-20261110-001`
    ([weakly aperiodic laws](../research/WEAK-APER-20261110-001/weak_aperiodic.md)): under Leibniz's rule every law with finite windows over a
    finite alphabet and infinitely many solutions has the tree as a board, since every infinite shift of finite type on the free
    group has a solution with trivial stabilizer (proved). So no such law forces the plane, and the reduction left by
    `LEIBNIZ-BOARDS-20261110-001` is closed.
  - 2026-11-10 · Sharpened by `BOARD-READ-20261110-001`: on every periodic board the route reading is a polygon norm up to a constant
    (proved), and on the square, triangular, honeycomb and kagome boards it is the board's own graph distance times g·d/2 (computed),
    so under these laws the dynamics reads the declared board's distance, scaled, and adds no shape of its own. The growth line's
    question 9 thus rests on this entry: a chosen shape needs the board to be part of the law
    ([readings on other boards](../research/BOARD-READ-20261110-001/board_read.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Extended by `LEIBNIZ-BOARDS-20261110-001`
    ([Leibniz boards](../research/LEIBNIZ-BOARDS-20261110-001/leibniz_boards.md)): under Leibniz's rule in full (names whose
    views agree at every radius are one place), an infinitary identity rule, no one-bit Life-like law and not the atom forces the
    plane, since each has finitely many solutions or a periodic solution with a free choice, which leaves the tree as a board
    (proved with a frozen exact census). For arbitrary finite alphabets this is reduced, not proved.
  - 2026-11-10 · Confirmed for planes within laws that name no relation, by `BOARD-DIST-20261110-001`
    ([the board as distinctions](../research/BOARD-DIST-20261110-001/board_dist.md)). With the board made of identity distinctions on
    the words in x, y and their inverses, a law whose identity literals relate only the same offset of two names allows the tree
    whenever it allows an infinite board, so it never forces the plane (proved); one named relation, the commutator, with an
    aperiodic tile law forces exactly the plane (proved); the frozen census of the 1,024 Life-like one-bit laws held all its
    predictions. The barrier belongs to uniform laws that name no relation, not to drawing. The naming (A54) and the per-name count
    of nativeness (A53) are recorded as new assumptions; Leibniz's principle in the limit, an infinitary rule, is open (B111).
  - 2026-11-10 · Under challenge by `BOARD-DIST-20261110-001` (backlog B92), pre-registered before any run: names are the reduced
    words in x, y and their inverses, e(u, v) says two names are one place, and a frozen census of the 1,024 Life-like one-bit laws
    and the atom reads which boards each allows; a proof ticket asks whether a law that names no relation can force an infinite board
    without allowing the tree.
  - 2026-11-10 · Relied on by `READ-GROW-20261110-001`: a growing plane of rings on a declared board reads its original rings'
    distances in order while every stage keeps its knots (to 40 × 40). In a pause the reading is the board's own taxicab distance
    (lags 12|v| − 4), recovered, not chosen; after a doubling it is not taxicab (a diagonal ring heard before a straight one), so the
    growth law shapes what is read, but the board is still drawn ([reading distance while growing](../research/READ-GROW-20261110-001/read_grow.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added at the owner's question whether the axes can follow rules, recording a default every space epic since
    `LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001` took.

### A52 · A grown handle is a class-forced torus piece

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · scope

- **Statement.** When learning is paired with growth, the growth move splices a torus piece, a torus drawn knot pattern less one distinction,
  whose torus has exactly one sign class that no state satisfies and is free at the removed distinction (flipping the sign of any one of
  the distinction's three sides leaves a satisfiable law). The piece brings a Kasteleyn law of its torus, the old signs are kept, and the three new sides take the
  signs that leave no even face. The Fano piece (7 distinctions) and the thin-torus pieces of even index (4 to 40 distinctions) are such
  pieces. The experimenter chooses the piece and the knot.
- **Why.** `OTHER-ATOMS-20261110-001` put it in place of A48: for exactly these pieces the piece rule holds on any host (proved, given
  freeness, which was checked for every lattice torus to 30 distinctions), and thin-torus pieces wrote and held addresses as the Fano piece does
  ([other pieces](../research/OTHER-ATOMS-20261110-001/other_atoms.md)). A piece whose torus satisfies every class is transparent in all
  four classes and holds nothing, so it is not a memory.
- **Rests on it.** The learning line's results from `GROW-HANDLE-20261110-001` to `ADDRESS-SEQ-20261110-001`, now read for any such piece;
  the reading that growth alone does not remove a sphere's dissonance
  ([learning that grows a handle](../research/GROW-HANDLE-20261110-001/grow_handle.md)); backlog B98 and B104.
- **Challenge.** Pieces of genus 2 or more (a grown drawn knot pattern less a distinction), tori that are not lattice quotients, a torus that is not free, or a
  growth law that chooses the piece itself (backlog B106). The cheapest test grows a knot sphere carrying two Fano pieces, less a distinction, as
  one piece (backlog B104): if it acts only where both inner pieces act, the premise extends to pieces of pieces.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "A grown handle is a class-forced torus atom" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · `NATIVE-ATOM-20261110-001` read the choice: a uniform growth rule (one sign pattern on every line, fired at a violated line)
    grows a remembering atom only with the Fano atom, the one handle whose plain law contradicts itself; every other class-forced atom grows
    transparent and needs its class supplied, which learning does ([which atom a growth law grows](../research/NATIVE-ATOM-20261110-001/native_atom.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · `NEST-ATOMS-20261110-001` extended the premise to composite atoms: a knot sphere carrying such atoms, less a point, acts
    as its line exactly where every atom inside acts (proved by hand, given flat states at every line of the host), so groups of atoms
    behind a three-edge cut are atoms of the next level ([atoms within atoms](../research/NEST-ATOMS-20261110-001/nest_atoms.md)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `OTHER-ATOMS-20261110-001`, replacing A48 (overturned as a limit).

### A53 · Nativeness of a board made of names is counted per name

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · reading

- **Statement.** When the board is made of identity distinctions on names, the source-aware reading counts a change by the name
  variables it flips. A value at a cell of a board other than the tree is one variable per name, so flipping it flips infinitely
  many. Under this count no distinction of a state off the tree, and no identity distinction anywhere, is native.
- **Why.** The source-aware reading (adopted 2026-10-20) is stated for the variables of the system, and in this encoding the variables
  belong to names. `BOARD-DIST-20261110-001` applied it as written; the alternative, a count per cell, was not mapped.
- **Rests on it.** Theorem 5's corollary in
  [boards of laws that name no relation](../proofs/BOARD-DIST-20261110-001A/BOARD-DIST-20261110-001A-P001_boards_of_laws_that_name_no_relation/proof.md),
  and the reading that the 60 knots between the square's identity distinction and the values are not native
  ([the board as distinctions](../research/BOARD-DIST-20261110-001/board_dist.md)).
- **Challenge.** Count changes per cell: finitely many cells' values changed with the board fixed, and identifications at finitely
  many cells. Re-read the census's square knots under that count. If they become native, a board made of distinctions can carry
  native knots, and the tension between the owner's idea and the source-aware reading lifts.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Answered by `PLACE-FLIP-20261110-001` ([nativeness up to renaming](../research/PLACE-FLIP-20261110-001/place_flip.md)), for boards of
    abstract names: counting changes per place with the board fixed, as the challenge asks, every board-value knot of the 90 one-bit
    count laws is native in some world of finite pieces, while on the free board 58 of those laws let no value change by any
    finite change. The board's own neighbour distinctions are native in place everywhere, through the switch.
  - 2026-11-10 · Bearing from `BARE-NAMES-20261110-001` ([abstract names](../research/BARE-NAMES-20261110-001/bare_names.md)): without letters
    places are names, and a board changes by switching two edges with one pair of values, so its knots pass the source-aware
    condition (90 one-bit count laws knot a neighbour distinction with its two endpoint values). The frozen boards that rest on
    this entry rest on the naming (A54). Renamings are then finite changes as well, so the condition excludes nothing (A59).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `BOARD-DIST-20261110-001`, which took the per-name count by default.

### A54 · Cells are named by words in two letters

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-10 · scope

- **Statement.** In the challenge of A51 the cells are named by the reduced words in x, y and their inverses. Every name then has
  four neighbour names, so the law's neighbourhood is fixed by the naming, and every board is a quotient of the tree of names.
  Confirmed for the obstruction: without letters no finitary law and no count law forces the plane either. The naming draws
  four neighbours a law can address and one connected world, and it freezes the board.
- **Why.** Backlog B92's first test chose this naming. A finitary uniform law needs a bounded neighbourhood, and the naming supplies one
  without a clause that names a degree. `BOARD-DIST-20261110-001` kept it.
- **Rests on it.** Not the obstruction itself, which holds without letters (`BARE-NAMES-20261110-001`). What does rest on it:
  the frozen boards of `BOARD-DIST-20261110-001` (its Theorem 5) and A53's reading; finite boards forced by finitary laws
  (its Theorem 4); every board being connected; and the stabilizer readings of `LEIBNIZ-BOARDS-20261110-001`,
  `RIGID-WORLDS-20261110-001` and `WEAK-APER-20261110-001`, which use the letters' group
  ([the board as distinctions](../research/BOARD-DIST-20261110-001/board_dist.md)).
- **Challenge.** Name cells without letters, with a neighbour distinction for each pair and laws that name no degree. The question
  is then whether a finitary law can force a bounded degree at all, and whether the unfolding theorem holds there. If the theorem
  fails, the naming was a hidden drawing; if it holds, the obstruction does not depend on the naming.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "Places are named by words in two letters" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Confirmed for the obstruction by `BARE-NAMES-20261110-001` ([abstract names](../research/BARE-NAMES-20261110-001/bare_names.md)), by
    the rule frozen in its pre-registration. Without letters every finitary law that allows a state allows a structureless one,
    every count law allows the tree, and a named face gives planes only componentwise (proved), so the obstruction does not
    depend on the naming. The naming draws neighbours a law can address and one connected world, and the frozen boards rest on
    it: without letters two edges with one pair of values exchange partners, a finite change.
  - 2026-11-10 · Bearing from `WEAK-APER-20261110-001` ([weakly aperiodic laws](../research/WEAK-APER-20261110-001/weak_aperiodic.md)): with
    this naming, under Leibniz's rule, the tree is a board of every law with infinitely many solutions (proved). The tree is the
    naming's own shape, so the challenge, names without letters, is the remaining test of whether the obstruction belongs to the
    naming.
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `BOARD-DIST-20261110-001`.

### A55 · Braun's bit-PHP preprint is correct

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · premise

- **Statement.** The lemmas of Braun's preprint "An exponential lower bound for the bit pigeonhole principle in resolution over
  parities" (arXiv 2609.23015) hold as stated: the generic criterion (Theorem 9.1, Corollary 9.2), the degree-preserving decoder
  transfer, the stability of the unary functional system below degree n/2, the matching-moment extension and the binary cube degree
  drop.
- **Why.** The preprint is unrefereed, but its main theorem and dependencies are formalized in Lean, and this program's audit
  (`BPHP-RES-PARITY-AUDIT-20261019-001`) replayed the formalization from source, checked its definitions against standard Res(⊕) and
  found no gap. The prose proof was not checked line by line.
- **Rests on it.** `HOLE-CRITERION-COLOURING-20261019-001B` (bit PHP with more pigeons), `SEP-LEFT-20261110-001A` (Theorem 2),
  `HOLE-SEP-20261110-001A` (Theorem (R)), `KERNEL-FRAC-20261110-001A` (the cap) and `WIDE-LEFT-20261110-001A` (the dag-like Res(⊕) lower
  bound for the binary left-pigeon principle).
- **Challenge.** Each lemma used above is among the Lean declarations the audit replayed (checked 2026-11-10). Still open: have the prose of §§3, 4 and 9 read by a Res(⊕) expert; a gap in any of them would reopen the results that rest on it.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-10 · Evidence for, status unchanged (`AUDIT-LEFT-20261110-001`): every lemma listed is among the declarations replayed in a
    fresh kernel (the audited aggregate imports the generic criterion), and their Lean statements cover the uses, any number of
    rows included. ECCC TR26-316 (Alekseev, Gryaznov, Itsykson, Pitassi, Robere and Tzameret, 10 November 2026) re-proves the main
    theorem in a stronger system, and Pang (arXiv 2610.00837) builds on it; both are unrefereed, and the generic criterion's prose has
    had no outside reading.
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `WIDE-LEFT-20261110-001`, whose bound rests on it.

### A56 · Time in a growing plane is counted at the plane's own scale

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · reading

- **Statement.** In a plane grown by the doubling law, a lag taken after k doublings counts as 2^-k of a lag: conformal time
  phi(t) = sum of 2^-k(tau) over the lags before t, where k(tau) is the number of doublings so far. Time lengths read in this clock
  are the plane's distances at its first scale.
- **Why.** `EXPAND-READ-20261110-001` took it by default to test whether the triangle excess of a growing plane's time lengths
  (`METRIC-GROW-20261110-001`) is its expansion and nothing else. The weight is computed from the law's own doublings, which the declared
  schedule fixes, not from a metric supplied by a simulator.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's test X3, whether the round trip in conformal time is a metric while the plane grows.
- **Challenge.** The doubling count is part of a declared schedule; a native clock would have to be read by the network itself, for
  instance from the time a front takes to cross one ring, which X2 measures. If that crossing time doubles exactly at each doubling,
  the network's own crossings count conformal time and the choice is not an observer's; if not, another clock would do differently.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-10 · Used by `CONF-METRIC-20261110-001`, which proved that in this clock the growing plane's first arrivals between
    original rings are 4g times their taxicab distance up to a constant, for every schedule and size, so the round trip is a metric up to
    42g + 4 ([the conformal metric as a theorem](../research/CONF-METRIC-20261110-001/conf_metric.md)). Kept as Assumed: the clock is a
    reading, now with a theorem about what it reads.
  - 2026-11-10 · Tested by `EXPAND-READ-20261110-001` (exploratory, exact, 891 schedules): every ring-to-ring crossing time doubles at
    each doubling (4g 2^k lags per original spacing in stage k, 2,368 of 2,368 readings), so the network's own crossings count this clock,
    as the challenge asked; in it the round trip breaks the triangle inequality in 28 schedules by at most 2 lags, against 240 schedules
    and up to 78 lags in plain lags ([expansion as a reading](../research/EXPAND-READ-20261110-001/expand_read.md)). Kept as Assumed.
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `EXPAND-READ-20261110-001`.

### A57 · An address asks for a bit per piece, each piece given by its region

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · reading

- **Statement.** An address asked of the layered memory is a bit for every piece, acting or not, and each piece is given to the learner
  by its region: its knots and every face they touch. The learner knows where each piece is and what is asked of it, and nothing about its
  class or role.
- **Why.** `NATIVE-WRITE-20261110-001` froze it before any run. With only the pieces asked to act named, a learner cannot tell the forced
  dissonance of an unnamed piece that acts from a free unit in the host, so it cannot check that the other pieces are clear; a memory word
  asks for its zeros as well as its ones.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's native learner, whose probe checks each piece asked 0 for a moment its region is free of dissonance
  ([native writing](../research/NATIVE-WRITE-20261110-001/native_write.md)).
- **Challenge.** Name only the pieces asked 1, as backlog B119 first phrased it ("a bias at the named pieces only"), and ask whether a
  learner can still write exactly, for instance by testing whether dissonance outside the named pieces ever moves.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "An address asks for a bit per atom, each atom given by its region" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `NATIVE-WRITE-20261110-001`.

### A58 · The outside holds inputs as distinctions and reads an output at the floor

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · reading

- **Statement.** An input-output interface of the layered memory is the three outer distinctions of a piece or composite. The outside
  holds two of them at values (the inputs), and the third is read at the floor (every knot satisfied, no even face) as decided (every floor state
  gives it one value), undecided (both values occur) or excluded (no floor state has those inputs). A learner is taught by the outside
  holding the inputs and the output it asks for.
- **Why.** `IN-OUT-20261110-001` froze it before any run. A role depends on the law alone, so held inputs cannot change it, and readings of
  roles are monotone (theorem M of `NOT-INSIDE-20261110-001`); a decided value alone allows, for two inputs, only copying or negating one
  of them (theorem D there). The undecided reading is what a gate does: an acting piece forces its third side exactly when the held sides
  agree.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's maps and its learning from examples
  ([inputs and outputs](../research/IN-OUT-20261110-001/in_out.md)).
- **Challenge.** Read the output differently: as the dissonance near it, as a decided value with more inputs (three held inputs allow
  majority and parity under theorem D), or as a probability over floor states; or let the outside hold inputs as signs rather than distinctions.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "The world holds inputs as points and reads an output at the floor" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `IN-OUT-20261110-001`.

### A59 · Renamings count as finite changes under abstract names

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-10 · reading

- **Statement.** When cells are names without letters, the source-aware reading counts a change by the distinctions it flips, as
  written. Exchanging finitely many names is then a finite change, so in a state with infinitely many copies of each cell every
  distinction passes the condition, which then excludes nothing.
- **Why.** `BARE-NAMES-20261110-001` applied the reading as written. A count up to renaming was not mapped.
- **Rests on it.** Nothing now: the 90 knots between a neighbour distinction and its two endpoint values found by that epic's census
  pass the source-aware condition through the exchanges of values ([abstract names](../research/BARE-NAMES-20261110-001/bare_names.md)),
  but each is also native in place, without renamings, in some world of finite pieces (`PLACE-FLIP-20261110-001`).
- **Challenge.** Count changes up to renaming: a variable is native when a finite change that is not a renaming flips it. The switch
  of two edges with one pair of values still flips neighbour distinctions; ask which values of the 90 laws are flipped by local
  moves without renaming.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Confirmed for the 90 knots by `PLACE-FLIP-20261110-001` ([nativeness up to renaming](../research/PLACE-FLIP-20261110-001/place_flip.md)):
    counted in place (A60), with renamings excluded, every one of them is native in some world of copies of a finite board, so
    none rests on renamings. On the free board 58 of the laws freeze their values, which no count of renamings changes.
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `BARE-NAMES-20261110-001`.

### A60 · Nativeness under abstract names is counted in place

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · reading

- **Statement.** When cells are names without letters, a distinction counts as native at a state when a finite change flips it that
  changes one kind of distinction only, values with the board kept or neighbour distinctions with the values kept, is not a
  renaming, and is minimal. The source-aware reading as written cannot tell a renaming from a change; this is the count used in its
  cell.
- **Why.** `PLACE-FLIP-20261110-001` froze it before any run, at the coordinator's request. Excluding renamings alone is not enough,
  since a cell can be transplanted by a finite rewiring that is not a renaming, so the count holds one kind of distinction fixed.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's marks: all 90 board-value knots of `BARE-NAMES-20261110-001B` native in place in some world of finite
  pieces, 26 native in place in some tree world and 64 in none, 58 laws rigid on every tree ([nativeness up to renaming](../research/PLACE-FLIP-20261110-001/place_flip.md)).
- **Challenge.** Count a change by whether it changes the world up to isomorphism, or allow both kinds of distinction to change in
  one minimal change that is not a transplant, and ask whether the marks move. It is a reading of the canonical source-aware
  condition, not a change to it; any change to the canonical test is the owner's.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `PLACE-FLIP-20261110-001`.

### A61 · Closing a cycle in a growth step declares that cycle

**Assumed** · 2026-11-10 · reading

- **Statement.** A growth step of an accretion law declares a cycle when it closes one, by joining a new cell to two or more existing
  cells or by joining two existing cells. Since the cycle runs through the step's view, the step fixes it. Merging cells and
  changing existing values are outside accretion.
- **Why.** `GROW-FACE-20261110-001` froze it before any computation, at the coordinator's request, as what would count as growth
  forcing the plane without a declared cycle. Under it a law that declares no cycle grows trees, so growth needs a declared cycle by
  definition; the epic's content is what laws that do declare cycles force.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's verdict for growth, and with it the board line's closing summary ([every accretion law](../research/GROW-FACE-20261110-001/grow_face.md)).
- **Challenge.** Read declaring more finely: a rule declares a cycle only when it fixes the cycle's length or offsets. Then ask whether a law
  that closes whatever short cycles it sees, without coordinates, can grow the plane in every fair order; such a law would show the
  cycle emerging rather than written in. Merging cells whose views agree while growing, a Leibniz rule, is another way to close
  cycles that no step fixes.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "Closing a cycle in a growth step names a face" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-10 · Added by `GROW-FACE-20261110-001`.

### A62 · A law gives a meeting pattern only by forcing it

**Assumed** · 2026-11-11 · reading

- **Statement.** Question 9 asks which uniform local law gives a meeting pattern that looks like a space of low dimension. Every
  epic on it has read "gives" as "forces": a law gives the plane, a round circle or a sphere (the drawn targets) only when every
  world it allows is of that kind, so a law that also allows the plane's tori, its covering tree or a long capped tube does not give it. This is a reading of the
  question, not of the knot test; the canonical K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** Taken by default and never compared with an alternative, from the pinwheel and sphere epics of 2026-11-07 to the board
  and growth lines of 2026-11-10, whose verdicts are all stated as forcing
  ([every accretion law](../research/GROW-FACE-20261110-001/grow_face.md)). Recorded on 2026-11-11, at the coordinator's decision,
  by a read-only review of why the space lines parked; no epic tested it.
- **Rests on it.** The negative verdicts of question 9 as worded: no undecorated pinwheel law forces roundness (A25); no law
  without hidden states tells the knot spheres from capped tubes
  ([sphere growth](../research/SPHERE-GROWTH-20261107-001/sphere_growth.md)); a law that names no relation allows the tree
  whenever it allows an infinite board ([the board as distinctions](../research/BOARD-DIST-20261110-001/board_dist.md),
  [weakly aperiodic laws](../research/WEAK-APER-20261110-001/weak_aperiodic.md),
  [abstract names](../research/BARE-NAMES-20261110-001/bare_names.md)); and growth chooses the plane only by declaring a cycle. Under
  this reading those verdicts are close to automatic: a law built from windows allows a state on a world exactly when every window
  is fine, so it allows the same states on every world whose windows look alike, a ring twice as long or a cover included. No
  bounded law tells a long chain from a ring ([growth as a law](../research/GROWTH-LAW-20261107-001/growth_law.md)).
- **Challenge.** Read question 9 under A15, where nothing selects one world: a law gives a meeting pattern when it is among the worlds
  it allows and some local feature (the link at each distinction, its curvature, the dimension bond balls read) is the same in
  every world it allows, with the global pattern and size left co-real. Under that reading the locality results answer the question
  rather than block it where every world has proper covers (flat and hyperbolic surfaces, d >= 6): there a law fixes local
  geometry and never size or the global pattern. At positive curvature (d <= 5) the worlds are a classical finite list with no proper
  cover, so a law can fix the global pattern and size outright (corrected 2026-11-11). The cheapest discriminator is backlog B144, a census
  of worlds made of knots alone, which asks what every allowed world shares. The entry is overturned if question 9 is restated
  under the co-real reading, and confirmed if a question about meeting patterns is found that only the forcing reading can pose.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "A law gives a shape only by forcing it", and its statement and challenge said shape for what is now a meeting pattern ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Challenged by `KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001`, status unchanged: at positive curvature forcing works, since a world
    with no cover can be fixed outright (the surface condition and d leave the classical list, and the not-all-equal law removes the
    projective plane, so at d = 5 it allows only the icosahedron); at d >= 6 no law fixes size, and that law does not fix
    orientability among flat worlds, so only the co-real reading says what it gives there
    ([knots alone](../research/KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001/knots_alone.md)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Challenge narrowed: "never global shape" holds only where every world has proper covers; at d <= 5 the
    classical finite lists fix shape and size (found by `KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001`'s pre-registration).
  - 2026-11-11 · Added by the read-only review of the parked space lines, at the coordinator's decision; nothing started.

### A63 · A declared cycle is put in, not native

**Overturned** · 2026-11-11 · reading

- **Statement.** A law that declares a cycle, by fixing which cycle of cells closes (a relation of the letters, a cycle length or
  offsets, or a cycle closed in a growth step as in A61), puts that cycle in: the cycle is part of what the law is given, not
  something it builds. Under this reading "the commuting board needs a declared cycle" is an obstruction. This is a reading of what counts as
  drawn, not a change to the knot test; the canonical K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** Taken by default in the board line of 2026-11-10, whose verdict reads declaring a cycle as the input the line could not
  remove ([every accretion law](../research/GROW-FACE-20261110-001/grow_face.md)). The opposite reading was taken, also by
  default, in the uniform-spheres epic of 2026-11-07, where faces were declared co-lines and a law of distinctions, knots and co-lines
  was said to build its own closed surface with nothing drawn, flat for co-line sizes (3,3,3), (2,4,4) and (2,3,6)
  ([uniform spheres](../research/UNIFORM-SPHERES-20261107-001/uniform_spheres.md)). Recorded on 2026-11-11, at the coordinator's
  decision, by a read-only review that found the two lines reading the same fact in opposite ways.
- **Rests on it.** The board line's verdict as an obstruction, "a uniform local law, static or grown, chooses the plane only by
  naming a face" (in the glossary's words, by declaring a cycle) ([the board as distinctions](../research/BOARD-DIST-20261110-001/board_dist.md),
  [abstract names](../research/BARE-NAMES-20261110-001/bare_names.md)); A61's definition of declaring; and the reading that the
  plane's declared cycle is the input space from a law cannot remove.
- **Challenge.** Count a declared cycle as native when its relation passes K1–K3 over the cycle's distinctions: a parity or not-all-equal
  relation around a cycle of open distinctions is then a knot, and a three-place knot already spans a triangle. Under that reading
  the board line's verdict says the plane is built from knots on its cycles, and the uniform-spheres result is the positive form of the same
  fact. The cheapest discriminator is backlog B144: patterns of three-place knots alone, with no declared cycles, read for the cycles
  their knots span. The entry is overturned if such patterns fix the literature's flat local geometry with nothing named but the knot, and confirmed if
  a knot on a cycle is shown to need an input the knot test excludes.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "A named face is put in, not native" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Overturned in the knots-alone setting, conditional on A64 (the surface count names no face, Assumed), with the
    surface count given and on computed evidence in range, by `LONG-LOOP-20261111-001`'s frozen rule, which applies this entry's
    registered criterion (flat local geometry fixed with nothing named but the knot). The face relation passed K1–K3 in the
    canonical form: the not-all-equal relation by `knotlab check`, and in every world each face knot as a bond of the allowed
    states with all other distinctions quantified away (frozen in advance); the canonical test is unchanged. The evidence: the not-all-equal law reads every 6-regular torus with 49 to 80 distinctions and Klein
    bottle with 19 to 40 of edge-width at least 5 faithfully (449 worlds), and on the 349 tori of edge-width at least 6 sets bond
    distance graph distance + 1 out to distance 3 (distance 2 proved given faithfulness). A face named only by a knot gives native
    flat local geometry; what must be put in is how knots meet (A64)
    ([the long-loop regime](../research/LONG-LOOP-20261111-001/long_loop.md)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Bears on it, status unchanged: the surface count cannot be dropped, since the not-all-equal law reads
    non-surface worlds faithfully too (`NAE-SURFACE-20261111-001`); any overturning of this entry keeps the count as an input
    ([does a faithful reading supply the surface?](../research/NAE-SURFACE-20261111-001/nae_surface.md)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Challenged by `KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001`, status unchanged: by its frozen rule the entry stands, since the
    not-all-equal law read 227 of 229 flat worlds without a non-facial triangle faithfully, not all, and its bond distance is graph
    distance + 1 only to distance 2. It came close: faithful on every flat world in range whose short loops have length at least 5
    (exploratory). What is still put in is the surface count (A64; backlog B145)
    ([knots alone](../research/KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001/knots_alone.md)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Added by the read-only review of the parked space lines, at the coordinator's decision; nothing started.

### A64 · Counting how knots meet at a distinction declares no cycle

**Assumed** · 2026-11-11 · reading

- **Statement.** In a knot pattern of knots alone, the two-fold count (every co-knotted pair in exactly two knots, the knots at
  each distinction one cycle) and the degree d (every distinction in d knots) are counts of how knots meet at a distinction; they
  declare no cycle. The face, in the drawing's word, is the knot itself. The canonical K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** Taken by default in `KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001`'s pre-registration, which read the two-fold count as an incidence
  count like d, so that a law with it names nothing but its knot ([knots alone](../research/KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001/knots_alone.md)).
  The alternative, in the spirit of A61's finer reading, counts a link's fixed length as a declared dual cycle.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's reading that the not-all-equal law reads the literature's flat local geometry natively with nothing named but the
  knot and the two-fold count, and A63's overturning in the knots-alone setting (`LONG-LOOP-20261111-001`), which holds only while
  this entry does.
- **Challenge.** Ask whether the count can be dropped: if no pattern that is not a two-fold weave is read faithfully by the not-all-equal law
  at d ≥ 4, the count is a consequence of reading the knots natively and this entry is confirmed in substance; a faithful
  pattern that is not a two-fold weave shows that the count is an input, to be weighed under the finer reading (backlog B145).
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "Counting how knots meet at a distinction names no face" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Bearing from `OVERLAP-READ-20261111-001`, status unchanged: the not-all-equal law's states depend only on how many
    knots lie on each pair (proved: two knot patterns with the same count on every pair have the same states), so a count of how
    knots meet is exactly what this law reads, and it cannot tell apart two patterns with the same counts
    ([what not-all-equal makes of overlapping bases](../research/OVERLAP-READ-20261111-001/overlap_read.md)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Challenged again by `GROW-COUNT-20261111-001`, status unchanged: growth does not supply the count either. Worlds
    grown by knots kept only while the not-all-equal law reads them faithfully accept pairs in three knots and pinched links, and
    with both named close links at every degree from 3 to 14
    ([the count from growth](../research/GROW-COUNT-20261111-001/grow_count.md)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Challenged by `NAE-SURFACE-20261111-001`, status unchanged: the count is an input. 160 of 822 degree-preserving
    knot swaps of faithful flat tori are read faithfully by the not-all-equal law though none is a surface, so a faithful reading
    does not supply the count; whether counting is naming is untouched
    ([does a faithful reading supply the surface?](../research/NAE-SURFACE-20261111-001/nae_surface.md)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Added by `KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001`.

### A65 · A held reference distinction is supplied by the outside

**Assumed** · 2026-11-11 · reading

- **Statement.** In a drawn knot pattern whose law is closed under flipping every distinction (the not-all-equal law of the
  learning line), a reference is a distinction the outside holds at one fixed value while it holds the inputs, and a map read with a reference is read at that
  value only. The reference is an input supplied by the outside, like the held inputs; the law does not produce it. The canonical K1–K3
  test is unchanged.
- **Why.** Taken by default in `HOLD-COUNT-20261111-001`'s pre-registration, to ask what one fixed value buys a drawn knot pattern that, by
  theorem D of `IN-OUT-20261110-001`, can read a count only up to its mirror. The alternative is a reference made natively, a distinction
  whose value the law or learning fixes; the not-all-equal law cannot fix one, being the only three-place knot with no preferred value
  (`PINNING-20261031-001`, `MUTUAL-SELECTION-20261031-001`).
- **Rests on it.** `HOLD-COUNT-20261111-001`'s readings with a reference (the count mod 3 and the least spaces holding it), and the
  reading in backlog B148 that a reference distinction turns self-dual maps into all maps.
- **Challenge.** Look for a native reference: a space or a learned frame in which one distinction's value is fixed in every floor state
  without the outside holding it, or a law with a preferred value that keeps the knot's other properties. Finding one would make the
  counts read here native; a proof that no complement-closed law fixes a value would confirm that the reference is always an input.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Its other fields reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Reworded under the owner's language rule, status unchanged: the title was "A held reference point is supplied by the world" ([glossary](../INTUITIONS.md#glossary)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Used by `HOLD-COUNT-20261111-001`, status unchanged: one reference point held at 0 gives no count of three held points
    mod 3 or mod 4 on any of its 1,128 spaces; the maps of four held points there never use the reference as a count mod 3 would
    ([what it takes to hold a count](../research/HOLD-COUNT-20261111-001/hold_count.md)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Added by `HOLD-COUNT-20261111-001`.

### A66 · Copies sharing distinctions keep each copy's own drawn faces

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** When copies of drawn knot patterns share distinctions (a distinction of one copy is the same distinction as one of
  another), the dissonance the learner feels is the sum of the copies' own: violated knots, plus each copy's faces, which belong to that
  copy's drawing, read as in that copy alone. A shared distinction's knots lie around one face-cycle per copy, not one in all. The
  canonical K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** Taken by default in `SHARED-COUNT-20261111-001`'s pre-registration, so that the learning line's learner, whose dissonance
  counts the drawing's faces, runs unchanged on the first overlapping bases. On a base with no drawing there are no faces, and the
  natural dissonance is violated knots alone; that reading is the alternative.
- **Rests on it.** `SHARED-COUNT-20261111-001`'s learning runs on copies sharing distinctions (the floor states are the satisfying
  states either way, since each copy's law is a Kasteleyn law in the literature's term; what changes is the path the learner takes).
- **Challenge.** Run the same learning with dissonance from violated knots alone, on copies sharing distinctions and on bases with no
  drawing (sub-goal B153); if learning succeeds or fails the same way, the faces were inessential; if it differs, the learner's success
  depends on them.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Confirmed by `UNDRAWN-LEARN-20261111-001`, for this reason: with dissonance at violated knots alone and no faces, the
    learner held the count in 0 of 10 runs on one copy and 0 of 10 on two copies sharing a distinction, where reading the faces it held
    10 of 10 on each; the faces did real work. Reopenable by a drawing-free learner that holds these counts
    ([learning without a drawing](../research/UNDRAWN-LEARN-20261111-001/undrawn_learn.md)).
  - 2026-11-11 · Under challenge by `UNDRAWN-LEARN-20261111-001` (sub-goal B153): the credit learner with dissonance at knots alone,
    on one copy, on two copies sharing a distinction, and on a base that cannot be drawn.
  - 2026-11-11 · Used by `SHARED-COUNT-20261111-001`, status unchanged: with each copy's faces in the dissonance, the count of five held
    distinctions was learned and held through one shared distinction in 10 of 10 runs, and the count of seven not within two hours
    ([counting through a shared distinction](../research/SHARED-COUNT-20261111-001/shared_count.md)). The statement, first written with
    "two-fold weaves" and "closed tours", now names drawn knot patterns and their faces, the glossary's words (a wording correction, Not a
    block).
  - 2026-11-11 · Added by `SHARED-COUNT-20261111-001`.

### A67 · A base is read on sets of at most three distinctions

**Assumed** · 2026-11-11 · scope

- **Statement.** What a law adds to a base is read on sets of at most three distinctions: frozen distinctions, tied pairs and hollow
  triples no knot names. A base is read faithfully when it has none of these and its knots' pairs are free (KNOTS-ALONE's
  definition). Constraints that first appear on four or more distinctions are not counted as added structure.
- **Why.** Taken by default from `KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001`'s definition of a faithful reading, and kept by
  `OVERLAP-READ-20261111-001`, whose catalogue of what the not-all-equal law forces stops at three distinctions
  ([what not-all-equal makes of overlapping bases](../research/OVERLAP-READ-20261111-001/overlap_read.md)).
- **Rests on it.** Every faithfulness verdict of the knots-alone line (`KNOTS-ALONE-20261111-001` to `OVERLAP-READ-20261111-001`)
  and the catalogue's counts; the rulers do not, since bond distance already reads larger sets.
- **Challenge.** Catalogue the minimal bonds of four and five distinctions a law adds, as the rulers already measure them: in the
  overlapping bases read faithfully, some pairs at graph distance 2 lie in no bond of five or fewer, so larger sets carry what the
  three-set reading misses.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-11 · Added by `OVERLAP-READ-20261111-001`.

### A68 · A repair flips distinctions only

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** After the learner stops and the world disturbs it, the repair that brings it back to rest flips distinctions only and
  never changes a sign of the law. The learner still changes signs while it learns. This is a rule supplied by us, not a property of the
  base. The canonical K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** `LAW-RIGIDITY-20261112-001` found, on one copy of the drawn knot pattern (2, 0, 4), that a learner resting only where the faces
  allow finds the count in 10 of 10 runs but holds it in only 4 of 10, because its repair re-signs knots; with this rule all 30 writes held.
  On a base with no drawing nothing makes a sign change cost more than a flip, so the rule stands in for the drawing's rigidity until a
  native source is found. The alternative is a repair free to re-sign, as before.
- **Rests on it.** `LAW-RIGIDITY-20261112-001`'s stage 2 (native rest sets on one copy and on a base with no drawing): with this rule a
  learned law holds whenever the repair reaches rest, by definition.
- **Challenge.** Find a native reason for a learned law to stay put on a base with no drawing: a rule of the knot pattern that makes a sign
  change cost more than a flip near rest, or a learner whose signs change on a slower clock for a stated native reason; if it holds as well,
  this rule is unneeded.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Returned to Assumed by `NATIVE-GRADE-20261112-001`, the evidence splitting by base: with a graded value-free rule of the
    knots and a free repair, the 6-distinction undrawn base held the count at the first target in 8 of 10 runs (passing the frozen mark;
    19 of 30 writes), so a native grade can do this rule's work there; one copy of (2, 0, 4), where no place lies on two shortest sequences,
    held 6 of 10 (17 of 30), so this rule is still needed there ([a native grade for rigidity](../research/NATIVE-GRADE-20261112-001/native_grade.md)).
  - 2026-11-12 · Under challenge by `NATIVE-GRADE-20261112-001` (sub-goal B160): a graded, value-free rule of the knots with the learner's
    own repair free to re-sign.
  - 2026-11-12 · Used by `LAW-RIGIDITY-20261112-001`, status unchanged: with it, a native rest set let the learner learn and hold the count
    in 30 of 30 writes on the 6-distinction base no drawing fits; sub-goal B160 would challenge it
    ([what does the drawing's work](../research/LAW-RIGIDITY-20261112-001/law_rigidity.md)).
  - 2026-11-12 · "Why" corrected: R held the count in 4 of 10 runs, not 5 (a miscount when the entry was written; Not a block).
  - 2026-11-12 · Added by `LAW-RIGIDITY-20261112-001`.

### A69 · The value of the shortest sums is supplied per base

**Overturned** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** In `LAW-RIGIDITY-20261112-001`'s stage 2 the learner rested only where the law's sum around every shortest cyclic
  sequence of knots and distinctions takes one value, and that value was chosen by us for each base: 0 on the 6-distinction undrawn base,
  1 on one copy of (2, 0, 4). The canonical K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** Each value was the only one of the two that left a counting law at rest on that base (exact); neither serves both.
- **Rests on it.** `LAW-RIGIDITY-20261112-001`'s stage-2 runs (30 of 30 on each base).
- **Challenge.** A value-free rule, the learner resting where all shortest sums are equal whatever the value
  (`NATIVE-GRADE-20261112-001`); it leaves a counting law at rest on both bases (exact).
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Overturned by `NATIVE-GRADE-20261112-001` for the two bases it was used on: resting where all shortest sums are equal,
    whatever the value, leaves a counting law at rest on both (4 of 64 laws on one copy, 1 of 2 on the undrawn base, exact), so no value
    need be chosen; on two further undrawn bases no shortest-sum rule leaves a counting law at rest at all ([a native grade for rigidity](../research/NATIVE-GRADE-20261112-001/native_grade.md)).
  - 2026-11-12 · Added, and put under challenge, by `NATIVE-GRADE-20261112-001` (recorded at the coordinator's request; the choice was
    made in `LAW-RIGIDITY-20261112-001`).

### A70 · A harmony is a periodic base whose allowed states all repeat

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** The owner's "harmony" is read as a periodic base along one shift (one block of distinctions and knots at every
  whole number) all of whose allowed states repeat, so that it has finitely many states up to shifting (the literature's finite
  subshift). Its periods are the periods of its states, not of its meeting pattern. Not the harmony of proof theory. The canonical
  K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** Proposed by the coordinator on 2026-11-12, after the owner asked what subtraction and division of logical harmonies are,
  and taken by default in `HARMONY-ARITH-20261112-001`'s pre-registration: a repeat the law makes, not one drawn into the meeting
