  pattern, is the closest finite object to "a pattern that repeats every p steps". Alternatives: a harmony as a repeat of the meeting
  pattern itself (a finite repeat of period p), as a relation where incommensurable parts cancel (the owner's 2026-10-22 picture), or
  as the relation between stitched parts rather than a single base.
- **Rests on it.** `HARMONY-ARITH-20261112-001`'s readings of which periods the law's harmonies have and what stitching them does.
- **Challenge.** Rerun the questions with a harmony read as a finite repeat of period p, or as a signed standing wave; if the
  arithmetic read (least common multiple, offsets, the mirror's power of 2) changes, the reading carries the result.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Used by `EVEN-PERIOD-20261112-001`, status unchanged: under this reading no not-all-equal harmony has an odd period up
    to 29 ([why the knot law's harmonies are even](../research/EVEN-PERIOD-20261112-001/even_period.md)).
  - 2026-11-12 · Used by `HARMONY-ARITH-20261112-001`, status unchanged: under this reading stitching gives least common multiples and
    offsets by definition, and the not-all-equal law's harmonies have even periods only to span 12
    ([arithmetic of harmonies](../research/HARMONY-ARITH-20261112-001/harmony_arith.md)).
  - 2026-11-12 · Added by `HARMONY-ARITH-20261112-001`.

### A71 · Each random process is one weighting of many

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · measure

- **Statement.** When the record says an outcome or a reading is typical or rare among meeting patterns, it means typical or
  rare under one named random process: uniform knots on a fixed set of distinctions, growth with new distinctions and closings
  anywhere, growth along co-knotted pairs, or growth among the newest distinctions. The weighting each census uses is one of
  many, and a statement holds for its process only. Growth among the newest distinctions also comes with the order in which
  distinctions arrive, so whatever order it reads comes with that process. The canonical K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** The owner's view is that every meeting pattern exists and ours is one random sample, with nothing special about it
  (2026-11-12), so the question is what is typical and what is rare. There is no uniform weighting over infinitely many
  patterns, so a census names the weighting it uses; `RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001` uses several and says which results depend on
  which ([pre-registration](../research/RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001/preregistration.md)). The alternative is to state only
  what holds for every pattern of a kind, as the proofs do.
- **Rests on it.** Every "typical", "rare" and every share of runs in `RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001`; not its proofs.
- **Challenge.** Find a result that changes between two weightings that allow the same patterns (for example uniform knots
  against growth to the same number of knots), or a weighting under which the readings stop depending on the process.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Used by `RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001`, status unchanged: outcomes and readings differ by weighting (uniform knots end in
    a jump over a fixed number of knots; growth that keeps adding distinctions carries large tie classes long before its end; only
    newest-four growth never ends), each stated for its process
    ([outcomes of random meeting patterns](../research/RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001/random_fates.md)); a shortest-description weighting
    is direction 3 there.
  - 2026-11-12 · Added by `RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001`.

### A72 · A growing pattern ends when it allows no state

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** A growing knot pattern ends, the owner's "dies", at the first knot after which it allows no state: it is
  then a contradiction (lesson 5.1). Its last base is the pattern just before that knot. The canonical K1–K3 test is
  unchanged.
- **Why.** The owner named three fates on 2026-11-12: some combinations "resolve to nothing", some "go for a while then
  die", and others "go on forever". Allowing no state is the one ending the law decides by itself. The other reading,
  asked of the owner and not yet answered, is that a pattern ends when it stops producing anything new (for example when
  no new knot changes what the law reads); `RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001` uses this one until the owner answers
  ([pre-registration](../research/RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001/preregistration.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The fates, lifetimes, death cores and last-base readings of `RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001`.
- **Challenge.** Read the same growths under the other ending: measure when new knots stop changing the tie classes or the
  bond distances, and compare those times with the times of no allowed state.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Confirmed by the owner: asked whether "dies" means that no state is allowed or that nothing new is produced, he
    said "This is fine. I don't know what the question is here." The other reading stays available as a measure, not as his
    meaning ([Intuitions, lesson 7.3](../INTUITIONS.md#73-every-meeting-pattern-none-chosen)).
  - 2026-11-12 · Used by `RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001`, status unchanged: under this reading uniform patterns end in a jump, the last
    base tying 87 to 89% of its distinctions into one class; the other reading is direction 5 there, awaiting the owner's answer
    ([outcomes of random meeting patterns](../research/RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001/random_fates.md)).
  - 2026-11-12 · Added by `RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001`.

### A73 · The owner's other realities are other knot patterns

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** The owner's "other realities" are read as other knot patterns of one kind of logic: distinctions and knots under
  the canonical K1–K3 test, differing in which knots meet (the meeting pattern) and possibly in their "binding patterns". The owner has
  said that what a binding pattern is "is what we're trying to discover", so it is an open definition with his two pictures: logic
  carried in each knot that binds whole knot patterns together, perhaps spreading influence between them in a way that does not add
  up part by part; or recursion, inner parts placed by the larger ones with no fixed number of dimensions. The working reading takes
  the first picture as the rules the knots carry. Where realities bind to each other, the binding is read as more meeting pattern: knots that
  share distinctions of both make one larger knot pattern. Two bases that share no distinction share no contact (A15). The
  canonical K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** The owner, 2026-11-11 (Pacific; 12 November UTC), asked whether other realities have a different law or the same law on
  a different meeting pattern: "Different meeting patterns! Yes! Both the underlying patterns and even possibly the binding patterns
  because there are infinite possibilities for both." "Binding patterns" can be read as the knots' rules or as bindings between
  realities; the coordinator took both, as above, and read "binding between realities is just more meeting pattern", which the
  owner accepted with "Yes I imagine it like we could have infinite recursions"
  ([Intuitions, lesson 7.3](../INTUITIONS.md#73-every-meeting-pattern-none-chosen)).
- **Rests on it.** Lesson 7.3's account of the owner's picture, and the reading that a census under one law (not-all-equal) studies
  one part of the owner's "everything", as `RANDOM-FATES-20261112-001` does.
- **Challenge.** Two ways. Find a binding pattern in either of the owner's pictures that is not a knot pattern on the distinctions it
  binds: a relation between realities that keeps them separate would make this reading wrong, and the foundation's rule that no
  contact is inferred across admissible bases would be the owner's to reopen. And test whether the law matters: rerun the census's outcomes and readings under the other symmetric knot laws
  on the same samples; if they differ in kind, "one kind of logic" has to name its law in every claim.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · The owner on "binding patterns", status unchanged: "This is what we're trying to discover. To me, it's like injected
    logic in each clause that binds a series of CNF together", possibly with influence "distributed between different CNFs in like a
    non-linear way", or else "bindings are actually recursive structures", like a fractal whose "nested structures depend on the
    larger structures to know where they are", but where logic "can hop around". Recorded as an open definition with two pictures
    ([Intuitions, lesson 7.3](../INTUITIONS.md#73-every-meeting-pattern-none-chosen)).
  - 2026-11-12 · Added from the owner's statements of 2026-11-11, recorded in lesson 7.3 of the Intuitions guide.

### A74 · Dimension across an unlengthened shift is read by across-counts

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** Where a law's balls are infinite because it does not lengthen one shift of a periodic base, the program reads
  dimension across that shift: by how many across-counts a ball of bond radius r reaches at far along-counts, as r grows. On base 16
  this gives zero dimensions along the unlengthened shift and one across it.
- **Why.** Dimension as how fast balls grow (lesson 4.6 of the Intuitions guide) is degenerate when a ball of bond radius 3 already
  holds infinitely many distinctions; counting the across-counts reached at far along-counts is the growth that remains, and it uses
  bond distance alone. Taken by default in `ACROSS-FOLD-20261112-001`
  ([decision note](../research/ACROSS-FOLD-20261112-001/across_fold.md)).
- **Rests on it.** That epic's answer, "one dimension across", and its reading for question 9.
- **Challenge.** Read dimension another way that also uses bond distance alone, such as the growth of a ball's distinctions within a
  bounded window of along-counts, or after identifying distinctions at bounded bond distance; or find bonds over several along-counts
  that cross more cheaply than heddles, which would change the rate but not the reading.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Added by `ACROSS-FOLD-20261112-001`.

### A75 · Bases within bases are stitched at one place

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** The owner's "each boolean variable could be its own micro infinite series of logic, that's bound to others" is read
  as: each distinction of an outer base becomes a copy of an inner harmony (A70), and each outer knot becomes a stitch of the three
  inner harmonies at every place, all at the same place (an unlagged stitch; the record first wrote "a stitch at one place"); lagged
  stitches are the variant tested beside it. Doing it again with the same harmony gives the base along k shifts whose every line is a state of it (the literature's axial
  power). The canonical K1–K3 test is unchanged.
- **Why.** Proposed by the coordinator on 2026-11-12, after the owner approved starting B164; the natural inner series is a harmony,
  and a stitch is how the line already binds harmonies (`HARMONY-ARITH-20261112-001`). The owner's later word on overlapping ("like
  fractal, or meeting patterns that are more complex than just triangle edges meeting ... globally influential") is tested by the
  epic's levels and its drawn three-copy nesting.
- **Rests on it.** `BASES-WITHIN-20261112-001`'s lemmas: stitched at one place, the inner harmonies keep the outer law exactly (the
  mirror lift), nesting never ends, and ties only multiply. Each of these is about this reading.
- **Challenge.** A substitution reading would differ: each distinction replaced by a finite block of distinctions, the outer knots
  binding chosen distinctions of the blocks, iterated toward a self-similar limit (the literature's substitution systems and
  self-simulating tile sets). Under it the mirror lift need not apply, and nesting could create forcing. Ask the owner which he meant,
  or run the substitution reading on the same seeds.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · The owner's answer, status unchanged (still open). Asked whether nested series are bound beside one another or
    inside one another: "We need to suss this out. Side might be misleading here [...] these are not sides in the typical "edge of a
    triangle" sense. We're not even in spatial reality yet. The "side" is like the continuous edge of the loop, there is no limit in
    what it's in contact with, so the "sides" can overlap where one side can touch many other sides, it's not a 1:1 thing." So
    "side by side" is not used as a term; the stitched and substitution readings stay the two candidates, and his picture of
    bindings as recursion, inner parts placed by the larger ones, is nearer to substitution without choosing it
    ([Intuitions, lesson 7.3](../INTUITIONS.md#73-every-meeting-pattern-none-chosen)).
  - 2026-11-12 · Added with `BASES-WITHIN-20261112-001`'s pre-registration.
  - 2026-11-12 · Under challenge: `SUBST-NEST-20261112-001` tests the substitution reading (blocks and motifs) on the same questions.
  - 2026-11-12 · Challenge answered, status back to Assumed: the readings differ in kind. Under substitution (each distinction a block
    whose own knots bind the copies) every distinction the block cannot spare is tied across the whole pattern, in 47 of 87 small
    blocks; under this reading nothing is. Which one the owner meant is his to say ([nesting by substitution](../research/SUBST-NEST-20261112-001/subst_nest.md)).

### A76 · Global influence is read as ties or travelling along a shift

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** The owner's "globally influential" (2026-11-12: meeting patterns "can fold in immensely complex ways and can be kind
  of globally influential") is read, on periodic bases, as a bounded set whose values decide distinctions arbitrarily many steps
  away along a shift: either every distinction tied to its translate (tied along the shift) or a finite pattern forcing its own
  translate (travelling); and on finite samples, where every distinction is a few steps from every other, as the share of the base a
  co-knotted pair or a knot decides.
- **Why.** These are the measures the law alone supplies (forcing in its allowed states), with nothing drawn; on base 16, the one
  sample studied before, travelling is exactly what gave its unlengthened shift. Taken by default in `COMMON-FOLD-20261112-001`
  ([decision note](../research/COMMON-FOLD-20261112-001/common_fold.md)).
- **Rests on it.** That epic's answer, that global influence is typical under dense overlap in three regimes.
- **Challenge.** Ask the owner whether influence he means is forcing (values decided) or correlation (values made likely), and
  measure the second with an exact point-to-set quantity or model counts; or count influence by what a set decides within a bounded
  number of steps rather than at unbounded steps.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Added by `COMMON-FOLD-20261112-001`.

### A77 · A walk around one cycle is a state that repeats along a short step

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** For copies of a harmony arranged along two shifts, a state is read as "a walk around one cycle" (the coordinator's
  question, from the owner's "globally influential") when moving every copy by some step of at most two copies along each copy shift
  gives the same state up to one shift or flip of the harmony; a state that repeats along no short step is read as two-way, and one
  that repeats along no step at all as genuinely two-way.
- **Why.** A state that repeats along a step is a state of the one-shift arrangement obtained by folding along that step, with the
  symmetry carried as a twist: a closed walk of a finite transfer. The bound of two keeps the folded knot within a window of three
  copies, as for rings. Taken by default in `SHIFT-LOCK-20261112-001`
  ([decision note](../research/SHIFT-LOCK-20261112-001/shift_lock.md)), with the advisor model's agreement before the freeze.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's answer: the 78 cells that open only through two-way states, and "locks at every size still come from
  cycles".
- **Challenge.** Allow longer steps (on a finite repeat whose sizes are coprime every state is a function on one cycle of nm copies,
  so without a bound every state would count as one cycle, and the bound is what makes the reading bite), or every permutation of the harmony's states keeping the stitch
  instead of shifts and flips (done after the run for 50 of the 78 cells, with no change), or read "one cycle" as a finite transfer of
  rows, under which strips count as cycles and two-way states never do.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Added by `SHIFT-LOCK-20261112-001`.
  - 2026-11-12 · Its referee (R1) moved the cut: at steps of 3 or 4, 42 of the 78 cells open through states that repeat, 21 have a side
    of 3, and 17 stay two-way at any cut; the claim is now stated relative to this reading. Status unchanged.

### A78 · A parity knot among copies is flip-blind parity, built from our knots

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · reading

- **Statement.** A parity knot placed among copies of a harmony is read as flip-blind parity: the three copies' placewise sum is all 0s
  or all 1s, through one hidden reference distinction per knot. So read, it is built from not-all-equal knots with hidden
  distinctions (it is complement-closed, which is exactly what the not-all-equal law can define), and is the program's own, not a knot
  brought in from outside.
- **Why.** Plain three-member parity (sum all 0s) is not complement-closed, so the not-all-equal law cannot build it: it needs a zero,
  as the count line found (`HOLD-COUNT-20261111-001`). Flip-blind parity differs from it only by a flip of every copy where the sum is all 1s; a
  reference shared by every knot would be a hub, one per knot is at the scale of a contact. Taken by default in
  `PARITY-MIX-20261112-001` ([pre-registration](../research/PARITY-MIX-20261112-001/preregistration.md)).
- **Rests on it.** That epic's reading of its outcomes as effects of a knot our law can make.
- **Challenge.** Use plain parity (brought from outside), or a single shared reference (a hub), and see whether the outcomes change; or
  build the gadget explicitly inside the harmony's format (its period and knot shape) and check it gives the same states.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Added by `PARITY-MIX-20261112-001`.
  - 2026-11-12 · PARITY-MIX's referee (R1) confirmed the buildability with an explicit gadget (57 knots, 53 hidden distinctions per
    place), built from bare not-all-equal knots on single distinctions; the advisor's NAE4 route builds 4-member not-all-equal, not
    parity. Scope noted: it is not buildable from one-order stitches between whole harmony copies, since every gadget of stitched copies
    allows all copies equal. Status unchanged.

### A79 · A pattern's weight under a description language sums its descriptions

**Assumed** · 2026-11-12 · measure

- **Statement.** Under a description-length weighting, a meeting pattern's weight is the sum of 2^−length over every description
  in the language that expands to it, so that sampling descriptions by fair coins samples patterns by that weight. The language,
  its code and its caps are one weighting of many (A71).
- **Why.** Sampling by fair coins is exact and needs no search; the other reading, weight 2^−(shortest length), needs the shortest
  description of each pattern, which a sample does not give. For a universal language the two agree up to a constant factor (the
  coding theorem); for this restricted language that is not shown
  ([pre-registration](../research/SHORT-DESC-20261112-001/preregistration.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The outcome shares and readings of `SHORT-DESC-20261112-001` under its description weighting.
- **Challenge.** Weight by the shortest description found by search for a sample of patterns, and compare the outcome shares.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-12 · Added by `SHORT-DESC-20261112-001`.

### A80 · The law's reach is read along the first-call ray

**Overturned** · 2026-11-13 · reading

- **Statement.** The least bond from the top of a nested pattern to depth s is read along one ray of copies: main's first call to
  the ray definition, then that definition's first SELF call at every level (after inlining the definitions that do not hold
  themselves), with every other self-holding call entering only by its limit relation. Growth, boundedness and absence are
  classified along that ray.
- **Why.** One ray makes the least bond a fixed-length shortest path in a finite graph, so its long-run class is decided per family;
  the first call was `BOND-DEPTH-20261112-001`'s convention, kept by the studies after it.
- **Rests on it.** The ray-bond classes of `BOND-DEPTH-20261112-001`, `PROVED-RATE-20261113-001` and
  `REACH-CENSUS-20261113-001`, and the target copy of `TRUE-BOND-20261113-001`.
- **Challenge.** Read another ray, or the least bond to any copy at depth s (the least over every path of copies), and compare the
  classes.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-13 · Overturned for growth by `EVERY-RAY-20261113-001`: over every descending path (the call chosen at each level, the
    entry too), in 69% to 78% of sampled first-ray growers with inequivalent SELF calls, for each large depth some path has a short
    bond (per depth; one path for every depth in 188 of 204), almost all without a carried port; growth classified along the first ray is not growth with depth. Bounded verdicts
    along the first ray still hold (L_path ≤ L_ray) ([every descending path](../research/EVERY-RAY-20261113-001/every_ray.md)).
  - 2026-11-13 · Challenge restated after `REACH-CENSUS-20261113-001`'s referee: of the 131 last-call-bounded growers, 99 carry a top
    port down that ray by identity, so their bounded bond is local (A81); the challenge rests on the 32 bounded without a carried
    port (14% of the 224), and on 31 of 645 sampled first-ray growers. `EVERY-RAY-20261113-001` reads every descending path, split
    into carried and uncarried.
  - 2026-11-13 · Added, and put under challenge, by `REACH-CENSUS-20261113-001`: on 3,000 families with a second ray the last-call
    ray gives the same class in 75.4%, and 131 of the 224 one-definition limit-only families proved to grow along the first ray are
    bounded along the last-call ray, at 3 to 5 members ([the reach census](../research/REACH-CENSUS-20261113-001/reach_census.md)).

### A81 · The reach may start from a top port carried down the ray

**Under challenge** · 2026-11-13 · reading

- **Statement.** The least bond from the top of a nested pattern to depth s may take as its top member any top distinction,
  including a port that the ray call passes down unchanged at every level (a carried port), which is then an end of every ray copy.
- **Why.** `BOND-DEPTH-20261112-001` and `BRANCH-DEPTH-20261112-001` defined the bond through any top distinction (a port of C_0),
  and the studies after them kept the definition; none counted how often that distinction is carried.
- **Rests on it.** The bounded classes of `BRANCH-DEPTH-20261112-001` (320 of its 432 bounded rules carry a top port), the true-bond
  values of `TRUE-BOND-20261113-001` and the bounded families of `TRUE-RATE-20261113-001`, and most bounded classes along the
  last-call ray of `REACH-CENSUS-20261113-001` (99 of 131 carry one).
- **Challenge.** Start the bond from a distinction that the depth-1 copy does not hold (a local of C_0, or a port that is not
  carried), and compare growth and boundedness. Overturned for the reach if the carried bounded rules then grow.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-14 · Used by `CARRY-BOUND-20261114-001`: 86% of the bounded weight of the non-forgetting first-ray growers is a bond
    through a carried top port read by a knot; the 93 order-label families are bounded without one, and a carried port that no knot
    reads does not bound the bond ([decision note](../research/CARRY-BOUND-20261114-001/carry_bound.md)). Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-13 · Added, and put under challenge, by `TRUE-RATE-20261113-001`: the true bond is proved bounded by 3 in 10 of 226
    families, all carrying a top port; across BRANCH-DEPTH's 664 rules 320 of the 432 bounded ones carry one and 112 do not
    ([decision note](../research/TRUE-RATE-20261113-001/true_rate.md)).

### A82 · Knots placed by address maps read the owner's nested parts that know where they are

**Assumed** · 2026-11-13 · reading

- **Statement.** The owner's recursion picture ("the nested structures depend on the larger structures to know where they are ... for
  logic, it can hop around") is read, for a census, as not-all-equal knots placed by a small invertible automaton on binary
  addresses: level n is the addresses of length n, a placement (g₁, g₂, g₃) of words of length at most 2 in the automaton's maps puts
  the knot {g₁x, g₂x, g₃x} on every address x where the three differ, and each map acts on a tail class's free letters (the addresses
  sharing their last letters) the same way whatever the tail, while which of the class's knots stay inside it and which join it to
  the rest depend on the tail. Descriptions are weighted by fair coins
  over automata and placements (A79's kind, A71).
- **Why.** A finite description must treat unboundedly many shared distinctions by a fixed rule: uniformly (shared lists, which stay
  finite-state, Lemma A), by a shift of position (flat rows, known), or by a finite automaton on addresses, which contains the shift
  as its simplest case and lets the number of joining knots grow with the level; it is the literature's self-similar groups
  (Bartholdi 2022), where decidability follows that growth ([pre-registration](../research/ADDR-KNOTS-20261113-001/preregistration.md)).
- **Rests on it.** The fate shares and the matched-density comparison of `ADDR-KNOTS-20261113-001`.
- **Challenge.** Place knots across levels too (an address and its one-letter extensions, so that depth is nesting depth and not only
  a level), use longer words or more letters, or weight by shortest description, and compare the fates; or find a recursion reading
  outside the three ways that is not finite-state.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-13 · Statement corrected after `ADDR-KNOTS-20261113-001`'s referee (Block for the sentence): a tail class's inner knots
    can depend on the tail (counterexample in the [referee report](../research/ADDR-KNOTS-20261113-001/referee-R1/report.md)); the
    old wording said they were the same everywhere. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-13 · Added by `ADDR-KNOTS-20261113-001` at its pre-registration.

### A83 · The top binds every depth when a bond of fixed size reaches every level of every finite pattern

**Assumed** · 2026-11-16 · reading

- **Statement.** In addressed patterns (A82), the top level 3 binds every depth directly when there is a bound c such that, for every
  s >= 1 and every depth N >= 3 + s, the depth-N pattern has a bond of at most c distinctions meeting level 3 and level 3 + s.
  Bounded is read at every finite depth, from level 3, with members anywhere; not in the pattern of every level at once, and not from
  another top level.
- **Why.** Taken by default in `DIRECT-REACH-20261115-001` ([decision note](../research/DIRECT-REACH-20261115-001/direct_reach.md)),
  continuing the census's reading of `LEVEL-REACH-20261114-001`. Finite patterns are what the census computes; constrained sets and,
  while the pattern lives, ties persist from one depth to the next, so a finite check can cover every depth; and the pattern of every
  level at once would need the source-aware reading for infinite systems, which has not been set up for addresses.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's verdicts that direct reach is bounded at every depth for 54 of the census's 100 constant descriptions
  (15 with a bond of two, 39 of at most three), and its statement of what the direct ones share.
- **Challenge.** Read the top's reach in the pattern of every level at once, saying which deep distinctions are native there (each
  flipped by changing finitely many others); or read it from top level 2, where 3 of 34 and 6 of 66 of the constant descriptions
  already read differently over s = 1 to 4; or count only bonds within a bounded number of levels below the target.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-16 · Added by `DIRECT-REACH-20261115-001`.

### A84 · Pang's degree-size relation holds as stated

**Assumed** · 2026-11-16 · premise

- **Statement.** Theorems 1.1 and 7.2(a) of Shuo Pang's preprint "A Degree–Size Relation for Resolution over Polynomials" (arXiv
  2610.00837) hold as stated: linear polynomial-calculus degree forces exp(Ω(n)) size in syntactic resolution over polynomials of
  constant degree, and random strict 3-CNF at density 8 needs exp(Ω(n)) proof nodes with probability tending to one, at
  characteristic two and degree one, two or 2^(d+1).
- **Why.** Admitted by the guarded-route studies of 2026-11-15, which checked its conventions against their own and did not
  re-prove it. The preprint is unrefereed and AI-assisted (its disclosure credits a language model with the core arguments), builds
  on Braun's preprint (A55), and claims a result that was open until September 2026; no published check or refutation was found
  (referee GUARDED-ROUTE-BRANCH-R1, 2026-11-16). For the degree-one (parity) case only, ECCC TR26-316 (Alekseev, Gryaznov, Itsykson,
  Pitassi, Robere and Tzameret, 10 November 2026, Theorems 6.5 and 2.3), also unrefereed and independent of Pang, states the same
  bound even for fully semantic Res(⊕).
- **Rests on it.** The inherited exponential lower bounds of `ROUTE-PARITY-TRANSFER-20261115-001D` (or on ECCC TR26-316 instead),
  `QUADRATIC-ROUTE-TRANSFER-20261115-001B` and `-001C`, `PRODUCT-NAME-TRANSFER-20261115-001B` and `-001C`, and
  `AFFINE-FACTOR-RANK-20261115-001C`'s lower-bound corollary. The transfers themselves are proved without it.
  [Route parity decision](../research/ROUTE-PARITY-TRANSFER-20261115-001/decision.md)
- **Challenge.** Have a resolution-over-parities expert read Pang §§3–5; a published check or refutation, or a gap in Theorem 1.1,
  would confirm or overturn it. A gap would leave the parity case standing on ECCC TR26-316 alone and remove the degree-two and
  named-product bounds.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-16 · Added after referee GUARDED-ROUTE-BRANCH-R1 found the four studies resting on it with no register entry.

### A85 · What must be worked in order is read by the phases of the forcing law

**Assumed** · 2026-11-16 · measure

- **Statement.** How much of an atom pattern with held distinctions must be worked in order is read by the number of phases of the
  forcing law: in each phase every atom with a forced demander is read as a two-place rule and everything reachable along those
  rules is forced at once, so an atom waits only when its two demanders are first forced in the same phase. On patterns without
  cycles the lesser of this count and the dual law's (which spreads unforcedness the same way) is read instead. Parallel time is
  then at most a polylogarithmic factor times this count.
- **Why.** Taken by default in `SEQUENTIAL-WALL-20261116-001`
  ([decision note](../research/SEQUENTIAL-WALL-20261116-001/sequential_wall.md)) as the knot-native law closest to the owner's
  untangling: settling one distinction of a knot leaves a two-place rule. Its count is the Horton-Strahler number of the best
  derivation (Esparza, Kiefer and Luttenberger, 2010), which makes it computable and comparable with propagation by rounds. Other
  parallel readings were not compared: depth of resolution proofs, laws that carry the joint record of an open atom, and arbitrary
  NC algorithms.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's readings that random atom patterns at the jump need few phases (12 at n = 2^20) though propagation
  needs many rounds (312), that unlayered random circuits need at most 4 phases, and that layered random circuits need about
  d^0.41.
- **Challenge.** A family with many phases that another native law decides quickly already exists without cycles (the ladder and
  the pyramid, ended at once by the dual law); on patterns with cycles, find one, or let the law carry the joint record of open
  atoms up to w distinctions and see whether the layered circuits' phases fall with w. Overturned if a law no stronger than
  two-place reasoning plus bounded records needs polylogarithmically many phases on the layered circuits.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-16 · Added by `SEQUENTIAL-WALL-20261116-001`.

### A86 · A typical atom pattern is uniform atoms at three per distinction with a uniform held order

**Assumed** · 2026-11-16 · scope

- **Statement.** Claims about typical atom patterns in `SEQUENTIAL-WALL-20261116-001` are made for one declared random process: n
  distinctions, 3n atoms each holding three distinct distinctions chosen uniformly (two demanders and the demanded one), and the
  held set the first k distinctions of a uniform order, read at the jump of the forced set (the least k forcing half) and at fixed
  held fractions.
- **Why.** Taken by default in that epic ([decision note](../research/SEQUENTIAL-WALL-20261116-001/sequential_wall.md)): at three
  atoms per distinction the mean field x = a + (1 - a)(1 - e^(-3x^2)) has a clear discontinuous jump (forced share 0.21 to 0.93 at
  a = 0.098), which is the regime where propagation is slow; densities below two have no jump, and other weightings were not tried.
- **Rests on it.** The epic's measured exponents at the jump (rounds like n^0.30, phases 0.57 per doubling) and the phase profile
  read against Newton's rates at a tangency.
- **Challenge.** Repeat at three per distinction replaced by 2.5, 4 and 6 atoms, with demanded distinctions of unequal weight, or
  with atoms placed by a growth law of the program (newest-w growth); the exponents are overturned as typical if they move outside
  the epic's ranges (rounds slope 0.29 to 0.37, phases 0.2 to 0.8 per doubling) under these changes.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-16 · Added by `SEQUENTIAL-WALL-20261116-001`.

### A87 · In forcing, the record a law carries is measured by how many distinctions it carries

**Assumed** · 2026-11-16 · measure

- **Statement.** When a law for forcing in an atom pattern carries open distinctions together instead of waiting a phase for each
  open use, the size of what it carries is measured by w, the number of distinctions carried at once; a phase then searches about
  n^w carried sets.
- **Why.** Taken by default in `CARRY-STRANDS-20261116-001`
  ([decision note](../research/CARRY-STRANDS-20261116-001/carry_strands.md)): the forced set is one state, so a joint record of
  carried distinctions holds only which ones are carried, and table size or states visited are functions of w (advisor). It is the
  pebble count of the classical games, which makes the results comparable with register allocation and pebbling.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's readings: spend-only phases ⌈σ/w⌉, rounds never below σ, keeping paying only where strands rejoin,
  and little rejoining in random atom patterns and random circuits.
- **Challenge.** In counting or satisfiability, where the allowed states are many, a carried record is a relation and its size can
  grow without bound in w; measure it by the number of allowed joint states carried, and see whether the conclusions change.
  Overturned for the owner's braid question if such a measure gives a saving that w does not see.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-16 · Added by `CARRY-STRANDS-20261116-001`.

### A88 · The owner's navigating agent is focused local search over violated knots

**Assumed** · 2026-11-16 · reading

- **Statement.** The owner's agent of 2026-11-16 (it reads the context at one knot, turns from knot to knot, acts on loops,
  remembers its steps, and activates the knot it chooses) is read as focused local search on a knot pattern of the not-all-equal
  law. Activating a knot means attending to one violated knot. Acting on a loop means flipping one of that knot's distinctions,
  a change of state; the law and the meeting pattern stay fixed. Turning to the next knot means choosing among the knots the last
  flip violated and a few drawn from the other violated knots. Memory means each knot's and each distinction's recency and recent
  count, which is what an attention over the agent's own past steps retrieves when it matches identities, plus a small recurrent
  state.
- **Why.** Adopted by `WHERE-NEXT-20261116-001` as the smallest reading that can be compared with tuned hand-written rules at
  matched cost. The alternatives were an agent that edits the law (as the learning line's learner does), one that acts on
  several knots at once, and a sequence model over raw step tokens.
- **Rests on it.** The verdicts of [WHERE-NEXT-20261116-001](../research/WHERE-NEXT-20261116-001/where_next.md): a win at the
  training sizes from memory, and no transfer to larger sizes or to another meeting pattern.
- **Challenge.** Train a sequence model that reads the raw history of visited knots and flipped distinctions, with no hand-chosen
  memory features, on the same instances and test cells. If it carries over to n = 12800 where the hand-chosen memory did not,
  the reading's memory was too narrow.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-16 · Adopted by `WHERE-NEXT-20261116-001`.

### A89 · Learned search is judged by work per unseen instance against re-tuned rules

**Assumed** · 2026-11-16 · measure

- **Statement.** A learned search policy is better only if its median work per unseen instance is lower than the best
  hand-written rule's. Work is counted as flips, incidences read and CPU in one shared engine. The rule is re-tuned on separate
  instances of the same size and family. The policy's training cost is then set against any per-instance saving to give the
  break-even count of instances.
- **Why.** Adopted by `WHERE-NEXT-20261116-001` after the advisor review of 2026-11-16. The alternatives were a total amortized
  over a test set the study chose itself, flips alone, and a rule tuned only at the training size.
- **Rests on it.** The real-win, mechanism-win and clean-loss verdicts of
  [WHERE-NEXT-20261116-001](../research/WHERE-NEXT-20261116-001/where_next.md).
- **Challenge.** Report quality at a fixed budget (fraction solved within k flips) and work against rules tuned only at the
  training size. A verdict that changes under either is sensitive to this measure. At `rand` n = 3200, against rules tuned at
  n = 800, R was 0.96 [0.82, 1.09], inconclusive rather than a clean loss.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-16 · Adopted by `WHERE-NEXT-20261116-001`.

### A90 · The owner's slow loop changes the pattern where the search dwells, keeping its allowed states

**Assumed** · 2026-11-17 · reading

- **Statement.** The owner's second stage (B191: "mutations of the knot structures to improve results", a fast search with a
  slower loop reshaping the structure, "braids and untangles" as ways of creating structure) is read as a slow loop that, between
  epochs of a fast local search, changes the knot pattern being searched where the search attended most. Its moves keep the allowed
  states: merging a tie class (allowed states kept one for one) and adding an implied knot (a signed three-place not-all-equal
  knot every allowed state satisfies). Novelty+ is the fast loop.
- **Why.** The coordinator's reading of 2026-11-17, adopted by `SLOW-LOOP-20261117-001` at its freeze. Moves that keep the
  allowed states make any saving a saving on the same question, and they are what a refutation can certify locally. The
  alternatives were: moves that keep only satisfiability (eliminating a distinction, breaking the complement symmetry); changes to
  the agent's own internal knot structure rather than to the pattern it searches; and the owner's "braid" read as carrying open
  distinctions together (the forcing line's reading).
- **Rests on it.** The verdicts of `SLOW-LOOP-20261117-001` and the reading of B191's first attempt.
- **Challenge.** The cheapest discriminator is a slow loop whose moves change the agent instead of the pattern (for example
  reweighting or adding knots to the agent's own reads), or moves that keep only satisfiability, compared at the same charged
  work. A saving under another reading that this one misses would overturn it as the reading of the owner's picture.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-17 · Part of the alternative tested by `SPEC-MERGE-20261117-001`: changes that do not keep the allowed states
    (speculative merges on the fast loop's co-flip evidence, undone on stalls). They did not pay anywhere, so this reading
    stands as the one under which structure changes have paid.
  - 2026-11-17 · Adopted by `SLOW-LOOP-20261117-001` (the coordinator's reading, challengeable).

### A91 · A slow loop is judged by charged work, both loops and every rebuild counted

**Assumed** · 2026-11-17 · measure

- **Statement.** A slow loop that changes the pattern is better only if the median over unseen instances of its charged work is
  lower than the better of the re-tuned fast rules alone. Charged work counts the incidences (knot-distinction pairs) read by the
  fast loop, by every probe (three per knot it reads) and by every rebuild. Each instance's figure is the geometric mean of five
  runs. CPU must also be lower for a charged win, and it is measured with the rebuild partly in Python.
- **Why.** Adopted by `SLOW-LOOP-20261117-001` at its freeze, extending A89 to two loops. Flips alone would not charge the slow
  loop at all, and CPU alone depends on how much of the rebuild is written in C. The alternatives were flips, CPU alone, and
  conflicts or propagations, as solvers count them.
- **Rests on it.** The charged-win, work-win and loss verdicts of `SLOW-LOOP-20261117-001`.
- **Challenge.** Weigh probes and fast-loop reads differently (a probe reads three incidences per knot, while a fast-loop break
  count reads one counter per incidence), or count CPU with the rebuild in C. A verdict that changes under a factor-of-two change
  in the probe charge is sensitive to this measure.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-17 · SLOW-LOOP's referee found the count incomplete: the rebuild's Python-side sorting and set operations
    (deduplication, probed-key sets, the roots' unique, the guided order's sort charged as linear) were not counted, 1.2 times
    the counted rebuild work for the eager loop. Its frozen verdicts stand because they also required whole-run CPU; the
    work-only ratios were corrected (`docs/CORRECTIONS.md`). `EARN-BUDGET-20261117-001` charges the sorting, at k log k per
    sort of k items, before its test.
  - 2026-11-17 · Adopted by `SLOW-LOOP-20261117-001`.

### A92 · A slow loop earns its eagerness by doubling and halving on the share its batched merges remove

**Assumed** · 2026-11-17 · reading

- **Statement.** A structure-changing slow loop sets its own budget as follows:
  - the moves it finds are kept until they number a fixed share of the classes, and the pattern is rebuilt then;
  - its next budget doubles (up to a ceiling) when that rebuild removed at least theta of the classes per unit of slow work
    relative to fast work since the last rebuild, and halves (down to a floor) otherwise.

  This is the rule by which the program reads "a slow loop that monitors its own changes".
- **Why.** Adopted by `EARN-BUDGET-20261117-001` at its freeze. It is the productivity-driven scheduling of inprocessing that CDCL
  solvers use, moved to a local-search fast loop. The alternatives were a fixed budget (SLOW-LOOP's, which stays lazy when tuned
  where nothing pays), a budget set from the probes' find rate (which SLOW-LOOP's runs showed does not separate the patterns), and
  pricing a batch before its rebuild.
- **Rests on it.** The reading-2 verdict of `EARN-BUDGET-20261117-001` and its diagnosis that the ramp, not the moves, limits the
  gain.
- **Challenge.** Price the pending moves before rebuilding (run the tie cascade alone) and jump to the ceiling on the first
  productive batch. If that closes most of the 3 to 4 times regret against the per-cell fixed budget, the doubling rule was the
  obstruction, not self-monitoring as such (`EARN-BUDGET-20261117-001`#1).
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-17 · Adopted by `EARN-BUDGET-20261117-001`.

### A93 · A speculative change is judged by whether the search then gets below its pre-change best

**Overturned** · 2026-11-17 · premise

- **Statement.** A change to the knot pattern made without proof, such as merging two distinctions at their current relative
  value, is good if the fast search, after it, gets below the least violated count it was stuck at before it within a few epochs.
  Otherwise the change is undone.
- **Why.** Adopted by `SPEC-MERGE-20261117-001` at its freeze as the undo rule for speculative merges: it costs nothing to read,
  and it is the natural "keep what helps" test of a mutation. The alternatives were a per-merge check (a bounded probe or a short
  local search on the merged knots) and no undo.
- **Rests on it.** The speculative arms of `SPEC-MERGE-20261117-001`.
- **Challenge.** It was challenged in the same study by logging every round against the planted state. On `local` w10 the rule
  undid 85% of the rounds whose merges were all right (AE). Two checks act before a round is kept:
  - **The contradiction check** undoes a round whose merges contradict each other or the knots. It undid 61% of AE's wrong
    rounds on `local` w10 and 78% on `rand` 2.1 at once.
  - **The stall test** (this assumption) kept the wrong rounds it judged at least as often as the right ones: AE 24.0% against
    17.7%, A0 20.3% against 11.9%, Aloc 26.1% against 19.7%, A 14.0% against 13.5% (the referee's rerun with the contradiction
    flag recorded).

  On `rand` 2.1, 10% of AE's runs stalled. The search's progress over a few epochs is no test of a merge. The lesson: a mutation
  needs a check of its own, not the search's short-term progress. The loop's one check of its own, the contradiction check, did
  the useful filtering.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-17 · The overturn made firmer by the referee of `SPEC-MERGE-20261117-001`. "Kept 9% of the wrong rounds" had counted
    the contradiction check's undos as the stall test's. The stall test alone was no better than blind.
  - 2026-11-17 · Overturned by `SPEC-MERGE-20261117-001`'s round log, in the study that adopted it.
  - 2026-11-17 · Adopted by `SPEC-MERGE-20261117-001`.

### A94 · An agent's memory across a change of the pattern is carried by SIGNED-NAV's rule

**Assumed** · 2026-11-17 · reading

- **Statement.** When the slow loop rebuilds the knot pattern, the learned agent keeps its memory through SIGNED-NAV's carry rule:
  - **values:** a class takes the slow loop's majority vote;
  - **ages:** a class takes its members' most recent flip;
  - **recent counts:** decayed and summed over the members;
  - **knots:** a new knot takes the most recent visit of its old knots, and their visit counts decayed and summed;
  - **the rest:** the recurrent state, the step count and the random stream carry over unchanged.

  Restarting instead keeps only the mapped values, with fresh memory.
- **Why.** Adopted by `AGENT-LOOP-20261117-001` at its freeze. It is the carry rule SIGNED-NAV built and replayed on real
  tie-merge sequences, and it is the natural mapping of a memory through a substitution. The alternatives were: carrying only
  the ages; resetting the recurrent state; and training the agent with the changes in its episodes, so that it learns its own use
  of carried memory.
- **Rests on it.** The memory verdict of `AGENT-LOOP-20261117-001` ("no difference shown").
- **Challenge.** First show that the agent's memory matters over the stretch between changes. Wiping it every K flips at the
  rebuild spacing must cost at least about 1.2, or the changes must come closer together. Then train the agent with the slow
  loop's rebuilds in its learning episodes, and test carry against restart again (`AGENT-LOOP-20261117-001#1`). A learner that
  uses carried memory where this one does not would show the verdict belongs to this rule and this agent, not to memory across a
  change.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-17 · Untested, after the referee of `AGENT-LOOP-20261117-001`. The study's memory test had almost no power:
    - under the loop, wiping the agent's memory every 100 flips changes nothing on `local` w30;
    - on block it matters only within about 2,000 flips, while the rebuilds were 3,500 to 6,400 apart;
    - a deliberately scrambled carry also reads "no difference shown" on the first block cell.

    The earlier "so the rule does no harm" is withdrawn. The rule is implemented as stated: carried through an unchanged pattern,
    it reproduces the agent's runs exactly.
  - 2026-11-17 · Adopted by `AGENT-LOOP-20261117-001`. Carried and restarted memory gave the same flips and work within 0.89 to
    1.02 on every cell with room. The history entry first said this showed the rule does no harm; it was not shown to help.

### A95 · Two fast loops are compared at the same proofs per flip in flips, and at the same share of work on proofs in work

**Assumed** · 2026-11-17 · measure

- **Statement.** When two fast loops of different cost per flip run under one slow loop, each measure is read under its own
  budget:
  - **flips** under a slow budget of a fixed 50 incidences per flip (times 3), the same proofs per flip for both;
  - **charged work** under EARN-BUDGET's rule, a slow budget of 3 times the epoch's own fast work, the same share of work on
    proofs for both.
- **Why.** Adopted by `AGENT-LOOP-20261117-001` at its freeze, after calibration showed that EARN-BUDGET's rule buys the learned
  agent (about 240 incidences per flip) about 5 times Novelty+'s proofs per flip, so that in flips the agent "won" by half through
  proofs alone. The advisor's review asked that each measure be read under the budget that makes it fair. The alternatives were
  one budget for both measures, or a budget fixed in proofs per epoch.
- **Rests on it.** M2 to M4 of `AGENT-LOOP-20261117-001`.
- **Challenge.** Rerun the comparison with a budget fixed in proofs per epoch, or with the unit set from each fast loop's own
  measured cost. A verdict that flips would show this choice was doing the work. The interaction test in work also passes on
  `rand`, where nothing is proved, because the probing cost is bounded and diluted by a costlier fast loop, so any reading of
  interactions in work should be checked against such a control.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-17 · Adopted by `AGENT-LOOP-20261117-001`.

### A96 · A role-preserving shuffle of a weave removes its steps apart and nothing else that matters

**Overturned** · 2026-11-17 · scope

- **Statement.** In `SPACE-ORDER-20261117-001`, the twin of a six-regular weave is the same knots after 5m degree- and
  role-preserving swaps (members exchanged only between slots of the same role), and differences between a weave and its twin
  are read as effects of steps apart (the weave's space).
- **Why.** Taken by default in that epic ([decision note](../research/SPACE-ORDER-20261117-001/space_order.md)), on the advisor's
  advice: plain swaps change how many knots demand each distinction, which confounds the comparison, while role-preserving swaps
  keep every distinction's counts in each role and destroy short cycles and steps apart.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's reading that space forces order: 256 phases on the weave against 9.5 on the twin at L = 384 for the
  two-demand law.
- **Challenge.** The twin also loses short cycles (the weave's triangles of co-knotted pairs), which matter for local forcing apart
  from distance. Compare with a twin that keeps short cycles but adds long-range shortcuts (a weave with a few random rewirings),
  and see whether the phase count follows steps apart (falls to about the new largest steps apart) or the cycles. Run by the referee
  `SPACE-ORDER-R1`: rewiring 0.2% of the knots keeps 99% of the pairs in two knots and cuts the largest steps apart from 256 to 52
  at L = 384, and the phases stay at 252 (from 260); they fall only as the short cycles go. The phase count follows the cycles.
  Lesson: a shuffle that preserves degrees and roles still removes local overlap structure, and a contrast with it cannot be read as
  an effect of steps apart alone.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-17 · Overturned by its own challenge, run by the referee `SPACE-ORDER-R1`; the reading that rested on it is withdrawn
    (`docs/CORRECTIONS.md`).
  - 2026-11-17 · Added by `SPACE-ORDER-20261117-001`.

### A97 · Distance is the only form of information

**Under challenge** · 2026-11-20 · interpretation

- **Statement.** The owner's reading, in his words (17 November 2026): "Distance is the only form of information. That's the
  entire basis of the 'this versus that' distinction in the smallest emergent unit." Read in the program's terms: what a base
  holds as information is carried by how far its distinctions are from one another, as its own law measures it (bond distance,
  which under the not-all-equal law is steps apart plus one on the endless six-regular weave, and on finite six-regular weaves
  while the steps apart stay below a third of their margin), and a distinction itself, this versus that, is the least such
  separation. Stated as the program's founding premise, with what hangs from it, in THEORY.md (section 1) and the Intuitions
  guide's lesson 2.4 ([lesson](../INTUITIONS.md#24-distance-is-the-only-form-of-information)). Asked the same day whether his
  distance includes which way or only how far, he answered: "Distance is topological, so it will have some starting point, then go
  off outward from there. So for example, 0 might be a starting point on the number line. In my view, there isn't really any such
  thing as "direction". Everything is just "relative", but it's also topological, so if you're at 3 on the number line, you can't
  just skip 4 to get to 5, so there is an order." The number line is his illustration, not a space the program assumes. Read in
  the program's terms, his distance has no direction, and nothing farther is reached without passing what lies between, so distance
  alone gives an order (the literature's betweenness, for a distance built from unit steps); that reading is the program's and is
  untested. Asked the same day (backlog B194) whether that order already exists before any knot, as the disagreement count orders a
  chain of plain implications, or only with one knot per step, as bond distance does, he said the question as put confused him and
  answered: "The knots are themselves the smallest form of spatial topology. "3" as a concept is a topological structure that
  exists but with relation to all other numbers, not just neighbors. Topology is everything, literally everything. We're building
  set theory using topology. We're building physics. We're building the genetic code. Consciousness." Logical topology "is the
  base thing that can build emergence because it creates topological rules, just like in mathmatics, just like in conways game of
  life", and "Logical topology is the very first instance of "this versus that". From that, we can create space, set theory,
  everything else." His words, not a result. The program's reading of them, not his: the knot comes first, so nothing counts as
  his distance before the first knot. The order the disagreement count reads on a chain of plain implications is then not yet his
  distance but something knots would have to build, and the foundation's "minimal contact-distance appears with the first knot"
  stands under the premise. "With relation to all other numbers, not just neighbors" is read as distance being a relation over
  every pair, not only between neighbours; he did not name a particular distance.
- **Why.** Recorded at the owner's request on 17 November 2026, in reply to the first reading of `SPACE-ORDER-20261117-001`
  ([decision note](../research/SPACE-ORDER-20261117-001/space_order.md)), "space forces order, and only through steps apart". That
  reading was later withdrawn by its referee: the phases there follow the weave's local overlap structure, and steps apart is only
  their lower bound. The owner's reading does not rest on it and stands as his interpretation. It continues his stance of 22 October
  2026 that there are no values, only relations seeded from the smallest distinction, this versus that. It is an interpretation, not
  a theorem: no result shows that every kind of information a base holds is a distance.
- **Rests on it.** No result. It frames the ruler results that make bond distance the law's own distance (`RULER-20261111-001`,
  `LOGIC-AXIS-20261107-001`).
- **Challenge.** Find two bases whose bond distances agree for every pair of distinctions but whose allowed states differ (for
  example in count, or in what is forced from the same held values): their difference would be information that is not distance.
  The cheapest first test is a census of small not-all-equal patterns grouped by their bond-distance tables, checking whether equal
  tables force equal relations (`OVERLAP-READ-20261111-001` found that equal pair counts give equal states, a related result).
  Parity knots, which hide one full bit, are a second test: is the hidden bit a distance? Run by `DISTANCE-INFO-20261117-001`
  ([decision note](../research/DISTANCE-INFO-20261117-001/distance_info.md)) on every not-all-equal base of 4, 5 and 6
  distinctions and 258,384 sampled at 7. Which distance is meant decides it. Pairwise bond distance fails at four distinctions (12
  tables for 16 relations; at six, 10,012 for 97,248), and which sets are bonds does not repair it. How often two distinctions
  differ, the number of allowed states in which they take different values, determined every base tested through six and every
  sampled one at seven (its referee `DISTANCE-INFO-R1`, recomputed by the study; computed, not proved). The test covers bases of up
  to seven distinctions, not the smallest emergent unit. Followed by `DISTANCE-ORDER-20261117-001`
  ([decision note](../research/DISTANCE-ORDER-20261117-001/distance_order.md)), after the owner's answer, with the reading of "no
  skipping" in A98. Proved: the disagreement count determines the states of every base defined by three-place not-all-equal rules on four or more distinctions, and fails at three
  (one knot against none, told apart only by the rates); in it, "c lies between a and b" is exactly a three-place constraint (a
  knot where its pairs are free), and a chain of m distinctions allows at most 2m joint values. On bases whose knots form no cycle,
  −log₃ |1 − 2d/N| equals steps apart, so one distance read from the states is built from unit steps and carries the base.
  Computed, under A98's exact additive betweenness: on the thread, a chain of knots and the six-regular weave, no distinction lies
  between two others in the disagreement count, while steps apart and bond distance (up to its offset), which have the order, do
  not carry everything. Read only as order (its referee `DISTANCE-ORDER-R1`), the weave's count has a local order with nothing
  skipped whose nearest neighbours are twelve, the six knot-neighbours and the six second neighbours of the start's own class, not
  the weave's own six. So the reading holds on knot trees; on the thread and the weave the split rests on A98, and it is not a
  property of cycles (cyclic bases with betweenness are common at five and six distinctions). Open: whether some reading of the
  weave's states gives both.
  The dimension test `DISTANCE-DIM-20261117-001`
  ([decision note](../research/DISTANCE-DIM-20261117-001/distance_dim.md); refereed) reads it with knots first, as the owner
  answered B194. Under not-all-equal knots no distance read from the states gave an order: no exact betweenness occurs on the
  thread, the weave or a chain grid. Nearness graphs depend on the test, and they grow as each meeting pattern does (two-way on the
  weave and the chain grid, one-way on the thread), so the distance reads the pattern's own growth and adds no dimension. Only off
  the critical point (the chain grid) do the counts give a linear ruler, and the weave is critical. Under plain implications the
  states are the order's Alexandrov topology, and its specialization order and dimension are read back exactly (classical):
  that is what knots would have to build.
  The knot-law test `WEAVE-LAWS-20261117-001`
  ([decision note](../research/WEAVE-LAWS-20261117-001/weave_laws.md); refereed) found that on the weave no tested knot
  law has a pair-count distance that both carries its information and is a ruler. Only the not-all-equal class gives bases
  determined by their pair counts (proved). On the weave, every law putting one such knot on every up knot and one on every down knot is critical or frozen
  (all 16; its referee). The laws that give a ruler there (OR; hard hexagons) keep part of their information in counts of three,
  and step outward only in envelope. Its referee's probes find a sparse mix of signed knots that looks disordered with every
  knot in the not-all-equal class, so the narrowing holds for one-knot-per-orientation laws only. (A 2 × 2 law first reported
  with it was withdrawn by the referee: one size past, its gap keeps falling.)
  `WEAVE-MIX-20261117-001` ([decision note](../research/WEAVE-MIX-20261117-001/weave_mix.md); refereed) tested mixed
  arrangements with periods up to three and did not settle them: its frozen test broke on the twist around the cylinder. Read
  again post hoc, one pair of 3 × 1 arrangements, each with a distinction meeting an odd number of inconsistent bonds, is gapped
  one size past (to width 18; its referee), and none with every count even reads disordered; the other post hoc survivors fall
  apart into independent pieces. That pair and the random 10% mix are the leads; neither's pair-count distance has been measured
  as a ruler.
  The order study `KNOT-ORDER-20261117-001`
  ([decision note](../research/KNOT-ORDER-20261117-001/knot_order.md); refereed) gave, under A98's reading (the disagreement
  count with exact additive betweenness), a case built from knots alone. A line of betweenness knots, grown one knot per step, is
  determined by its pair counts, and its count is an exact ruler with nothing skipped in which every distinction relates to every
  other. An outside rule reading only its states finds where the next knot must go: between two neighbours (a knot with only
  those two) or beyond an end (a knot with both ends). The line is the flip-symmetric implication chain: its order is put in by
  where the knots go and read back, and by `WEAVE-LAWS-20261117-001`'s transfer-gap reading it is frozen (2n states).
  `GRID-GROW-20261117-001` ([decision note](../research/GRID-GROW-20261117-001/grid_grow.md); refereed, one Block) grew the
  grid's two orders under the same reading, on the given two-way arrangement. Betweenness knots grow a base whose disagreement
  count is twice the arrangement's L1 distance, at fixed costs per new cell: one forced knot for a new line, three for a fill. The
  arrangement says where each next cell goes: shapes with the same states can have different placements, and a straight run's
  states admit a fill at every inner distinction, so the states do not say where the second order starts (they give only where a
  new line starts). The two orders are the arrangement's own, read back as its halfspaces, and the base is nearly frozen
  (2(r + c) − 2 states for r·c distinctions).
  `STATE-RULES-20261117-001` ([decision note](../research/STATE-RULES-20261117-001/state_rules.md); referee pending) then let
  uniform rules that read only the states (and, in a second arm, which knots meet) choose every step, under A99's move set. The
  cheapest one-knot steps keep the start's number of orders (proved); the cheapest three-knot steps make two orders at once from
  one knot, chosen by the states alone, and then grow the cube of three orders at eight distinctions and of four at sixteen; uniform
  choice gives many. Two orders appear as a stage and are kept by none of these laws, with or without reading the knots.
  `KNOT-CAP-20261117-001` ([decision note](../research/KNOT-CAP-20261117-001/knot_cap.md); referee pending) added caps on
  contacts: the two tightest (three knots per distinction, two per pair) stop the growth exactly at the 2 × 2 pattern, looser ones
  pass it, and none keeps two orders growing. On the owner's premise it found that how knots meet is information the states lack:
  from matched starts it changes what grows and, for one pair (end hub against middle hub), how many orders; after the knots are
  erased the states keep a fading imprint of it (the fading due to erasure shown for the six-starts; its referee).
  `CUBE-DOUBLING-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/CUBE-DOUBLING-20261118-001/cube_doubling.md); referee pending)
  proved the cheapest law's cube growth for every size under A99: it gains one order at each doubling and never keeps two.
  `WEAVE-PAIR-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/WEAVE-PAIR-20261118-001/weave_pair.md); refereed) settled
  `WEAVE-MIX-20261117-001`'s surviving pair: one two-way arrangement, disordered in both twist sectors to width 21, whose distance
  carries all its information and decays at a fixed rate, but oscillates, so it steps outward only in envelope. Its referee found
  that `WEAVE-MIX-20261117-001`'s third survivor, decoupled into strips of two columns, is a one-way ruler with full information
  that decreases strictly from every start. So on the weave the one-way premise is met and the two-way one is not.
  `LOCAL-ROOM-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/LOCAL-ROOM-20261118-001/local_room.md); refereed) showed that no
  reach limit read from the states holds two orders and fills them, and that one remembered class per split, handed over by the
  start, does: knots only across the two classes grow full two-way grids forever. So two orders need more than a reach limit; a
  rule reading the crossing of the splits is not excluded (narrowed after the referee).
  `NEVER-NEST-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/NEVER-NEST-20261118-001/never_nest.md); refereed) found such rules.
  Told to take no fill that makes a third order, and to lengthen by a subdivision when no other fill is left, the growth reads
  which order each split belongs to from the states and grows full two-way grids forever from a line: proved when knots may join
  any pair, computed (nearly every run to n = 40) when they may join only pairs that already share a knot. So two orders need no
  memory, only the cap. A rule that completes a corner only across two crossing splits does the same once its first crossing
  reaches every split. The two is wholly the cap's: uncapped, the same rule makes every order (A101). These are the first grown
  bases on which a two-way distance can be read.
  `MEET-ORDERS-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/MEET-ORDERS-20261118-001/meet_orders.md); refereed) found
  which information that is, for fewest-knot presentations (one redundant knot changes the chances; A100). The fewest-knot presentations of a line are its bracketings, which knot lies within which half of
  which (proved), and under KF3 the chance of two orders after the second new distinction is an exact formula in the bracketing
  read without halves (proved): 5/12 for chains (shared distinction at an end or next to an end), 11/18 when the knot holding both
  ends holds one knot in each half. That reading decides the whole distribution through the third new distinction, and the
  halves enter at the fourth (computed, every presentation of 5 to 9). Post hoc, the meeting pattern alone, with the line ignored,
  decides how the growth branches. Whether the bracketing is a distance is open: it is read from the states' disagreement counts
  together with the knots, and the states alone do not give it.
  `MEET-GROW-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/MEET-GROW-20261118-001/meet_grow.md); referee pending) found that,
  under KF3, the meeting pattern grows by the same law wherever it sits along the line: from any two presentations with one
  meeting pattern, one renaming carries the whole law of the grown co-knotted pairs, state by state (computed, every presentation
  of 5 to 9 distinctions; refereed), while the number of orders differs with the placement. It does not grow by itself: the
  states still steer which pairs come next (its referee, Narrowing).
  `MEET-TWIST-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/MEET-TWIST-20261118-001/meet_twist.md); referee pending) proved why:
  moving the line's ends one step round the cycle of its knots, keeping every distinction's odd set, carries KF3's options knot
  by knot, so the growth's law does not depend on where the ends sit (every line length, until a state is added, which happens
  within 2^(n0 − 1) distinctions; refereed); the ends decide only the
  number of orders.
  `MEET-DIST-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/MEET-DIST-20261118-001/meet_dist.md); refereed, one Block on a
  reading, corrected) measured how much of the nesting becomes distance. All presentations have the same disagreement counts (the
  setting). After KF3 growth one base's counts, read on unnamed distinctions, carry about 0.4 to 0.7 of the information about the
  bare bracketing; with the distinctions' names, chiefly the order in which the new ones came, they carry all of it at five and
  0.91 at six; in law they carry all of it from the first new distinction. So the share the unnamed distances miss is held in the
  order of growth, which is history, not distance. `MEET-HIST-20261118-001`
  ([decision note](../research/MEET-HIST-20261118-001/meet_hist.md); refereed) located it: what the distances miss is a small part
  of the order of growth (it adds 0.07 to 0.49 of the share, 0.22 to 0.36 bits), and that part is not in the states even with the
  line named (the order is 85 to 90% undetermined by them); knowing which distinctions are the line's adds 0.005 to 0.30.
  `MEET-ADDED-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/MEET-ADDED-20261118-001/meet_added.md); refereed, narrowed): given the
  grown states and told which knots the growth added, a reader recovers the all-named share (exactly at five and six, within
  0.0002 at seven); the states or the added knots alone do not suffice, and being told which knots are added is itself history.
  In the finite pure parity growth family, bond distances through five determine every junction and the full set of next joins at every size. This includes the cubic rounds and all legal serial rearrangements. All 552 presentations through seven junctions were independently checked within the task. [Proof and checks](../research/BOND-NEXT-JOIN-20261117-001/next_joins.md)
  The inverse above assumes exactly three fresh distinctions at every added junction. With two or three allowed, the specified seven-junction example gives twelve three-parent choices with mutual bond distances 6, 8 and 8. At the same width of eighteen, two other histories give identical full states and different single and two-parent choices for the ninth junction. [Finite comparison and parity proof](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001A/first_three.md)
  At nineteen distinctions under the variable fresh-count law, the three candidate rows plus the parity of the total number of pairs at bond distance four give the exact three-parent choices in all 8,191 arrangements. The rows alone can agree on a legal and a forbidden triple. [Width-nineteen comparison](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001B/triples.md)
  With two or three fresh distinctions per junction, the full scalar table preserves three-parent choices in all 19,414 width-twenty arrangements. Three marked rows plus global distance-four parity fail at every width from 27. Parity restricted to the part surrounding the triple gives the exact choices at widths nineteen and twenty. Full-table sufficiency at arbitrary size remains open. [Comparison and proofs](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001C/all_sizes.md)
  With two or three fresh distinctions, the scalar table recovers the exact single-distinction branches at every size. Equal allowed states preserve triple eligibility on the retained part with anonymous boundary distinctions. Three candidate rows plus its distance-four parity fail at width 22. A width-32 part with eight legal triples has a scalar symmetry that loses a six-place parity equation and preserves every legal triple. All-size scalar sufficiency is open. [Separator, state theorem and witnesses](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001D/decision.md)
  Pair and full three-distinction bond readings together with the fixed growing end carry every later complete round from every one of the 30,189 reached census stages examined. At a larger reached meet-once example, a renaming changes an equation while preserving these readings and their later tables. Unrestricted one-round transport was open at that checkpoint. [Marked three-distinction reading](../research/GROWN-END-READING-20261119-001/decision.md)
  For all later bond readings under complete parity growth, the worst-case current reading order grows linearly in the number of junctions. In literal distinctions it lies between square-root and linear growth. With the growing end fixed, testing which sets of shared distinctions are themselves bonds recovers all parity equations. [How many distinctions must be read together?](../research/BOND-READING-SCALE-20261119-001/decision.md)
  Even with the growing end fixed, for every fixed number of distinctions read jointly there is a reached parity stage whose complete readings through that order match under a renaming and whose next pair bond distances differ. This holds with at most one shared distinction per ordinary pair of junctions. The original two-attachment example still matches at all later rounds. [Later bond distances](../research/LATER-BOND-READING-20261119-001/decision.md)
  Even when every two junctions meet at most once, a renaming that fixes every current bond distance and the entire growing end pointwise can fail at the next complete round. The smallest reached failure has 12 junctions and 23 distinctions, with both minima exhausted over all ages. At the next round one old pair reads two and its image reads three. [Minimum meet-once failure](../research/GROWN-END-MEET-ONCE-20261119-001/decision.md)
  Under complete-round cheapest-count parity growth, a renaming can fix every current bond distance and every live junction pointwise and fail on an old pair one lawful round later. The two distances then read two and three. The six-junction start has repeated meetings. The identity still carries its successor. [Reachable matching](../research/GROWN-END-SWAP-20261119-001/decision.md)
  In the one-spent fourth-junction family a current legal three-parent choice forces the complete physical presentation against every reachable rival from bond distances through five, at every size. No one-for-one or larger replacement of the four attachments can hide. At 31 distinctions a lawful exchange removes both triples while preserving distance four, and distance five distinguishes it. Under a source-family promise even an empty three-parent menu is decidable from the table. An empty member against a positive outsider remains open. [Four attachments recovered](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001P/decision.md)
  With one fourth-junction output spent and a live three-parent choice, bond distances through five fix both surviving loose outputs and every smaller physical junction at all sizes. Only overlapping four-attachment sets of a single six-place junction can still differ. All 3,476 one-spent sources through 29 distinctions are uniquely determined. Distance four fails at 23. Whether a larger overlapping attachment can reverse a mark remains open. [One-spent holder recovery](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001O/decision.md)
  At four three-output additions, two arrangements on 31 distinctions agree on every pair distance while the changed junction holds a legal triple. Every reachable rival for this table allows the same three choices, already through distance four. Distance five recovers every junction on all 50,681 four-addition source types through width 27. When the fourth join takes three parents and its three outputs are still loose, distance five is sharp for deciding every triple at arbitrary size. The wider four-addition permission claim stays open. [Four additions at the proposed parents](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001N/decision.md)
  With a unique three-place site and exactly three later three-output joins, bond distances through five determine all three-parent permissions, sharply and against any reachable mixed rival. A third three-output join with one parent has sharp cutoff four, as does one with two parents. In the one-parent branch four also identifies the entire allowed-state relation. A last choice between complementary triples is the only possible hidden junction, first at width 32 with an empty menu. [A sharp reader for all three parent counts](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001M/decision.md)
  When the third and last three-output addition takes two parents, bond distances through four determine every three-parent join at all finite sizes. If any triple is legal, those readings identify every actual junction, even against unrestricted reachable rivals. Four is necessary at width 20. At width 32 identical exact pair readings and states can hide the last junction with both menus empty. [Sharp cutoff and junction recovery](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001L/decision.md)
  When the third and last three-output addition takes three parents, bond distances through five determine every current three-parent join at all finite sizes, sharply. The complete distance table can hide the six-place parity equation at width 28. Both histories then forbid every current triple. [Sharp distance cutoff and a hidden equation](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001K/decision.md)
  A true six-bit bond on nine ordinary sites appears at width 21. Across the minimum-width single-ternary family, every junction and next join is determined through bond distance four at all finite sizes, including after a second shrink. A full table can still mislead the published ring algorithm by its choice between tied candidates on this serial source. [Seven-to-six inverse and ring comparison](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001J/decision.md)
  The six-bit candidate on the four-place cycle is excluded through bond distance three, even after arbitrary mixed additions. With one two-parent addition making two outputs anywhere and all other additions making three, distances through five recover all actual joins against every reachable rival. The horizon is sharp. [Cycle and all-size inverse](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001I/decision.md)
  For any finite three-dimensional predecessor arrangement in which three-parent junctions may omit one unused output, bond distances through five determine all junctions and next joins against every reachable mixed rival. The cutoff is sharp and fully unrestricted mixed triples remain open. [High-junction theorem and sharp example](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001H/decision.md)
  In serial parity growth, if one presentation has at most four distinctions at every junction, every full-table isometry identifies the actual junction supports and the whole next menu.
