For two three-fresh additions distances through four suffice and three can give opposite triple choices. For each larger count the sharp horizon is five. Full-table triple sufficiency remains open when both presentations contain a five- or six-place junction. [Incidence theorem and sharp cutoffs](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001G/decision.md)
  For the serial law with two or three fresh distinctions, the full bond-distance table fixes every marked three-parent verdict through width 26. A first opposing example would need at least 27 distinctions. Junctions of at most four places and at most one three-fresh addition also give a sufficient all-size condition on one source. [Six-width comparison and restricted theorem](../research/VARIABLE-OUTPUT-READER-20261118-001F/decision.md)
  With the original eight betweenness distinctions attached, two mixed presentations have identical full states on the same 36 distinctions. One permits a complete next round of eleven joins and the other cannot run a complete round. The difference persists through one more common join. [Proof and checks](../research/MIXED-NEXT-JOIN-20261117-001/hidden_eight.md)
  On the aligned three-dimensional continuation with the original eight kept and hidden, anonymous bond distances through five determine all permitted next joins at every complete round. Permuting the three loose distinctions at a growing junction can realize any of their allowed assignments. [Proof and checks](../research/ALIGNED-NEXT-JOIN-20261117-001/aligned_rounds.md)
  Starting with the original eight alone, temporary tests using their own betweenness recover all twelve neighbor pairs from scalar bond distances. Every recovered orientation reaches the first aligned round. Four literal arrangements give identical feedback under fresh parity alone. [Proof and checks](../research/BOOTSTRAP-ALIGNMENT-20261117-001/getting_aligned.md)
  `COUNT-BALLS-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/COUNT-BALLS-20261118-001/count_balls.md); refereed) read the
  count's balls. On the six-regular weave the start's class fills a round outline and on chain grids a smooth one just off
  round, where bond distance gives polygons; the outline's growth against the ball reads the meeting pattern's dimension, while
  the count's own scale reads d/η. One start's growth does not read the number of orders: from the middle of its line a ladder of two
  orders and a comb of one grow identically (proved), and every start's growth taken together, read without names, merges bases
  of two and three orders from seven distinctions (computed), while the full table keeps them (narrowed after the referee). So the orders sit in the count's arrangement, which start's row is
  which, not in its growth.
  `ROW-ARRANGE-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/ROW-ARRANGE-20261118-001/row_arrange.md); refereed) located
  them: one round of reading each start's growth against every other's, tagged by count, tells apart every grown base to eight
  distinctions and every base in three samples at nine, so it recovers the orders there (computed; a complete invariant there, not
  special to orders); no local view does (reach 8 to 10 here,
  and for every reach two rings sharing a distinction fool it, proved); no fixed round does in general (simplex graphs of rings,
  proved), and the number of orders cannot be read from the full table in polynomial time unless P = NP
  (classical). Distance holds it, at a cost.
  `CUMULANT-DIST-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/CUMULANT-DIST-20261118-001/cumulant_dist.md); refereed, B200)
  found one distance for both laws on small trees and parity bases (narrowed after the referee; and bond distance already does
  so on every knot tree, steps apart plus one under any mixture, `FREE-READ-20261118-001`, corrected the same day): the least number of extra distinctions a set holding the pair needs, plus −log₃ of how
  strongly the set is bound beyond its parts (its joint cumulant). It gives one step per knot under parity and not-all-equal
  alike, steps apart on every parity tree (proved) and on every small knot tree under any mixture (computed to five knots), but
  not on large not-all-equal trees with a hub (corrected the same day: proved counterexample of 65 knots), bond distance minus
  one on every parity base (proved), and the count's τ on a chain grid. It ignores which value is called 1, so it reads a
  split line as a ring and cannot read the orders a betweenness growth builds (A103).
  `TREE-STEPS-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/TREE-STEPS-20261118-001/tree_steps.md); refereed) repaired it on
  trees: counting only irreducible sets (bound, with no smaller correlated set holding the pair: a relative of K2, not K2) removes
  the hub shortcut (proved), keeps τ on correlated pairs and bond distance minus one on parity bases (proved), and is steps apart
  on every knot tree except one mixed case, computed there (every six-knot tree, 94,848 pairs). On a mixed cycle (a not-all-equal
  path closed by a parity knot) it misreads the parity knot as k steps (the referee), so freedom, not zero correlation, is still
  to be read.
  `FREE-READ-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/FREE-READ-20261118-001/free_read.md); refereed) reads it: bond
  distance, the knot test applied to sets, is steps apart plus one on every knot tree of parity, not-all-equal and betweenness
  knots in any mixture (proved), hub-free, and reads the mixed cycle's parity knot as one knot. It needs no measure, only which
  values occur. Freedom and correlation part on cycles: on split grids every pair lies in a knot of three, so bond distance is
  flat and only correlation grades them; and freedom cannot mend the cumulant distance's hub shortcut, whose set is free (post
  hoc). Its referee proved bond distance satisfies the triangle inequality on every parity base; off parity bases and knot trees
  it fails once a pair that no bond holds is read as infinitely far (`BOND-METRIC-20261118-001`).
  `BOND-METRIC-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/BOND-METRIC-20261118-001/bond_metric.md); refereed) settled where:
  bond distance (and bond distance minus one) is a metric wherever knots compose like cycles, so on every parity base (proved), and
  among the 34 three-place laws it is a metric on every base of copies of one law exactly for parity (proved by a failing base for
  each of the other eight). Under not-all-equal it is a distance on trees and on the tori checked, not in general: two knots sharing
  two distinctions put their thirds infinitely far apart, each one knot from a shared distinction.
  `NAE-METRIC-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/NAE-METRIC-20261118-001/nae_metric.md); refereed) found where under
  not-all-equal: bond distance minus one adds across a joint (a distinction whose removal splits the knots), so bond distance is a
  true distance exactly when it is one in every block (proved for every not-all-equal and betweenness base); trees of knots and rings
  are true distances with bond distance one more than steps apart (proved); every small failing block has two knots meeting twice,
  and every series-parallel pattern of single meetings of plain not-all-equal knots checked passes (computed; one betweenness knot
  can break it, its referee).
  `DOUBLE-MEET-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/DOUBLE-MEET-20261118-001/double_meet.md); refereed) looked inside
  the weave: its double meetings are harmless. On every patch without holes in its census (22,143), bond distance fails to be a true
  distance exactly when some pair is unbonded (beyond the census, from twelve knots, also through such a pair bonded too far: its
  referee's Block on the general reading), and every unbonded pair is two lone distinctions (each in one knot) of opposite classes
  facing each other; the lone double meeting is the smallest case. Bond distance is at least steps apart + 1 there (sketched through
  the height function, checked) and equal to it on all but 64 bonded pairs.
  A single temporary test pair can certify a true neighbor on a favorable reading. Under the connected growth rule the present bond distances cannot guarantee the first neighbor: even direct replacement admits continued ambiguity. [Proof and checks](../research/ONE-PROBE-ALIGNMENT-20261118-001/one_pair.md)
  A rule with 75 patterns compiled for the 35-junction target and its join order reads the current bond-distance table. Its instrument can replace both tests together. The construction is computed, with a symmetry argument for the last 27 joins. Individually moving one table-identified pair at a time can be blocked before the second join, even with perfect recall in the referee’s finite game. [Two live tests and referee](../research/TWO-PROBE-ALIGNMENT-20261118-001/two_pairs.md)

### A98 · The owner's "no skipping" is read as betweenness of a pairwise count, with flips counted

**Assumed** · 2026-11-17 · reading

- **Statement.** In `DISTANCE-ORDER-20261117-001`, the owner's "if you're at 3 on the number line, you can't just skip 4 to get to
  5, so there is an order" is read on a candidate pairwise distance D in four ways:
  - D is built from unit steps: it is a whole multiple of its least value, with a unit step toward every farther distinction;
  - betweenness: v lies between o and w when D(o, w) = D(o, v) + D(v, w);
  - the gap graph of pairs with nothing between, which is a path for a line;
  - the longest chain from a start, each member between the start and the next.

  The flip of a distinction, the same distinction read with its values exchanged, counts as a distinction for betweenness.
- **Why.** Taken by default in that epic
  ([decision note](../research/DISTANCE-ORDER-20261117-001/distance_order.md)), on the advisor's advice, as the literature's metric
  betweenness and graphic metrics. A level-by-level test was dropped because an exact least value is attained by too few pairs.
  Flips are counted because the owner says there is no direction: betweenness on distinctions alone changes when one distinction's
  values are renamed.
- **Rests on it.** That epic's reading that the disagreement count has no order on the thread, the knot chain and the six-regular
  weave, and so A97's narrowing on bases whose knots form cycles.
- **Challenge.** Read "no skipping" another way and test it on the same bases. Two candidates:
  - order by levels from a start, where reaching a farther level needs a member of each nearer level, measured on a coarsened
    count;
  - betweenness of sets rather than of single distinctions.

  Or ask the owner whether his order is between three distinctions, as a knot is, or in levels from a starting point.
  Partly run by the referee `DISTANCE-ORDER-R1` with the weakest order-only reading: w lies between u and v when it is nearer to
  each than they are to each other (the literature's relative neighbourhood graph), in τ = −log₃ |1 − 2d/N|. On the weave at L = 6, 9
  and 12, a start then has exactly twelve neighbours with nothing between: its six knot-neighbours and the six second neighbours of
  its own class. Every farther distinction has something between. So under that reading the count carries a local order with
  nothing skipped, but not the weave's own steps, and A97's narrowing on bases with cycles holds only under this entry's strict
  reading.
  "Levels from a starting point" was then run by `DISTANCE-DIM-20261117-001` (frozen). Under the relative-neighbourhood test the
  levels have bounded degree: 12 on the weave, 8 and 2 on a not-all-equal chain grid, 3 to 4 on the thread. They grow as each
  meeting pattern does: quadratically on the weave and the chain grid, linearly on the thread, and exponentially on a knot tree.
  Other nearness tests give other graphs (its referee: the six-nearest test splits the weave into its three classes, and the
  unit-ball degree grows with L), and none gives an order. Exact ties need exact arithmetic: floating
  ties broke one prediction there. Under plain implications this entry's strict reading is satisfied exactly: betweenness is the
  order's intervals.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-25 · [KNOTS-WITHIN-REACH-20261125-001](../research/KNOTS-WITHIN-REACH-20261125-001/decision.md) (computed, referee pending): the registered betweenness test on triples of members fails on some stage under each of four laws (for example 16,639 of 61,925 triples under the ordinary count), and the order test against arrival delay fails from a stage of 7 knots. These are stage readings on rounds 1 to 3 of small starts. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-17 · Scoped after the referee `DISTANCE-DIM-R1`: degree 12 belongs to the relative-neighbourhood test.
  - 2026-11-17 · Challenge "levels from a start" run by `DISTANCE-DIM-20261117-001`; status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-17 · Challenge partly run by the referee `DISTANCE-ORDER-R1` (the order-only reading above); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-17 · Added by `DISTANCE-ORDER-20261117-001`.

### A99 · States-only growth is judged on conservative betweenness extensions with up to three knots per step

**Assumed** · 2026-11-17 · scope

- **Statement.** In `STATE-RULES-20261117-001`, and in `GRID-GROW-20261117-001` before it, a growth step adds one distinction with
  one to three betweenness knots, each on that distinction and two old ones. Every old state must still extend, and the new
  distinction must be constrained and not a copy of an old one. A rule weighs these steps by what it reads (the state count, or
  also which knots meet), and the number of orders is read as the least number of lines whose product holds the distinctions.
- **Why.** Taken by default, on the advisor's advice. Betweenness knots keep both constant states, so the states read as a split
  system. Three knots is the fewest that allow a fill, so a second order can start in one step (the owner's item that the moves must
  include the event counted). Keeping every old state makes a step an extension of the base, not a rewrite of it.
- **Rests on it.**
  - That study's obstruction: no uniform law tried makes two orders and keeps them. The one-knot rule keeps the start's number of
    orders.
  - `CUBE-DOUBLING-20261118-001`'s theorem: the cheapest rule with three or more knots per step reaches the cube at every
    doubling (its L4 uses that every old state is kept).
  - `KNOT-CAP-20261117-001`'s, `LOCAL-ROOM-20261118-001`'s and `NEVER-NEST-20261118-001`'s laws, built on top of it.
  - `MEET-ORDERS-20261118-001`'s Theorem 1 and census (KF3 from the presentations of a line).
- **Challenge.** Widen the move set and rerun the same weightings:
  - all 34 three-place knots, or four-place ones (the parity of a filled cell is one four-place knot);
  - more knots per step;
  - steps allowed to remove old states;
  - a cost on knots per distinction.

  A law in the wider set that stops at two orders would narrow the obstruction.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-18 · `FAR-SIGN-20261118-001` built on it (SEED with knot-local moves, its crossing-making fills, and fills on any pairs
    for an ablation); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `COPY-CLASS-20261118-001` built on it (SEED with knot-local moves, its copy steps); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `COPY-READ-20261118-001` built on it (SEED with knot-local moves, its crossing-making fills); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `MID-RULE-20261118-001` built on it (the two-middle and one-end rules, knot-local and free); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `KEEP-READ-20261118-001` built on it (SEED with knot-local moves, every move's local pictures); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `KNOT-SWITCH-20261118-001` built on it (KF3, NN, NN2 and SEED with knot-local moves, every knot recorded);
    status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `PICK-TWO-20261118-001` built on it (caps, prices and rules told no number, on fills and subdivisions); status
    unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `NEVER-NEST-20261118-001` built on it (rules that take only the fills keeping two orders grow full two-way grids;
    its move set is this entry's); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `MEET-TWIST-20261118-001` built on it (proof for this population and rule); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `MEET-GROW-20261118-001` built on it (the same population and rule); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `MEET-ORDERS-20261118-001` built on it (KF3 from every fewest-knot presentation of a line); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `CUBE-DOUBLING-20261118-001` proved the cube growth under this entry (its referee: for any cap of three or more
    knots per step); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-17 · Added by `STATE-RULES-20261117-001`.

### A100 · How knots meet is compared on fewest-knot presentations of a line

**Assumed** · 2026-11-18 · scope

- **Statement.** `MEET-ORDERS-20261118-001` compares how knots meet on presentations of a line by the fewest betweenness knots,
  n − 2 of them, which are exactly the line's bracketings, and reads mirror images as one. Presentations with more knots than
  needed (redundant ones), with other knot kinds, or of bases other than a line are not in its population.
- **Why.** Taken by default. The earlier matched starts (`STATE-RULES-20261117-001`, `KNOT-CAP-20261117-001`) were fewest-knot
  presentations; with n − 2 knots each interior distinction is the middle of exactly one knot, which makes every presentation a
  bracketing and the census finite (Catalan(n − 2) per labelling).
- **Rests on it.** That study's Theorem 1 and census, and its reading that the bracketing is what the states do not carry.
- **Challenge.** Add redundant knots to a bracketing (a knot whose rule the others already imply) and rerun KF3: knot-locality
  reads the extra co-knotted pairs, so the order distribution can change with the states and the bracketing fixed. Then try
  presentations of the 2 × 2 pattern or of a branching tree.
  First result (`MEET-ORDERS-R1`, 18 November 2026): adding one redundant knot to a five-start (the states unchanged, co-knotted
  pairs added) changes KF3's chance of two orders at n = 7 in all 21 cases tried, by 0.04 to 0.33, and the placements no longer
  sort by bare bracketing (371/600 and 1523/2352 occur for both chains and the middle). The bracketing account holds for
  fewest-knot presentations only; with extra knots, how the knots meet is more than the bracketing.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-18 · `MEET-ADDED-20261118-001` built on it; status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `MEET-HIST-20261118-001` built on it; status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `MEET-DIST-20261118-001` built on it; status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `MEET-TWIST-20261118-001` built on it (proof for this population and rule); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `MEET-ORDERS-R1`: material (one redundant knot changes the second-step chance in 21 of 21 cases); status
    unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `MEET-GROW-20261118-001` built on it (the same population and rule); status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · Added by `MEET-ORDERS-20261118-001`.

### A101 · The number of orders a growth rule keeps is given: two

**Under challenge** · 2026-11-18 · scope

- **Statement.** In `NEVER-NEST-20261118-001`, the rules that grow two-way grids from a line are told the number two. NN and NNF
  take only the fills after which the base has at most two orders (read from all the splits at once), and lengthen by a
  subdivision (one knot on a pair one split apart) when no such fill exists; CC is given a first crossing. Nothing marks which
  split belongs to which order, and nothing is remembered, but no rule there chooses two by itself. NNF is the cheapest rule with
  the cap added; uncapped, it makes every order (`CUBE-DOUBLING-20261118-001`), so the two is wholly the cap's.
- **Why.** Taken by default: B197 asked whether a rule that never nests its fills keeps two orders, which fixes two as the target.
  The cheapest growth with no target gains an order at every doubling (`CUBE-DOUBLING-20261118-001`), and a rule told only to keep
  the number of orders it has never fills a line, since every fill of a line makes two.
- **Rests on it.** That study's capability for G1: a law that grows full two-way grids forever from a line, read from the states
  (proved for NNF, computed for NN). G1's target, space of low dimension from a uniform local law, is met there only with the
  dimension given.
- **Challenge.** Tell the rule a cap of d orders and test whether it fills full d-way grids (the arguments for N3 and N4 look as if
  they carry over); then look for a rule told no number that settles at two, for instance one that refuses any fill raising the
  number of orders once a fill has kept it, or one that reads the cap from the start.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-18 · `FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001/fresh_count.md); referee
    pending) gave the parity counterpart: with the cubic study's joining rule, one fresh count for every join must equal the first
    knot's size, the old cap of three is what made four stall, and the fewest counts close the first knot; no rule picks a number
    from nothing there either. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `FAR-SIGN-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/FAR-SIGN-20261118-001/far_sign.md); refereed, confirmed
    with fixes) found the referee's sign exact on SEEDK's fills (149,162 fresh fills, computed; two computed facts with L4 and L5). It
    is not a theorem of the fill, since a base of seven distinctions breaks both directions. It proved that under the sign the copy
    step is "the moved splits are one whole class". The copy step stays global in what it reads. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `COPY-CLASS-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/COPY-CLASS-20261118-001/copy_class.md); refereed, Block on
    its reading) proved that the copy step is decided on the splits separating the fill's two end pairs and a model's crossers. Its
    claim that the fill's own pairs name the model was withdrawn: the rule restated SEED's clause. The referee found one sign that
    decides it, the far end pair separated by every crossed split, which reads every crossed split. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · `COPY-READ-20261118-001` ([decision note](../research/COPY-READ-20261118-001/copy_read.md); refereed) found
    that SEED's copy step, which grows two orders over the whole line, is not decided by the fill's own knots or the betweenness
    among its distinctions, but every copy step met has one closure picture, shared by under 2% of crossing-making fills (the

    distinction's two sides. Caps of d give d, and prices settle where their increments say. Status: Under challenge.
  - 2026-11-18 · `NEVER-NEST-20261118-001`'s referee (R1): what is given is the cap and the subdivision fallback, not only the
    number; the two is wholly the cap's. Statement narrowed; status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · Added by `NEVER-NEST-20261118-001`.

### A102 · An endless base's counts are read in its maximal-entropy weighting

**Assumed** · 2026-11-18 · reading

- **Statement.** On an endless base, the disagreement count of two distinctions is read as the limit of the uniform weighting of
  allowed states on ever larger finite pieces, the weighting of greatest entropy per distinction. On the six-regular weave under
  not-all-equal this is the translation-invariant weighting in which every co-knotted pair has equal values with chance 1/3
  (Kenyon's kernel for the honeycomb's perfect matchings); on chain grids it is the unique weighting of the high-temperature Ising
  law the chains reduce to.
- **Why.** Taken by default in `COUNT-BALLS-20261118-001`, as earlier studies read finite tori and long strips
  (`DISTANCE-DIM-20261117-001`, `WEAVE-LAWS-20261117-001`). It is the natural reading of "uniform over the allowed states" when there
  are infinitely many, but on the weave it is one of a family: weightings of other slopes of the height also give translation-
  invariant limits, from pieces with tilted boundaries.
- **Rests on it.** `COUNT-BALLS-20261118-001`'s round outline on the weave (the amplitude, its isotropy, the ball counts), and the
  chain grids' outlines.
- **Challenge.** Read the weave in a weighting of another slope: the height's stiffness is then anisotropic, and the count's outline
  should be an ellipse, not round. The cheapest test is Kenyon's kernel with unequal pair weights (exact, as in
  `COUNT-BALLS-20261118-001B`), stopping at the ellipse's ratio as a function of the slope; then ask whether any uniform local rule
  on finite pieces selects the round one.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-18 · Added by `COUNT-BALLS-20261118-001`.

### A103 · A base's values are data: which value is called 1 is part of the base

**Assumed** · 2026-11-18 · reading

- **Statement.** A base is a set of allowed states with the values 0 and 1 as given, not up to flipping a distinction's values.
  Readings that use the values (the raw disagreement count, the all-equal states, the splits' sides) are taken as readings of the
  base itself. Flipping one distinction's values (the literature's switching) turns a betweenness knot into a not-all-equal knot and
  a split base into another set of states with the same switching-invariant readings.
- **Why.** Taken by default in every split-base study: the betweenness growth (`STATE-RULES-20261117-001`, `CUBE-DOUBLING-20261118-001`,
  `NEVER-NEST-20261118-001`) keeps both all-equal states, which fixes the values up to one global flip, and the count is twice the
  L1 distance only in that convention (`GRID-GROW-20261117-001`). `CUMULANT-DIST-20261118-001` showed what changes without it: up to
  flipping values a split line is a ring (proved), so a value-free distance reads the line of eight as a ring of eight, and
  `COUNT-BALLS-20261118-001` found that a base and its translate by one of its states share every state distance while their
  numbers of orders differ.
- **Rests on it.** The count as twice the L1 distance on split grids and the λ = 2 ruler; the number of orders k and λ of grown
  bases; every census of orders.
- **Challenge.** Read the program's results value-free, as the owner's "no values, only relations" would suggest: which survive
  switching (the cumulant distance, the knot trees' steps apart) and which do not (the split line's ends, the orders). The cheapest
  test is the switching group of the grown split bases to n = 8: how many orders does each switching class carry, and is there a
  value-free reading that recovers a two-order grid's ruler? `CUMULANT-DIST-20261118-001` found that in two orders no switching
  equivalence takes a corner's neighbour to the far corner, so something value-free distinguishes them there.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-18 · Added by `CUMULANT-DIST-20261118-001`.

### A104 · Parity growth is judged under the complete-round joining rule

**Under challenge** · 2026-11-19 · scope

- **Statement.** Growth of parity bases from one knot is studied under the joining rule of `CUBIC-FROM-A-KNOT-20261117-001`
  applied in complete rounds: every set of current junctions that pairwise share a parent and share none all together is joined
  at once, every pair so placed and in no such set is joined, and every other loose distinction gets a junction of its own; a round
  is unavailable when some junction has too few loose distinctions. The count rule decides only the number of fresh distinctions.
- **Why.** Taken by default from the cubic study by `FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001`, which asked what the fresh count decides with the
  rule fixed. The serial rule, adding one junction at a time, never stalls and admits trees (the cubic study's own remark), so it
  constrains nothing the study measured.
- **Rests on it.** Every result of `FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001`
  ([decision note](../research/FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001/fresh_count.md)): the grown pattern is the ideals of the descent order, one
  count for every join must equal the first knot's size, the cap of three picks three, the fewest counts close one knot into the 2^a
  pattern.
- **Challenge.** A serial or asynchronous rule that reads only bond distances and joins whatever its own test allows: does any such
  rule avoid trees without the complete round, and what does it build? The cheapest discriminator is the serial rule with a
  distance-only preference (join before extending) from one knot, compared with the complete round on the same seeds.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-24 · `LOCAL-ROUND-20261123-001` ([decision](../research/LOCAL-ROUND-20261123-001/decision.md), referee pending) swapped the complete round for births made one at a time under a guard: a knot acts when every knot made alongside it has parents that have finished giving, and replays the round on that record. In every order searched this builds what the complete rounds build, with waiting, without it and under ordinary growth. The round is then kept by nobody, but it is still what the law replays, from a written record of parents (A242). Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · `RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001`, ticket C ([decision](../research/RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001/decision.md), referee pending): under the added rules of A224 the complete round can be replaced by rounds taken part by part in any order, with the same structure built (726 runs and an argument), but not by one birth at a time with every knot reading again, under which the ring jams. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-20 · INFLUENCE-CONE-20261120-001 · Within the identified class every fair writing of the fixed structure gives the complete-cut cone at each size. Recomputed individual births can lose the exact receiver pair even with no edit. [Scheduling controls](../research/INFLUENCE-CONE-20261120-001/orders.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · GENERATORS-20261120-001 · On identified starts, the emitter and both-persist meeting are fixed by parentage. A first direct mixed birth is possible at only the first parent step after simultaneous release. Delivering births along the fixed map and serial recomputation make different predictions in the explosive controls.
  - 2026-11-20 · READ-ORIGIN-20261120-001 · A complete ordinary boundary carries every subsequent complete layer without an origin tag. A correctly written partial layer with the same physical holdings as a new start can instead need linear information for its remaining births. The size of a complete layer and the choice of observation in a partial one have separate operational consequences.
  - 2026-11-20 · SERIAL-REPAIRS-20261120-001 · The two seven-knot sources recognize the underlying ready births from present physical holdings in bounded ordinary radius. Strict global joins-first with weak fairness only on its filtered list can deprive a distant piece of every further birth for any fixed ordinary radius. The least-unfinished-depth guard restores completion under both policies while restricting interleaving by recoverable formation size.
  - 2026-11-20 · FRESH-DEMAND-20261120-001 · Complete-roster serialization and the completed identified map preserve two versus one in every fair completion of the seven-knot starts. With present-state admission and recalculation the same comparison holds for some unrestricted schedules, and no joins-first schedules making both exact births. Distinguish permanent loss from unfair starvation of U,V. The completed map supplies future incidence, while only parents must precede each cluster.
  - 2026-11-20 · BOND-CLOSURE-20261120-001 · Both proposal legality and funding are premises of the closure reader. The same local inputs may exhaust an otherwise unfunded held group. In the not-all-equal comparison both funded births continue, including the one exhausting all distinctions. A seven-knot example separates one from two fresh distinctions on two funded continuing histories for both laws.
  - 2026-11-20 · ORDINARY-DYNAMICS-20261120-001 · The complete funded parity update on a connected physical start can require the fact that a birth consumes every loose distinction, even inside the identified class. The same four-knot sources under not-all-equal lose the closing difference. If continuing is promised, one shared-parent observation step suffices in the identified class and two in general.
  - 2026-11-19 · `STATE-FEEDS-GROWTH-20261119-001` · With proposals read on the old stage, a hot-parent veto and ordinary cold growth give one coloured result up to renaming. Interleaving an independent value change and irreversible births gives different physical structures. These statements require their respective event conventions.
  - 2026-11-19 · The law does not matter by `NAE-GROWTH-20261119-001`: under not-all-equal the same policy gives the same counts at
    every round, except that parity's closing tops relay (proved; the whole census checked). The rule's results are not parity's
    alone.
  - 2026-11-19 · Under challenge by `ROUND-FREE-20261119-001`: serial laws that read only the present presentation build
    order-dependent, thinner structures and can decide whether a growth lives; the round does one job, telling each new junction
    how many partners it will have, and in the cluster rule's scope the cluster map does that job without a tick (round = size − 1).
  - 2026-11-18 · Adopted by `FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001`.

### A105 · A growth starts from the smallest knot

**Assumed** · 2026-11-18 · working premise

- **Statement.** A growth from one knot starts from the smallest knot of its law: under parity, the three-place parity knot, two
  loops' distinctions and one contact.
- **Why.** Taken by default from the cubic study, which started from one three-place parity knot, and from the foundation's knot of
  two loops and one contact (A2). `FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001` proved that one fresh count for every join must equal the first knot's
  size, so the dimension of that growth is the first knot's size, and three only for a three-place first knot. A four-place parity
  knot also passes K1–K3.
- **Rests on it.** "From the smallest knot, one count gives dimension three" (`FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001`,
  [decision note](../research/FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001/fresh_count.md)); the cubic study's choice of three fresh distinctions.
- **Challenge.** Give a native reason why the first knot should be the smallest, for instance the cheapest knot by allowed states
  (2^(a−1) for a places), or a competition between first knots of different sizes in one base, and test whether the three-place
  knot wins; or show that nothing in the law prefers it, which leaves the number a choice of start.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-18 · Returned to Assumed by `FIRST-KNOT-20261118-001`
    ([decision note](../research/FIRST-KNOT-20261118-001/first_knot.md); referee pending): nothing in the parity law or the
    joining rule prefers the smallest first knot. The rule reads only a first knot's open distinctions, never its size; a three-place
    and a four-place first knot, apart or touching once, cannot grow under any one count, and neither wins. The premise now rests on
    the foundation's first emergence, two loops joined by one contact (A2), and the dimension also depends on how the first knots are
    arranged and presented.
  - 2026-11-18 · Under challenge: `FIRST-KNOT-20261118-001` asks whether the parity law or the joining rule prefers the smallest
    first knot (two first knots of different sizes, apart and sharing a distinction, under one count and under the fewest counts).
  - 2026-11-18 · Recorded by `FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001`.

### A106 · Arrangements of first knots are weighted uniformly up to five knots of three to five places

**Overturned** · 2026-11-19 · measure

- **Statement.** "Typical" among arrangements of first knots means frequent among the distinct connected arrangements of up to
  five parity knots of three to five places (each shared distinction in two knots, counted once up to renaming), or among their
  labelled descriptions (knots labelled, places not), and holding at each number of knots.
- **Why.** Taken by default by `TOUCH-CENSUS-20261118-001` to read the owner's no-choice direction (nothing chooses an
  arrangement); the advisor noted that neither weighting is canonical and that the cutoffs are a choice.
- **Rests on it.** "Two-dimensional growth is typical, three and four rare" (`TOUCH-CENSUS-20261118-001`,
  [decision note](../research/TOUCH-CENSUS-20261118-001/touch_census.md)).
- **Challenge.** Extend to six knots or to six places, weight by random pairing of places, or by a growth process that adds knots
  one at a time, and test whether two stays typical and three rare.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-19 · `CAP-CHAINS-20261119-001` (proved; [decision note](../research/CAP-CHAINS-20261119-001/cap_chains.md)): the cap
    at five places, not the weighting, is what bounds the chains of starts with no dead knot. In the proved class every piece then
    carries at most two, so tree starts grow in at most two dimensions. Five is sharp: six places, or a dead knot at five, allow
    any number. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · Overturned as a reading of what the growth makes typical, by `WEIGHTINGS-20261119-001`
    ([decision note](../research/WEIGHTINGS-20261119-001/weightings.md)). That epic froze seven weightings before reading any and
    read them with the proved theorem.
    - Its two named alternatives keep two typical and three absent: random pairing of places (a configuration model) and growth
      that adds knots one at a time.
    - Its third, more than five places, does not. Under uniform random trees, whose knots get the places their joins need, three
      or more dimensions is 12% of growers at twelve knots and 37% at twenty. Every one of those starts has a knot of six or more
      places.
    - So "two typical, three and four rare" belongs to this weighting's cap of five places, crowded or not (referee R1: 0 of
      25,133 in-class growers within five places reach three). It is not a property of the growth. A bare tree of such knots is a caterpillar with at most two chains (proved post hoc).
    - The census's figures stand as figures under this weighting (A71). The published claims that read them as the growth's are
      narrowed (docs/CORRECTIONS.md, 2026-11-19).
    - Status Assumed → Under challenge → Overturned.
  - 2026-11-19 · Under challenge by `WEIGHTINGS-20261119-001`.
  - 2026-11-19 · `SCALE-SHARE-20261119-001`: extended to 20 knots by Metropolis sampling, the labelled measure makes stalls
    typical for a counting reason (open places per knot fall like √(5/n)); among growers two dimensions takes over (98% at 20
    knots); distinct and labelled shares converge. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `JOIN-GROWTH-20261118-001`: a part joined to a ring knot needs a knot of five or six places at the joint (with
    fewer, round 1 stalls), so the cutoff at five places leaves out every ring with a part joined to it, the starts where every
    joined part speeds the ring; status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-18 · Adopted by `TOUCH-CENSUS-20261118-001`.

### A107 · Small joins of a ring and a two-fold piece are weighted uniformly over one bare census

**Assumed** · 2026-11-18 · measure

- **Statement.** "Typical" among joins of a ring of knots and a two-fold piece (two touching knots) means frequent among the 93
  bare starts of `JOIN-GROWTH-20261118-001`'s census D, counted once each: a ring of five four-place knots, bare or with one surplus
  distinction at the joined knot, one away or two away; the piece joined by one shared distinction, through a shared knot, or along
  a path of one or two knots; 0 to 2 listed surplus distinctions on each of the piece's knots (the path of two only with a bare
  ring); every knot given the fewest places that let round 1 start (shared distinctions plus round-1 demand, at least three).
- **Why.** Taken by default by `JOIN-GROWTH-20261118-001` to read the coordinator's "what is typical across the small ways of
  joining them, not one hand-picked join". The fewest places are forced: with fewer, round 1 stalls, so a join at a ring knot needs a
  knot of five or six places there, outside `A106`'s sizes.
- **Rests on it.** "Every small join of a ring and a two-fold piece makes the ring grow faster, by the product law"
  (`JOIN-GROWTH-20261118-001`, [decision note](../research/JOIN-GROWTH-20261118-001/join_growth.md)).
- **Challenge.** Rings of seven and nine, pieces of three or more knots, surplus distinctions placed at random, or a weighting by
  labelled descriptions; the law is stated in surplus counts, so a different convention changes the counts, not the law.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-19 · `WEIGHTINGS-20261119-001`: under random trees with one random extra line, at the places the joins need, every
    in-class start whose one cycle has knots attached grows exponentially (94 of 94 at six knots, 391 of 391 at twenty). Only a
    cycle through every knot stays one-dimensional, as the proved theorem says. "Every join speeds the ring" holds beyond the bare
    census; status unchanged ([decision note](../research/WEIGHTINGS-20261119-001/weightings.md)).
  - 2026-11-18 · Adopted by `JOIN-GROWTH-20261118-001`.

### A108 · The cheapest growth reads which junction holds which distinction, not only the allowed states

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-19 · scope

- **Statement.** The cheapest growth (`FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001`'s policy and fewest counts, A104) is applied to a presentation:
  a list of junctions and the distinctions each holds. Which distinctions are loose, which junctions are candidates, and how many
  fresh distinctions each new junction takes are read from that list. Two presentations with the same allowed states can therefore
  grow differently.
- **Why.** Taken by default from the cubic study and kept by every growth epic since. The policy is stated on junctions, and no
  study has defined it on a set of allowed states.
- **Rests on it.** Every result of the growth line: `FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001`, `TOUCH-CENSUS-20261118-001`,
  `JOIN-GROWTH-20261118-001`, `RING-PAIR-20261119-001`, `CLUSTER-ALL-20261119-001`, `ROUND-FREE-20261119-001`,
  `SCALE-SHARE-20261119-001` and `NAE-GROWTH-20261119-001`.
- **Challenge.** Define a growth that reads only the allowed states, for instance on a presentation fixed by them, such as the
  irredundant one under not-all-equal. Ask whether it is well defined on the stages where same-state presentations join
  differently (`NAE-GROWTH-20261119-001#1`).
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · Both ordinary laws give different growth from identical states on three named distinctions and on distinct physical supports with nine names. The parity family requires unbounded parent information even under renaming. [Named and renamed comparisons](../research/FOUR-READINGS-20261120-001B/results.md).
  - 2026-11-19 · `THREE-RIGID-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/THREE-RIGID-20261119-001/three_rigid.md); referee pending): with bond readings over sets as large as the largest knot (which force the states) and the growing end, the growth's later rounds are carried (Theorem M with Claim B*). Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `PROVE-B-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/PROVE-B-20261119-001/prove_b.md); refereed R1, no Block): the next table reads, for each bond, which growing junctions it holds (the split formula); the pair table records only least bond sizes, so it misses this where a tie is broken by a join's discount. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `GROWN-END-SWAP-20261119-001` (Codex's construction; [decision note](../research/GROWN-END-SWAP-20261119-001/decision.md); refereed R1): the growth reads the same joins on both sides of a reading-keeping table symmetry, yet the next table differs: what it reads beyond the table is which old pair a join brings closer. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `HUNT-SHORTEN-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/HUNT-SHORTEN-20261119-001/hunt_shorten.md); refereed R1, no Block): what keeps a table matching through a round is the round's whole reading, not backing alone: backed joins made by hand break matchings too. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `EXTEND-ROUND-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/EXTEND-ROUND-20261119-001/extend_round.md); refereed R1, no Block): proved what one round reads: the live junctions, their loose counts and the sets of them met by one junction (Lemma A). Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `CREATED-PAIR-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/CREATED-PAIR-20261119-001/created_pair.md); refereed R1, no Block): the growth reads the growing end (its loose distinctions), which the table does not mark: the created pair's isometries keep no loose distinction and the next round parts them, while carried pairs' isometries keep them and grow in step. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · Reach line corrected after the referees of `STATES-GROWTH-20261119-001`, `MANY-VALUED-20261119-001` and
    `DROPPED-JUNCTION-20261119-001` (R1). With every minimal constraint, not only those up to one place above the largest knot, the
    knots are the states' minimal constraints on 79.8% of small irredundant stages, not 93%, and undefined points also come from a
    single knot. What survives is MANY-VALUED's form: drawn from M(S), the presentation is unique up to renaming at every point
    tested (1,031 with every constraint), and the dependence comes from redundant knots and non-minimal supports. Under parity the
    eddies' dependence on the dropped junction is confirmed. Status unchanged: the statement is existential and holds.
  - 2026-11-19 · Under parity, by `DROPPED-JUNCTION-20261119-001`: the eddies depend on the dropped junction. On all 23 eddies only
    the original choice feeds itself, and the fewest-places rule departs from the cheapest growth. The parity side of the

### A109 · Self-feeding loops are few in kind and recur as shared routines, the owner's eddies

**Under challenge** · 2026-11-19 · working premise

- **Statement.** The owner's direction, not a result. The loops of knots that feed their own growth (eddies) come in a small
  number of kinds. Each kind acts the same way wherever it occurs, like a shared library routine, so a short catalogue of them,
  with what each does to whatever joins it, describes the self-feeding part of every growth. Testable form: in the cheapest growth
  (A104, A108), the distinct self-feeding loops up to a stated size fall into a few families, and a small generating set composes
  their ratios.
- **Why.** John Cullen, 18 November 2026 (Pacific time): "It's an interesting idea, loops of logic, then like eddies in a river
  that are shared across the universe essentially there are 'core-libs' kind of processing." Supporting facts so far:
  - a ring's ratio depends only on its knots and spares;
  - a ring keeps exactly its own growth inside a join;
  - working rings are about one grower in a thousand, though growers are full of cycles;
  - the same loops do the same thing under parity and under not-all-equal.
- **Rests on it.** The framing of `LOOP-CATALOGUE-20261119-001`. No result rests on it.
- **Challenge.** `LOOP-CATALOGUE-20261119-001` builds the catalogue. It is overturned if the distinct self-feeding loops up to the
  stated size need ever more independent kinds, or if no small generating set composes their ratios. It is confirmed, reopenable,
  if a few families and a short generating set cover them. "Shared across the universe" is an interpretation and stays one: no
  catalogue can settle it.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-19 · `WALL-READ-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/WALL-READ-20261119-001/wall_read.md)): the explosive kind
    holds one member read completely, partnered runaway growth, which never ends; it needs knots of eight places. The 14 sampled
    explosive starts were followed one or two rounds further and stay unread. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `MERGE-BURST-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/MERGE-BURST-20261119-001/merge_burst.md)): of 16
    explosive starts, 2 are read, and both end after one merge and the binomial burst of one block (9 and 781 junctions). 14 are
    unread within 6 GB and 15 minutes. None is shown to keep growing. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `EXPLOSIVE-20261119-001` refereed (R1). Narrowed: overlapping groups were present in every jump but are not shown
    to drive them (16 of 117 overlapping seven-place starts jumped), and growth without bound is unread. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `EXPLOSIVE-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/EXPLOSIVE-20261119-001/explosive.md)): outside the class,
    groups of three act as filled cells where they do not overlap. The explosive kind needs overlapping groups of three and more
    than five places. Its smallest case closes after one group of 309 parents and a binomial burst. The other explosive starts
    were not followed to their end, so it stays under challenge.
  - 2026-11-19 · `DEAD-CAP-20261119-001` refereed (R1, confirmed line by line). The in-class catalogue within five places is
    complete. Rates for two or more cycles are system singularities, computable but not closed forms. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `DEAD-CAP-20261119-001` (proved; [decision note](../research/DEAD-CAP-20261119-001/dead_cap.md)): within five
    places the whole proved class is classified, dead knots included. The growth class is the rank of the map's pieces: one cycle
    gives a rational count series, two or more an algebraic one. A branch system of a few equations gives every eddy's count
    exactly. So few in kind holds in the class within the cap, with the catalogue's factors and, for two or more cycles, the
    system's branches. The fastest found is the theta's 2.3830. A first-round group of three is exactly a cycle of three blocks,
    the entry point of the explosive kind outside the class, which keeps A109 under challenge.
  - 2026-11-19 · `RING-CORES-20261119-001` refereed (R1, confirmed). Narrowed: the golden-ratio ceiling is for rings of live
    knots. With dead knots, an 11-knot start with two dead hubs grows at about 2.383 (`DEAD-CAP-20261119-001`, referee pending),
    so the in-class catalogue within five places is completed by that study, not by the rings alone. Status unchanged.

### A110 · Blow-up is expected, and the unlimited structure is read by sampling it

**Assumed** · 2026-11-19 · working premise

- **Statement.** This is the owner's direction, not a result. The system is like a simulation whose simulator has no limits, so
  growth that blows up is the expected case, not a defect to avoid. The way to understand it is to sample the underlying
  structure, accepting that a sample will sometimes give outlier results. The program does not seek harmonies, or any other
  restriction, in order to avoid explosive growth. A study of explosive growth asks:
  - what a sample of this kind typically shows, under a stated weighting (A71);
  - whether that kind has its own regularity, or is where finite description ends.
- **Why.** John Cullen, 19 November 2026 (Pacific time), on the explosive-kind study: "When we talk about 'explosive', isn't that
  kind of expected? Whenever I envision the intuition, it always blows up. Imagine it's a simulation, where the simulator has no
  limits. That is the kind of system we're dealing with. My thought process was that we would need to do what amounts to
  essentially a sampling somehow of the underlying structure. Of course, this would result in outlier results sometimes I think,
  but it's the way I always envisioned it. I guess my question is, are we trying to get to harmonies to avoid explosive results?
  Or what's the intuition behind what's going on here?" The coordinator answered him in the project chat: no; blow-up is expected,
  and growth is sorted into stalls, fixed dimension, eddies and the not-yet-describable kind, so that a sample can be read against
  what is typical under a weighting.
- **Rests on it.** The framing of `EXPLOSIVE-20261119-001`: explosive growth is a kind to describe and sample, not an outcome to
  exclude. No result rests on it.
- **Challenge.** It could be challenged in two ways:
  - **Atypical under natural weightings.** Under the crowded weightings measured so far, most starts stall, and the eddies within
    five places are rare (`WEIGHTINGS-20261119-001`). A weighting under which explosive growth is the typical outcome, or one
    under which it never is, would test "always blows up".
  - **No stable sampled behaviour.** It would also be tested if the explosive kind admits no stable sampled behaviour at all:
    shares that do not settle as samples grow, under every weighting tried. Then sampling would not read it.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · Uniform draws of actual knots within a partnered runaway and a jump-selected start have different release outcomes. In the partnered case every birth inherits the entire cohort two parent steps earlier. These are conditional conclusions for the chosen hosts. [Late sampling](../research/FOUR-READINGS-20261120-001D/deep_explosive.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · GENERATORS-20261120-001 · The 480 labelled draws measure funding and complete growth among small starts, and the 16 archived descriptions are jump-selected. Those measurements supply no frequency of unending emission. Unbounded release sizes are consistent with the sampling premise.
  - 2026-11-20 · READ-ORIGIN-20261120-001 · The registered 640 lawful draws use equal counts of 64 per size from four to eight in a uniform labelled-tree weighting and in a tree-plus-one-uniform-missing-meeting weighting. Each holder has its minimum loose counts with an independent one-in-four spare. A uniformly permuted funded first layer and uniform proper prefix length yield one middle per funded start. Larger-size frequency and inverse computational cost remain open.
  - 2026-11-20 · SERIAL-REPAIRS-20261120-001 · The 640-source sample weights 64 independently drawn labels at each of five sizes, under a uniform labelled tree or a tree plus one uniform missing meeting, with a Bernoulli spare of probability one quarter per knot. Counts of whole funded births, distinct first actions and randomly chosen finite prefixes have different denominators. No weighting on infinite fair orders is assumed.
  - 2026-11-20 · FRESH-DEMAND-20261120-001 · For the dated source panel each of five size strata has 64 independent uniform labelled trees, with per-knot extra loose probability one quarter. One weighted observation per funded birth and one uniformly marked multi-parent event per funded start give different denominators. Reweighting either choice changes the empirical shares. All admissible-order results on the built pairs instead follow from proofs.
  - 2026-11-20 · BOND-CLOSURE-20261120-001 · The frequencies here use 128 independent uniform labelled tree draws in each of five source sizes with a Bernoulli extra loose distinction per holder, and a separate paired weighting of three random routes with an optional second part. Reweighting starts or sampling one proposal instead of all funded occurrences changes the interpretation of 127/247 and 128/128. Neither weighting represents all starts.
  - 2026-11-20 · ORDINARY-DYNAMICS-20261120-001 · The locality obstruction is proved by two finite sources and a stretching argument. One preparation closes, another has unending dimension-two formation counts. No random samples of unlimited growth were needed or drawn; the later source panels stopped at the registered gate.
  - 2026-11-20 · The owner's picture, recorded with the layer reading. On the layer reading, the rounds read one fixed unlimited
    structure outward, layer by layer; nothing is chosen, and each round is the next layer of the same structure. The owner's own
    picture, in his words: "I imagine it to be something like fractals, except the branches of the fractals are able to join with
    each other and are not separated so much by space." Both are interpretations, not results. They frame the world line (B207), the
    fate lead (B206) and sampling (B209). Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · The owner accepted a reading of what the rounds are. Asked "The structure changes over time? Like it adds
    more logic to itself?", the owner was answered that, as computed, each round adds knots, but where a knot's round is its cluster's
    size less one (`ROUND-FREE-20261119-001`, inside the cluster theorem's class) nothing has to change over time: the rounds
    read one fixed structure outward from its smallest pieces, a layer at a time, the start being the piece read from, a
    blow-up the structure getting far richer further on, and "does it end" asking whether the whole is finite. The owner replied:
    "This makes sense, yes." Recorded as the owner's reading of this premise, open for the explosive starts outside that class
    (Intuitions lesson 8.1, "One structure, read outward"). Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-19 · `WALL-READ-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/WALL-READ-20261119-001/wall_read.md)): a blow-up that
    provably never ends exists and is read exactly, the partnered start: each round about two to the power of half the one before.
    A part of one growth, read exactly, bounds its next round from below, a first sampling inside one growth. On the 14 sampled

  - 2026-11-19 · `MERGE-BURST-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/MERGE-BURST-20261119-001/merge_burst.md)): of 16
    explosive starts, 2 are read, and both end after one merge and the binomial burst of one block (9 and 781 junctions). 14 are

  - 2026-11-19 · `EXPLOSIVE-20261119-001` ([decision note](../research/EXPLOSIVE-20261119-001/explosive.md)): the first sampled
    reading of the explosive kind. Under these weightings it is rare: absent within five places (0 of 2,191), 1.6% within seven.

### A111 · A moving pattern is measured against one allowed state

**Confirmed** · 2026-11-19 · reading

- **Statement.** In the single-state motion test, occupied distinctions are differences from a fixed allowed assignment. Renaming distinctions transports this reference with the state.
- **Why.** The motion study compared keeping the reference with allowing every translation of the allowed binary states while forgetting it. Keeping it lets the current shape distinguish its own direction.
- **Rests on it.** The minimal ten-distinction moving pattern in `MOTION-AT-EDDY-20261119-001` uses this reading. The parity growth law itself does not require a preferred assignment.
- **Challenge.** Under all allowed value translations, a deterministic update of one state must add a fixed allowed difference. Iterating it returns after two applications. A marked arrival or differences between multiple states give other readings to test.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-22 · The old reference follows the same paid physical distinctions in both matched histories. Under not-all-equal the reference on a newborn must be extended by an additional supplied convention. The two conventions at A141 give different possible individual responsibility at the same target. [Paid veto](../research/WRITTEN-BIT-AND-JOIN-VETO-20261122-001/paid_veto.md).
  - 2026-11-19 · `RING-TO-END-20261119-001` · The once-loaded ring and all the subsequent active-parent checks are measured against the same transported reference. Erasing only fresh pairs at identical incidence can change second-layer births.
  - 2026-11-19 · `STATE-FEEDS-GROWTH-20261119-001` · The occupied-parent bit uses this transported reference. Jointly changing the allowed assignment and reference preserves growth. Keeping the reference fixed while changing a stored ring can alter the next structure.
  - 2026-11-19 · `MOTION-LAW-20261119-001` uses this reference for both the ring switch and compact motion. Quietness together with covariance under every allowed value shift again selects the identity. No stage-wide reference is inferred from growth.
  - 2026-11-19 · The current-state reading and its alternative were tested in `MOTION-AT-EDDY-20261119-001`. The fixed reference is retained for this class of moving patterns. The translation argument gives its exact scope.

### A112 · The logic comes before space and would sit beneath locality

**Assumed** · 2026-11-19 · interpretation

- **Statement.** The owner's interpretation, in the owner's words (19 November 2026, Pacific time): "This kind of contradicts
  Einstein's theory of locality/space if true, right? Similar to quantum mechanics, the logic is not spatial yet." Read in the
  program's terms: space is not given, nearness is read from the allowed states alone, and the whole can hold relations that no
  pair of distinctions shows, as entangled states do. That does not contradict Einstein's locality, which governs space once
  there is one, provided ordinary locality comes out at large scales; on this reading the logic would sit beneath locality, not
  against it. The likeness to quantum mechanics is one of structure (the literature's GHZ state in Mermin's form is built from
  parity rules), not of physics: the program's knots have definite states, with no probabilities or amplitudes. Stated in the
  Intuitions guide, lesson 8.5 ([lesson](../INTUITIONS.md#85-locality-before-space)).
- **Why.** Recorded at the owner's request, after the project discussion of what pair distances miss (lesson 8.4).
- **Rests on it.** No result. It frames lesson 8.5.
- **Challenge.** It would be challenged if the structure the whole holds beyond pairs were shown to reduce to pair distances
  (the likeness to entanglement would then go), or if no law of this kind can give a largest speed at which a change spreads at
  large scales, so that ordinary locality cannot come out. The cheapest first test is a law that moves a pattern by reading only
  what it shares distinctions with, then asks whether its reach per round is bounded; `EDDY-WORLD-20261119-001`
  ([decision note](../research/EDDY-WORLD-20261119-001/decision.md), referee pending) found patterns that keep their identity and
  stay attached, and travelling state patterns that the growth rule never chooses.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · The owner’s possibility question was tested on ordinary growth. Independently set common-weight readings obey CHSH ≤ 2; a built parity start gives a conditional score of 4 with fair outcomes and a perfectly readable other setting in a complete local pair. The interpretation remains Assumed. [Study](../research/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001/decision.md).
  - 2026-11-19 · Recorded, in the owner's words, as the owner's interpretation; framed by Intuitions lesson 8.5.

### A113 · Fresh distinctions inherit the fewest state changes allowed by each new parity knot

**Assumed** · 2026-11-19 · reading

- **Statement.** In the state-feedback comparison, every old distinction retains its value on growth. Compared with the transported reference A111, a new knot with an even input gets no occupied fresh distinction. An odd input gets one, with each tied choice admitted. A sole implied closure has no fresh distinction.
- **Why.** The parity relation specifies the allowed assignments. The comparison needs a choice for how a current assignment extends to a new knot. The fewest new changes supplies a reproducible, renaming-invariant family of lifts.
- **Rests on it.** The one-layer sensing of a state held only on old shared distinctions and the conservation of occupied loose distinctions under hot-join veto use this extension. Counts at the first birth roster depend only on the current state and action table.
- **Challenge.** Admit a fresh pair of changes on an even input, or a local event that transfers stored changes to the growing end. Test whether old-state information can reach a second layer while preserving parity and giving the same structural outcome for every event order specified by the law.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-22 · On the marked five-founder clock each newborn's incoming reference pair is homogeneous, with an opposite homogeneous fresh reference. Minimum filling then yields a signal fraction of zero on infinitely many actual returns. Releasing that minimum permits every binary signal stream under either law separately. [Signal fractions at a returning receiver](../research/QUIET-RETURNS-AND-SIGNAL-FRACTIONS-20261122-001/returns.md)
  - 2026-11-22 · On every funded quiet prefix in the registered parity and not-all-equal growth laws, the actual old words and every physical binding admit the references of all newborns together. They uniquely minimize deviations there. The pre-write payment history alone cannot exclude that extension. [Payment history and the first write](../research/PAYMENT-HISTORY-AND-FIRST-WRITE-20261122-001/fixed_history.md)
  - 2026-11-22 · With a fully quiet parent and a legal transported reference, zero fresh deviations is the unique minimum. Permitting all legal fresh words on the first complete cohort gives parity alternatives 00 and 11 on the designated writer, and not-all-equal alternatives 00, 01 and 10. A legal choice can initiate a paid veto. Neither law forces the first deviation. [First write](../research/QUIET-START-AND-FIRST-WRITE-20261122-001/first_write.md).
  - 2026-11-22 · The archived minimum fresh deviations write a physical mark on a unary parity newborn when its payment deviates. In a later unary holder the same rule forces a second deviation. Under not-all-equal the choice of the reference extension recorded at A141 changes whether a companion is forced. [Paid veto](../research/WRITTEN-BIT-AND-JOIN-VETO-20261122-001/paid_veto.md).
  - 2026-11-19 · `DETECTION-PACE-20261119-001` · With the minimum lift of shared-only inputs the occupied values stay on the original loop. Plain-path growth changes its distant component first at max(1,d−1), measured structurally.
  - 2026-11-19 · `SAME-SIDE-READING-20261119-001` · With the minimum fresh assignment after a shared-only initial ring load, the first roster change reaches same-side knot counts even though all later loose values are quiet.
  - 2026-11-19 · `RING-TO-END-20261119-001` · When all loose distinctions start quiet, the minimum fresh assignment stays quiet on every later growing end. The separate A115 lift at birth transfers the signal. The original allowed-state projections in each realized structure survive either lift.
  - 2026-11-19 · Adopted and challenged for the closed sixteen-table feedback comparison in `STATE-FEEDS-GROWTH-20261119-001`. A matched control distinguishes an exhausted ring from a state with ten occupied loose distinctions.

### A114 · Growth can read whether any distinction on a joining knot departs from the reference

**Assumed** · 2026-11-19 · reading

- **Statement.** The sixteen-table feedback study gives a proposed group one bit. It is active if any distinction in any parent differs from the transported allowed reference. The bit is read before allocating loose distinctions, for the entire proposed group. Either bit value can select ordinary growth, a veto or one extra or fewer fresh distinctions.
- **Why.** One variable binary reading is enough to distinguish two state preparations. The predicate, the choices it controls and its use of all the parent distinctions are supplied law choices. A111 supplies the reference.
- **Rests on it.** The selection of hot veto among the sixteen tables, matched-holder equivalence and one-layer source effect use this reading. Each structural alternative retains its own evidence class.
- **Challenge.** Compare a count of deviations, a reading sensitive to their arrangement and a reading restricted to the spent inputs on the same prototypes. Two preparations giving identical five active holders and different loose values supply a controlled discriminator. The full local parity sum is already constant.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-22 · Both matched preparations reach the same target proposal. Its full parent holdings permit a one-mark veto under the primary not-all-equal extension and require a two-mark indication on the tested parity histories. The upstream diversion changes which join occurs while preserving the total number of births. [Paid veto](../research/WRITTEN-BIT-AND-JOIN-VETO-20261122-001/paid_veto.md).
  - 2026-11-19 · `DETECTION-PACE-20261119-001` · Across all sixteen candidate actions, the four constant laws ignore all 64 physical words structurally; the twelve variable laws respond to every nonzero word on the registered sources. Equal-weight placements can change the first nearest-end response layer.
  - 2026-11-19 · `SAME-SIDE-READING-20261119-001` · In the all-offset ordinary count family, the two-knot decrease depends on activity at the attachment holder. Equal numbers of loaded distinctions can give different readings. Four-arm ball counts can be nonadditive.
  - 2026-11-19 · `OLD-MARK-GEOMETRY-20261119-001` · For a fully loaded ring the five active holders alone change the subsequent bond distance of the same two old marks. With doubled meetings, the first crossing response counts which two immediate ring neighbors are active. A short same-side pair can still give an equal reading.
  - 2026-11-19 · Adopted in `STATE-FEEDS-GROWTH-20261119-001`. The old ring and a cut with the same four-distinction pulse give the same first extra births. Old loose values held at zero make a spent-input-only reader insensitive to the source ring.

### A115 · A pair of fresh changes may be part of a knot's birth

**Assumed** · 2026-11-19 · reading

- **Statement.** On a hot unary with quiet spent input, a declared feedback law may make two fresh distinctions occupied together when the knot forms. The old values persist. Quiet and odd children keep the minimum extension. The stated guards can restrict the new even pair to a two-place fresh set, or to the children of a parent with two loose distinctions.
- **Why.** An atomic pair is the smallest nonzero even signal at an individual newborn. The program supplied its coupling to the birth, its guard and its comparison up to fresh-name renaming.
- **Rests on it.** With the original five ring distinctions retained, the second layer, at fixed first incidence, can sense the state on its own growing end. The bare hot sector can implement this as independent single-port births. Arbitrary interleaving with an independently optional later deposit has different outcomes.
- **Challenge.** Find a rule preserving the number of occupied distinctions that transfers the original ring's state without continuing to copy it, or prove that the requested symmetry or event freedom forbids it. Requiring unchanged old values and exactly the A113 minimum prevents fresh activity from an even, quiet input.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-19 · Tested in `RING-TO-END-20261119-001` against a delayed deposit, a fixed-incidence erasure and a reader reaching one older holder. The deterministic total-write minimum depends on the symmetries of the start.

### A116 · A midpoint reading counts knots near every middle of a shortest route

**Assumed** · 2026-11-19 · reading

- **Statement.** For two original shared distinctions, give each ordinary holder incidence one hop, include all central nodes of shortest routes, and count a knot once within a chosen radius of this set. The earlier source midpoint supplies a second comparison. Literal bond distance remains the least size of a containing bond minus one.
- **Why.** Several routes can tie in the formed stage. Using all possible midpoints removes dependence on an arbitrary route or on a mark's name.
- **Rests on it.** For one background pair three ordinary mark steps apart, the two-hop neighborhood of all midpoints changes from 14 to 16 knots under loading at the second layer. The same two-hop neighborhood of its original midpoint has nine knots in both arms.
- **Challenge.** Compare each midpoint separately, weight midpoints by the number of shortest routes crossing them, or hold the original midpoint fixed. Equality of ordinary shortest lengths can coexist with different counts under these readings.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-19 · Adopted and compared with the fixed source midpoint in `OLD-MARK-GEOMETRY-20261119-001`. No weighting over all starts or law asserting that one midpoint is physically preferred was introduced.

### A117 · A same-side reading can be tested by its present reach or by the origins of every counted knot

**Assumed** · 2026-11-19 · reading

- **Statement.** On a path with an attached ring, record the source positions of the marked distinctions, whether the current ball reaches an original ring knot, and whether every counted holder has all its original ancestors in the same source component as the marks after deleting that attachment. These are distinct tests. A law making a knot reads its ordinary parent information and the transported state. It does not see these ancestry scores.
- **Why.** A mark-centred reading can encounter a descendant of the ring through a new knot before it reaches any original ring holder. The owner asked whether a distant reading is possible and how confined it is.
- **Rests on it.** The remote seven-hop and bond-radius-seven witnesses in `SAME-SIDE-READING-20261119-001` are observations before the original ring is reached. The ordinary all-age null for balls whose entire ancestry is confined uses the stronger test in both arms. A116 records an earlier, distinct midpoint convention.
- **Challenge.** Ask for a test of confinement from the current unlabelled stage alone, or vary the supported ancestry relation while retaining identical mark positions and present readings. An ancestral certificate used by the investigator need not be available to the observer.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-19 · `DETECTION-PACE-20261119-001` · Requiring any original ancestor beyond d yields first response at ceil(d/2) for d positive. Requiring all such ancestors gives identical regions forever from d=3. The physically clipped observation in A118 supplies another test.
  - 2026-11-19 · Registered with the original-mark, padded-background and two-donor comparisons in `SAME-SIDE-READING-20261119-001`. No weighting over possible starts is assumed.

### A118 · A distant reader can be given only the connected part holding its old mark

**Assumed** · 2026-11-19 · reading

- **Statement.** To test what a distant observer can detect without looking back toward an attachment, one experiment keeps every physical distinction and knot at a specified current separation, shows only the incidences between retained items, and supplies one old mark. An observer may receive its connected part or every retained part. Both choices can be compared to a ball around the mark, or to a region traced from its old holders under A117. A boundary supplies only what is shown.
- **Why.** The answer changes when the observer can count pieces disconnected from the mark, when it is told the missing degree of a boundary knot, or when it can enlarge a ball until it includes descendants near the attachment. Defining the observer specifies which information must actually get far from the load.
- **Rests on it.** The connected and all-parts first detection layers in the 2026-11-19 remote detection study. The start, the shared-only loading, the transported reference and the complete feedback law are separate choices.
- **Challenge.** Let the system identify the remote region without an investigator supplying its boundary. Or give the observer the true degree of each boundary knot and compare first detection layers on the same sources.

Anonymous current-end reception after dropping the original mark is a separate test. [Local observation](../research/INFLUENCE-CONE-20261120-001/signalling.md).

  - 2026-11-20 · GENERATORS-20261120-001 · A full size-cut reader recovers the seven-knot source up to exchange and returns an equivariant family. The live witnessed-end reader can exchange the 21-knot return types, and arbitrary partial writings can hide an original in the smaller source. Recognition and fresh-count independence are separate questions.
  - 2026-11-20 · READ-ORIGIN-20261120-001 · At a fixed display distinguish an attached source-membership flag, a pointer, a binary start/middle answer and anonymous answers up to renaming. The proved independent-site family is rigid at the queried parents. Knowing the original size still leaves a linear worst case. On an unbounded restricted family the source has five places at every original and ordinary growth produces only four-place knots.
  - 2026-11-20 · SERIAL-REPAIRS-20261120-001 · The present-only raw reader here sees actual holders, loose positions, and current incidences around all proposed parents. It receives neither a supplied map nor dates. Repeated source meetings intrinsically mark the originals on these sources. Treat intrinsic source recovery, weak fairness before versus after strict priority, and the choice to impose a whole-stage depth guard as separate scaffolds. Test replacements on the identical eight-knot restart and the unit-meeting outside source.

A witnessed step reader also receives common-holder rosters and their live sizes. The two instruments can differ even at a fixed fully specified parent display. Fairness for a fixed event map, fairness for current enabled moves, and a predetermined sibling roster are separate scaffolds.

Removing it leaves closure only within the component of those parents.
