Providing all live parts, or providing the exhaustion bit with a sufficiently large local ball, restores it. The new observer uses anonymous shared-parent steps, named rooted patterns, a physical source pairing and no old mark.
  - 2026-11-19 · The supplied cutoff, old mark and induced boundary are measurement scaffolds. Swapping the connected part for every retained part changes the path onset by one layer for d at least three. An intrinsic observer would need to identify its location and supported region within the stage.
  - 2026-11-19 · `DETECTION-PACE-20261119-001` · The path calculation distinguishes the connected part at max(1,d−1), the full exterior at max(1,d−2), and the arbitrary-radius rooted ball at one. A future intrinsic construction would have to identify the attachment and boundary from the stage.
  - 2026-11-19 · Registered the present-separation observers and their comparison with old ancestry. [Detection study](https://github.com/jscul/knotlab/blob/main/research/DETECTION-PACE-20261119-001/definitions.md)

### A119 · A setting can name which of two marked distinctions to read

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Select the first or second marked distinction and report its value. The external protocol uses the same distribution of source states for each selection. The embodied alternative reads a third mark as the setting.
- **Why.** To compare a setting varied independently with a setting already recorded in an allowed state.
- **Rests on it.** This is one observer protocol in the Bell study. [Study definitions](../research/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Compare the pair-pattern reading and actual selector conditioning with independent re-reading on the same states. Ordinary growth presently supplies neither a choice of observable nor a measurement operation.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · On the built parity start, varying the indices gives CHSH 2; sorting by the observed settings gives 4 with each selected bit fair. The full Alice pair identifies the other selector.
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the comparison of separated readings.

### A120 · A setting can name a two-bit pattern to test

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Use the setting to name 00 or 11, and report whether the two local distinctions have that pattern. This requires both values.
- **Why.** To test a different two-valued reading with the same native distinctions.
- **Rests on it.** The pair-pattern correlations depend on this choice of question. [Study definitions](../research/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Compare with reading one distinction. A future native reading operation could specify its own test; neither question follows from ordinary growth.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · The two-mark pattern can exhibit correlations hidden from each single-mark reading. In the built parity example the relabelled embodied score is 4, with a 1/2 change in Alice’s reported marginal under uniform weights.
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the comparison of separated readings.

### A121 · Separation for a reading counts steps between all its marked distinctions

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Each local group of marks lies in an actual bond. Compare a minimum of two co-knotted steps between the two groups with three and with having no chain. Include both selectors when they supply the indices. Compare a selected-bit marginal with the entire marked pair.
- **Why.** To say in intrinsic terms what the two observers can read.
- **Rests on it.** The separation and the operational interpretation of the reported marginal tests. [Study definitions](../research/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Vary the step threshold and visible register; markings and a stable ordering for allocating and naming indistinguishable places are scaffolds. Growth supplies the meeting pattern but has no designated observers or marking rule.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · The cohort includes 366 placements two steps apart, 316 at least three finite steps apart, and 12 without a chain. In the built parity case the closest marks are three steps apart, yet the whole Alice pair reveals the other selector.
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the comparison of separated readings.

### A122 · A Bell reading uses a declared weighting of the states

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Compare equal weight for each current state, equal weight for each initial state divided over extensions, and double current weight when one marked distinction is one. Each external setting uses the same weighting.
- **Why.** Support preservation does not prescribe how much an extension counts.
- **Rests on it.** The numerical four-context tables and whether conditional marginals hide a setting. [Study definitions](../research/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Repeat with all three weightings. A future native rule would have to derive weights and their behavior under extension, as well as how settings are supplied.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-21 · On ordinary parity growth the three named ways of distributing weight agree from a uniform start. On a not-all-equal source they can give different old marked probabilities and different fixed-order probabilities on the same support. A positive coherent source and the dephased control can also share all the same ordinary probabilities yet give different sign capacities. [The unread companion and three ordinary weightings](../research/UNREAD-COMPANION-AND-ORDER-FREE-WEIGHTING-20261121-001/decision.md)
  - 2026-11-20 · Under not-all-equal the uniform grown weighting changes the distribution on retained old marks in 221 of 233 grown placements. The equally lifted weighting preserves it in all 233. All common weights obey the external-settings bound.
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the comparison of separated readings.

### A123 · Built and labelled sampled starts supply a Bell comparison

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Compare smallest built starts, a built connected control with two relays, labelled sampled trees in fixed size strata with matched extra loose distinctions, and three literal starts selected from the archived explosive panel. The selected markings are fixed without reading outcomes.
- **Why.** To distinguish a construction that can happen from its frequency under a named weighting.
- **Rests on it.** Only the bounded samples and measured maxima use these source, marking and label choices. [Study definitions](../research/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Swap the built and sampled families, the two knot laws, old and newborn readings and the separation bands. A sampling convention stays with the observer until a weighting is supplied by the model.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · No ordinary placement exceeded 2 on the 33-source panel, including the three archived jumping starts. A separate deliberately built ten-knot start produced a conditional parity excess. These have different source denominators.
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the comparison of separated readings.

### A124 · One context can be given a changed outcome rule

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** For a fair shared bit, switch the second outcome only when both setting labels are one. Also test a biased bit, no switch and a partial switch. Count the number of altered contexts relative to four identical rules.
- **Why.** This gives a sharp comparison for the precise addition sufficient to exceed the classical bound.
- **Rests on it.** Only the explicit added-rule control and its one-context minimality use this scaffold. [Study definitions](../research/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Remove the switch, vary the mask and check both marginals. A future model would need to derive the dependence and preserve marginals itself; the ordinary join law has no such pair-of-settings input.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · The partial switch yields CHSH 2+2t under a fair shared bit; bias can expose the dependence in one observer’s reported outcome. A different demonstrator weights the actual settings and their joint outcome five to one on two unlinked parity knots. It reproduces a quantum-compatible table at 8/3; uniform weighting there gives zero. Both context-sensitive changes are supplied. [Study](../research/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001/decision.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the comparison of separated readings.

### A125 · Independent products can be read by their parents

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Recognize a product as a finite set of newly born knots whose later members take every parent from the preceding members of that same product. Require infinitely many disjoint living releases for a generator. Permit extra mixed births outside each product. Read from complete present holdings with no names or growth log. The chosen source, product language and release sizes may be unbounded in the reading.
- **Why.** The generator study used this test to make the owner's question about separate lasting things falsifiable. The requirement that every parent stays inside excludes a descendant that needs its source at each step.
- **Rests on it.** Interpreting the proved periodic and nested families as things produced by the source. The physical births, their inputs and the matched fresh counts follow from the ordinary law independently of that interpretation.
- **Challenge.** Require independent replay of all outgoing births and loose counts, a fixed packet shape, or a reader restricted to the growing end. A product that passes one of these stronger tests could replace the corresponding present scaffold. The current periodic examples change in isolated replay and the symmetric end misses the designated pair.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-22 · The indirect child-count study supplies the founding payer rosters, spent witnesses, two launch packets and their enrollment by actual earlier payments. A different prepared connection diverts one donor payment and its inverse restores it. Count every funded direct child of the enrolled receiver member, including mixed children. Compare arbitrary payment bindings, lawful values, renamed roles and the requirement of continuing descendants through the counted member. Recovering the enrolled parties without the supplied founder roles would replace part of this reading. [Reviewed design](../research/INDIRECT-ENCOUNTER-AND-CHILD-COUNT-20261122-001A/preregistration.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · At 16 parent steps the full and growing-end readings agree on anonymously counted releases and direct meetings under both labelled-start weightings. Donor admissibility can still require the earlier physical holdings. [Rates and interventions](../research/FOUR-READINGS-20261120-001D/results.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · COPIES-AND-MEETINGS-20261120-001 · The all-parent reading gives a finite bound on identified products at any fixed launch width. An inclusive-only singleton can instead be selected without end and requires an outside parent. [Physical and alternative readings](../research/COPIES-AND-MEETINGS-20261120-001/bounded.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · Registered for the [ordinary generator study](../research/GENERATORS-20261120-001/preregistration.md). Tested both knot laws, full and growing-end readings, any-parent and all-parent ancestry, and the [matched physical preparations](../research/GENERATORS-20261120-001/meeting.md). The stronger isolation requirement and recognition of fixed copies stay open.


### A126 · Growth never forbids what the old knots allowed

**Under challenge** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** The cheapest growth (A104, `FRESH-COUNT-20261118-001`) is written so that every state the old knots allow can
  still be completed into an allowed state after a round: each new knot either brings fresh distinctions that can satisfy it or is
  implied by the old knots, so a new knot never forbids a combination the old knots permitted. This was built into the law, not
  found. Its named rival is **joining can forbid**: a law under which joining knots may rule out some old combinations.
- **Why.** Taken by default with the joining rule of the cubic study and the cheapest counts; it keeps every round an extension of
  the base, never a rewrite of it.
- **Rests on it.** The ordinary proof that every starting assignment has a continuation, the ordinary funding counts and the identified births use this postulate. For the free-setting Bell bound, the [refereed reading proof](../proofs/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001A/ENTANGLEMENT-READ-20261120-001A-P001_common_state/proof.md) needs only a common distribution on the four prospective answers. A setting-blind join may delete old states while preserving that common explanation.
- **Challenge.** Goal G18 sets rival laws against this one on the same questions. The first test (backlog B229): the simplest law
  under which joining can forbid, asked whether its structures survive or end with nothing allowed, then the entanglement test on
  what survives. The other rivals: merging what the allowed states prove tied, state feeding growth (B210), and a different knot
  rule under the same growth (parity against not-all-equal). As a scaffold of the current studies, the model is expected to
  settle which law it uses, or to show which results hold under every reasonable law (G18).
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · Localizing the spent-pair permission retains extension of every old word on a funded cut under both knot laws. When a distant donor is one loose name short the complete cut can end while all focal permissions and funds persist. [Join study](../research/NEWEST-KNOTS-AND-LOCAL-JOINS-20261120-001B/results.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · The rule allowing two spent knots to authorize a paid birth preserves every old word under either knot law on every funded cut. A funding veto leaves the old presentation intact. [Paid join](../research/LOOSE-NAMES-AND-SPENT-JOINS-20261120-001B/results.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · The two rival join choices, adding a direct live pair and including a live witness, preserve every old allowed word and its full local knot projections under both laws. Each funded nonclosing birth has its own fresh completion. A veto retains all prior words. Both rivals nevertheless veto the funded seven-knot recurring source. State extension alone does not decide which join to use. [Extension proof and costs](../research/STATE-DISTANCE-AND-REACHED-GROWTH-20261120-001A/rival_joins.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · Under challenge as a choice of growth law. Two explicit rivals delete old states while retaining a common explanation for all free readings. Ordinary extension remains proved within the ordinary law. [Competing postulates](../research/FORBIDDING-JOIN-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded at the owner's request with goal G18, naming "joining can forbid" as its rival.

### A127 · A change in later output can be read through a common descendant

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** To count an indirect effect of one continuing product on the output of another, require actual parental paths from both to a shared later birth, and a matched physical start in which the shared output disappears or its number of fresh distinctions changes. Separately score an immediate child of a current product member, a shared descendant farther along, and changes to the product’s own continuing members.
- **Why.** A common past can give two marked parties similar futures without any later birth requiring them both. Distinguishing which output changes makes a test of the owner’s question possible.
- **Rests on it.** Calling the witnessed delayed births an interaction between products. The existence of the births, their true parents, their counts and which complete preparations support them follow from ordinary growth.
- **Challenge.** Require both parties to survive in both preparations with the same receiver at launch, require the number of its immediate children to change, or require its own pure production to change. Compare the complete present reading with the growing end alone.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-22 · The enrolled separation-five source pair supplies an extra loose distinction at X4, marks two actual releases and their first common newborn, and compares its anonymous holding at size eight with its actual spending at size nine. The eligible group and the extra grant have the same older cause. A payment from the newborn is physically required, while any of its fresh distinctions can fill that role. Both prepared starts and every same-prefix allocation are compared under both laws. [What the common newborn pays](../research/SPARE-AND-NEXT-PAYMENT-20261122-001/payment_trace.md)
  - 2026-11-20 · INFLUENCE-CONE-20261120-001 · Ancestry-based influence and an across-start difference in a matched birth have distinct sharp bounds. Changing a witness can affect the fresh count of a receiver descendant one step before any common descendant exists. The earlier definition remains a stricter scaffold. [Matched edit theorem](../research/INFLUENCE-CONE-20261120-001/fixed_edits.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · COPIES-AND-MEETINGS-20261120-001 · The matched single-holding intervention changes a pure parent’s actual child price by one. A connected split-return preparation retains both releases and removes the mixed child. A one-shared-distinction comparison also preserves both releases and changes later joint output. [Matched comparisons](../research/COPIES-AND-MEETINGS-20261120-001/indirect.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · Registered in the [physical comparison of released products](../research/COPIES-AND-MEETINGS-20261120-001/preregistration.md) and its [later-meeting check](../research/COPIES-AND-MEETINGS-20261120-001/late_test.md). Both knot laws are tested.

### A128 · The growth law comes before the logic of the knots

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · interpretation

- **Statement.** The owner's interpretation, in the owner's words (2026-11-20), of the law that grows knot patterns: it "can be
  thought of as 'the thing that comes before logic'. Unless the law itself can be derived through logic somehow (which seems like
  it'd be difficult to test)." Read in the program's terms, two branches: the growth law is prior to the logic of the knots,
  given before any knot's rule acts; or it follows from that logic, so that how a knot joins is decided by what it is made of and
  there is no separate law. The second branch is the third strength of the several-laws lead (backlog B232).
- **Why.** Recorded at the owner's request with goal G18 (comparing growth laws) and lead B232.
- **Rests on it.** No result. It frames G18 and B232.
- **Challenge.** The second branch would be shown by a growth law derived from the knots' own rules, with every rival law
  collapsing into it or turning out to be the same law in disguise (G18); the first branch is supported, not proved, if rival
  laws stay distinct on the same knots. As the owner notes, a derivation may be hard to test.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · The bounded and whole-holdings paid rules preserve the same full histories on every tree source and the two named recurring sources, but give a funded 18-fresh common child versus permanent separation on one edited preparation. These inputs do not determine the supplied participation policy. [Join study](../research/NEWEST-KNOTS-AND-LOCAL-JOINS-20261120-001B/results.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · The two named word weightings fix the complete next birth roster on one wider reached example given ordinary growth. The same holdings admit a separately supplied paid join. The extension property and both recurring sources survive this change, and the marked ruler fails to decide arrival under both choices. [Pair readings](../research/LOOSE-NAMES-AND-SPENT-JOINS-20261120-001A/results.md). [Paid join](../research/LOOSE-NAMES-AND-SPENT-JOINS-20261120-001B/results.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · With ordinary growth, marked distances of two holders do not determine their arrival. Both supplied alternatives fit a narrower funded all-live tree reading but fail on equal marked state data at two versus infinitely many parent steps. The full named not-all-equal relation gives each rule’s witnessed partition in the prior reconstruction class, conditional on the supplied rule. This gives a criterion for a law, and leaves its logical derivation open. [Comparison of rules](../research/STATE-DISTANCE-AND-REACHED-GROWTH-20261120-001A/rival_joins.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · The finite state-only proof recovers the arguments of the ordinary not-all-equal growth law, including across repeated meetings. The ordinary extension and its cost are still supplied. It remains open whether logic alone requires this extension. Under parity full states can leave the arguments of even this specified law ambiguous. [Study](../research/THREE-READERS-AND-GROWTH-20261120-001B/results.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · A dedicated comparison tests whether full states determine the holders and therefore the input to the ordinary growth rule. A derivation of the rule from the states would require ruling out alternative laws as well. [Study](../research/THREE-READERS-AND-GROWTH-20261120-001B/preregistration.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded, in the owner's words, as the owner's interpretation; framed by G18 and B232.

### A129 · A join may receive one fewer fresh distinction

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** At every funded birth, remove one from the ordinary fresh count when doing so respects its three-place floor. Preserve an ordinary implied zero. Impose the actual relation even when an old state loses its last extension.
- **Why.** A one-unit shortage is the first uniform change of the fresh allowance with the ordinary proposal group, input map and law at each birth.
- **Rests on it.** The supply, stalls, products and retained states measured under this rival law. [Study definition](../research/FORBIDDING-JOIN-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Compare the arity-floor and ordinary laws on the identical starting structures. A future law would need to explain the allowance in its own terms.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · Added in the first G18 comparison of laws under which joining can forbid. Results are referee pending.

### A130 · A join may receive only the fresh distinctions required by its arity

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** At every funded birth supply only enough fresh distinctions to reach three places, capped by the ordinary count. Preserve the ordinary implied zero. Impose the full relation on the actual inputs.
- **Why.** This is the other end of the same finite choice at each birth. It can impose the relation directly on three or more old distinctions.
- **Rests on it.** The supply, stalls, products and retained states measured under the arity-floor law. [Study definition](../research/FORBIDDING-JOIN-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Compare the one-fewer and ordinary grants on the same starts, including births where the three grants differ. The ordinary future birth map needs to be tested again.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · Added in the first G18 comparison of laws under which joining can forbid. Results are referee pending.

### A131 · Starting weights can be restricted once to states that still have extensions

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** If some starting assignment has no extension, report the inherited equal-start/equal-extension weighting as undefined. In a separate comparison give equal weight to the starting states with an extension and split each of their weights equally over its completions, using the identical set for all free settings.
- **Why.** This makes the loss of starting mass visible while permitting a second defined reading of the surviving relation.
- **Rests on it.** Only the separately named conditional-weight comparisons. The common-state Bell bound holds for every defined common nonnegative weighting. [Study definition](../research/FORBIDDING-JOIN-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Compare complete-state weights, the one-sided tilt, and the literal inherited rule. Report the original denominator. A filtering rule that reads the settings is a different experiment.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · Added in the first G18 comparison of laws under which joining can forbid. Results are referee pending.

### A132 · One freely supplied setting pair can change how an output pair is formed

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** As a control, vary both settings independently of three fair preparation bits. Supply a different output rule only at 00. An alternative retains half the preparations at 00 and reports its retention fraction.
- **Why.** Counting changed contexts makes the least additional resource test precise and separates a total response from discarding trials.
- **Rests on it.** The added 5/2 table and its equal full-pair marginals. The earlier A124 supplies another one-context comparison. [Study definition](../research/FORBIDDING-JOIN-20261120-001/setting_dependent_control.md).
- **Challenge.** Suppress the change, try the pair-pattern reading, or bias the shared bit. A native explanation would have to produce the dependence on both settings as well as the stated weighting.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · Added in the first G18 comparison of laws under which joining can forbid. Results are referee pending.
  - 2026-11-20 · Tested in G18 B233. Ordinary all-or-none funding supplies a dependence on two actual edits on a prepared source. Four fixed addresses can hide the signal, while using the visible local shortage to select an address transmits a message. The earlier 5/2 calibration remains a supplied response. [Frozen design](../research/WHOLE-STAGE-SETTINGS-20261120-001/preregistration.md).

### A133 · Signalling can be tested by two distinguishable local births

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Compare two matched lawful starts that differ in a declared sender preparation. A passive receiver can read one binary choice when a formed local knot in each arm gives a distinguishable observation. Report separately whether a reader is instead handed a completed stop, a global projection, a mark identifying the receiver, or a schedule. Charge a physical edit both to its written holder and to its changed loose or witnessed data.
- **Why.** Detecting the sender setting in the receiver's holdings is a different question from whether a descendant receives actual inputs from both families. A lack of births can also be ambiguous for a receiver with no completion signal.
- **Rests on it.** The operational interpretation of the matched three- versus four-place receiver knot as a one-bit signal. Its true parents, number of holdings, closure, and conditional local bounds follow from the ordinary law independently of the interpretation.
- **Challenge.** Hide all original marks and spent holders, demand a receiver that continues to read after either symbol, or require a sender able to change the setting inside one unaltered growth. Repeat under the other knot law and with steps restricted to exact two-parent groups.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · INFLUENCE-CONE-20261120-001 · Source-level one-incidence, fixed positive input, projected-state and silent-stall readings are separately tested. Only positive events at the receiver give the demonstrated clock-free two-symbol readout in both arms. [Guards and signalling](../research/INFLUENCE-CONE-20261120-001/signalling.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · The [influence study](../research/INFLUENCE-CONE-20261120-001/preregistration.md) compares these readings and separates shared descent from a counterfactual edit to initial holdings.

### A134 · A free local edit may supply one extra loose distinction

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Keep a marked holder live and append zero or one loose distinction. Read whether a persistent private distinction gains a second holder in the funded round. Both parties' edit sites remain separate.
- **Why.** A native funding failure has an ordinary local consequence even when the two parties have no common child.
- **Rests on it.** The smallest two-sided funding test and its deterministic four tables. [Frozen definitions](../research/WHOLE-STAGE-SETTINGS-20261120-001/preregistration.md).
- **Challenge.** Use the addressed edit, transport the names of the offered distinctions, and separate the growing ends further.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the ordinary two-setting study, G18. Independent referee pending.

### A135 · Closing can be read at the holder of a persistent input

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Append zero or one spare at a persistent parent, and zero or one at a separate spent holder. Read whether the fixed input's child has zero fresh distinctions and whether the other holder's optional mark gained a holder.
- **Why.** This reads the native closing exception on all trials, including an absent birth.
- **Rests on it.** The minimum two-parent and three-parent closing witnesses. The two-place ordinary exception is reported separately. [Frozen definitions](../research/WHOLE-STAGE-SETTINGS-20261120-001/preregistration.md).

- **History.**
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the ordinary two-setting study, G18. Independent referee pending.

### A136 · A free setting may name which of two local holders receives a spare

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Supply one loose distinction to the holder named by the address. The binary result is one precisely when a designated old distinction is one and the detector's persistent loose distinction gained a holder. A stall gives zero.
- **Why.** The same local predicate can be used on all four settings and every trial has a result.
- **Rests on it.** The addressed whole-stage funding comparison. The observer supplies the setting and the reading. [Frozen definitions](../research/WHOLE-STAGE-SETTINGS-20261120-001/preregistration.md).

  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the ordinary two-setting study, G18. Independent referee pending.

### A137 · Independent preparation choices may set the ordinary connections between detector marks

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Draw two independent fair readiness labels before either free setting. Each label marks its own local funding deficit and controls two predeclared interchanges of three ordinary connections. Use uniform allowed states within each preparation.
- **Why.** A repeated pair of independent interchanges generates different mark correlations without a rule that reads the two actual settings.
- **Rests on it.** The all-trial Bell excess in the concealed, balanced mixture. Ordinary growth neither selects these starting presentations nor assigns their weights. [Frozen definitions](../research/WHOLE-STAGE-SETTINGS-20261120-001/preregistration.md).

### A138 · A local detector sees both edit sites and a fixed range of connections

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Include all accessible local values and incidences before and after the free edit, both address holders, the complete three terminal bits, the structural birth flag and the specified fixed connections. Do not give the detector the hidden source choice for the other party.
- **Why.** Equality of a selected bit's marginal alone can miss a locally readable signal.

The full local preparation can hide the deficit and still support a signal from an old mark. [Frozen study](../research/LOCAL-EDIT-POLICIES-20261123-001/preregistration.md).
  - 2026-11-20 · Recorded for the ordinary two-setting study, G18. Independent referee pending.

### A139 · An added loose distinction can carry the pre-settings state through a local edit

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Sample a complete state before the two settings. For parity an appended place can be zero in every trial, or a local fair split can change a private old bit and the new bit together. ### A144 · A reader can measure a change, with chances given by squared weights

**Assumed** · 2026-11-20 · scaffold

The score is 3 against 2 and all one-reader chances are independent of the other choices. All finite parity structures admit a common list for individual read-or-change questions. The two-place reader may read and retain the common value to free the other two bits. At the one-place reader the same physical value prevents an entangling return. [The unread companion and three ordinary weightings](../research/UNREAD-COMPANION-AND-ORDER-FREE-WEIGHTING-20261121-001/decision.md)
  - 2026-11-21 · Varied the original law, full and partial dephasing, safety on actually reached words versus on arbitrary ancillary inputs, measurement versus a guessed label and a synchronized joint offer. The separate parity optimum is zero, the separate not-all-equal optimum is 1/3 and the synchronized parity construction succeeds with certainty. This concerns the supplied coherent preparations and operational access. [The split-knot meter and the three closing signs](../research/SPLIT-KNOT-METER-AND-CLOSING-TRIPLE-20261121-001/decision.md)
  - 2026-11-21 · Registered prospectively for the two studies. [Design and decision](../research/SPLIT-KNOT-METER-AND-CLOSING-TRIPLE-20261121-001/decision.md)

### A183 · A complete three-sign reading at two retained keeper banks

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Each sign is to be delivered to both original readers and both data registers returned with their entire projected state, including correlations with untouched spectators. Charge for every shared coherent pair, message, source port and writable keeper slot.
- **Why.** A joint sign becomes an ordinary report only when its words and their validity can be recovered. Merely computing the three effects does not determine the returned state.
- **Rests on it.** The minimum coherent resources for the complete closing-knot reading.
- **Challenge.** Compare one, two and three fresh pairs and distinguish inferring a sign, measuring it again, and rereading its saved report.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-21 · Varied the number of shared coherent pairs, source and meter records, the presence and direction of messages, the order of local offers, and which parts of a damaged keeper a reader can access. The complete closing reading costs two pairs. A third pair verifies the already determined third sign. Each bank needs the other reader's two messages for a local complete record. [The split-knot meter and the three closing signs](../research/SPLIT-KNOT-METER-AND-CLOSING-TRIPLE-20261121-001/decision.md)
  - 2026-11-21 · Registered prospectively for the two studies. [Design and decision](../research/SPLIT-KNOT-METER-AND-CLOSING-TRIPLE-20261121-001/decision.md)

### A184 · An unread companion stays in the holding throughout a split-knot read

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Use the positive coherent state or diagonal state on the complete holding of two triples sharing one distinction. The readers may access only the first triple and their private registers. Every individual change must preserve both knots on reached histories.
- **Why.** An unread distinction can still preserve a physical record of alternatives.
- **Rests on it.** The per-launch capacity to supply both exact sign projections from this joined holding.
- **Challenge.** Vary the placement, both knot laws, coherent and diagonal sources, and state what resolving an unread word adds.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-21 · With two names shared instead of one, preserving both physical projections on every reached word gives zero exact single-copy success in either placement. For split not-all-equal names, permission to rely on coherent cancellation gives an explicit complete return at (7−√13)/90 per launch. [Spent pasts and two shared names](../research/SPENT-PASTS-AND-TWO-SHARED-NAMES-20261121-001/decision.md)
  - 2026-11-21 · Compared both placements of the shared name, all four pairings of the ordinary laws, positive and fully dephased preparations, and the effect of giving the readers an additional observation on the companion. With the companion inaccessible the coherent not-all-equal optimum changes from zero to 1−√5/3 depending on placement. A fully projecting unread holding attached through that one name gives the same bound when each value has an exclusive unread completion. [The unread companion and three ordinary weightings](../research/UNREAD-COMPANION-AND-ORDER-FREE-WEIGHTING-20261121-001/decision.md)
  - 2026-11-21 · Declared before the two studies ran. [Design and decision](../research/UNREAD-COMPANION-AND-ORDER-FREE-WEIGHTING-20261121-001/decision.md)

### A185 · Compare three ordinary weightings on the same lawful support and growth history

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** At a stage compare uniform present words with fair branching on each newly revealed binary distinction whenever both values have a full lawful extension. Across a fixed growth event compare uniform present words and uniform sharing among a parent's lawful extensions, beginning from a stated weighting.
- **Why.** A local coin, present-state counting and preserving an old word's chance express distinct choices.
- **Rests on it.** Any claim that a binary law forces agreement of the three weighting procedures.
- **Challenge.** Test every reveal order, nonuniform starting weights, nonconstant extension counts, the canonical knot test, and both ordinary knot laws.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-22 · For the quiet-founder comparison, each permitted fresh bank gets a conditionally uniform word at its actual birth, independently on the reached roster. Exact first and later probabilities use products of these conditional weights. Under all-lawful filling the entirely quiet three-cut path has mass 1/8 for parity, 1/108 for primary not-all-equal and 1/216 for its alternative. A weighting is supplied independently of lawful support. [First write](../research/QUIET-START-AND-FIRST-WRITE-20261122-001/first_write.md).
  - 2026-11-22 · The stored-bit comparison uses uniform draws on each newborn’s allowed fresh words at a fixed complete old input. With two other paid zeros, not-all-equal changes the chance of the fresh word 00 from zero to 1/4 and of a later zero payment from 1/3 to 1/2. The same complete physical payments remain. No weighting is put on the input backgrounds. [The value on a real payment](../research/STORED-BIT-AND-NEXT-ACTION-20261122-001/paid_bit.md)
  - 2026-11-21 · Equal old extension counts control full ordinary reweighting. When histories are added as coherent alternatives, distinct old rows reaching the same new word can also change a retained spectator’s weight. The unary not-all-equal example has three extensions of either input and two shared outputs. [Spent pasts and two shared names](../research/SPENT-PASTS-AND-TWO-SHARED-NAMES-20261121-001/decision.md)
  - 2026-11-21 · Established the all-size, every-order affine classification with the uniform initial prior and the separate condition at each onto growth event. A fixed order, randomized order, nonuniform prior, attributed histories or unsupported old word can have a different verdict. The not-all-equal family changes the old pair under present-uniform weights while transport preserves it. Feasibility queries and count-biased coins require different information. [The unread companion and three ordinary weightings](../research/UNREAD-COMPANION-AND-ORDER-FREE-WEIGHTING-20261121-001/decision.md)
  - 2026-11-21 · Declared before the two studies ran. [Design and decision](../research/UNREAD-COMPANION-AND-ORDER-FREE-WEIGHTING-20261121-001/decision.md)

Another assigns each completion the same positive amplitude, removes that record and squares their sum. A supplied three-reader measurement may ask for a value or a change.

### A187 · Require each reader's offer to preserve both physical knots on every reached word

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Supply a four-name starting holding of two ternaries with two readable names shared by identity. The companion-only name stays unread. The readers may make serial local offers with private work, measured outcomes and classical messages. Every offered transformation must obey both knots on each reached physical word. Compare this with requiring lawfulness on the prepared coherent state on each branch.

The explicit changed-rule return weighs 1/45 under the first calibration and (7−√13)/90 after rescaling the same filter. [Spent pasts and two shared names](../research/SPENT-PASTS-AND-TWO-SHARED-NAMES-20261121-001/decision.md)

### A188 · Keep the other newborn records unread when testing three readers

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** On a positive-age knot with at least three loose distinctions, give one to each reader. Leave every other loose value unread. Each reader independently chooses the ordinary value or its calibrated change. With the retained or clean preparation fixed before those choices, test all eight settings against one distribution of six predetermined answers.

With one fixed ordinary sibling name unavailable, no announcement reaches 1/28 on the recorded orientation inequality. All nine loose names suffice. [Sibling announcements and receiver births](../research/SIBLING-ANNOUNCEMENTS-AND-RECEIVER-BIRTHS-20261121-001/decision.md)

The first not-all-equal child instead violates a probability bound by 1/14 conditional on either named paid value (each occurs on half the launches). Announcing one uniform nine-bit sibling word at fixed orientation also gives this violation. Pooling opposite orientations restores the common explanation. [Newborn tables and three-donor returns](../research/NEWBORN-TABLES-AND-THREE-DONOR-RETURNS-20261121-001/decision.md)

### A189 · Compare a current third at a return with a three-donor founding group

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Supply a founder who can pay with two other donors at the marked receiver return, its two private sender pulses, the receiver's own earlier mark and a third family address declared independently of that return. To test the earlier physical match, retain all names on all occupied original donors, including those shared with another holder. Forget precisely the remaining names.

Check a return accepting an extra original donor against a reader requiring the old mark alone.

The full donor/parent/grant record and joint states on the earlier 262 names match. The hidden spent support exchanges a marked payment with an unmarked one. Crossing third eligibility at fixed contacts leaves the counts unchanged, and the single-addition mark counts one in every full comparison. ### A190 · Announce a fixed newborn inequality through measured sibling words

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Use one actual first child of the homogeneous four-place source, one orientation and the ordinary-or-change inequality on its three loose names. Set the excess threshold at 1/28. Before any setting use a diagonal announcement on the nine other loose names. Price deterministic predicates separately from flags supplied with independent randomness. Include the whole clean instrument's launch cost.

Under all-plus clean erasure it raises them by factors 113/14 and 61/18. Under full access to all erasure signs the nine-ordinary comparator gives 20271/537824 and 39120/1034831. [Sibling announcements and paid-value keepers](../research/SIBLING-CHANGE-ANNOUNCEMENTS-20261121-001/decision.md)

A general independent coin can include part of a boundary word. Deterministic flags retain 1/14 excess, while the best randomized flags give 1/28. One supplied sibling change read reaches excess 2/53 on 53/537824 full launches. [Sibling announcements and receiver births](../research/SIBLING-ANNOUNCEMENTS-AND-RECEIVER-BIRTHS-20261121-001/decision.md)

### A191 · Require a keeper’s answer while both focal questions are still available

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** A living receiver records its actual paid births, parents, fresh distinctions and funding failures. Test a fuller reader with ordinary access to named new values and particular paid inputs. For a reusable orientation-only write, require a real recorded outcome before the setting choice, an intact seven-word preparation, and the two focal questions still accessible on that same reached cut or transported by a demonstrated lawful operation.

  - 2026-11-21 · A reusable receiver word can be written at the same reached cut under supplied guarded motions with an added ready apparatus, while every focal distinction stays loose. The fixed original ordinary births do not provide that apparatus. Tests retain both true values and all 14 allowed focal cases. [Sibling announcements and paid-value keepers](../research/SIBLING-CHANGE-ANNOUNCEMENTS-20261121-001/decision.md)
  - 2026-11-21 · At the first cut the focal paid value and three loose target names coexist. On the cut paying the pairs, the old targets become twice-held. The common-stage requirement therefore distinguishes immediate paid-name access and later statistical inference from an independently written flag. Full future new values with up to two prior payment pairs never certify the orientation in the frozen class. **Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

Use one public go or decline announcement before the independent focal settings. Charge every launch, including source refusal. Keep a literal predicate and a general privately randomized predicate separate.

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Use the same reached child, fixed orientation, eight ordinary sibling questions and one calibrated sibling change question as A192. ### A194 · Send the erasure message before the sibling record is known

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** In one independently launched copy, an eraser sees only its four genuine signs and issues one message chosen by a rule fixed before the launch. The writer gets this message and the sibling answers, then sends one binary announcement before the three focal settings. Charge the largest number of distinguishable eraser messages, including any silence or refusal a receiver can distinguish, and report its fixed-length binary size. Keep a fixed deterministic encoder, a seedless writer, private coins and shared coins distinct.

- **Challenge.** Compare all pairs of true sign records that an encoder might merge, multiple optimal flags, noisy encoders or shared independent random choices, and a diagnostic with feedback. Count every launch. The original argument must specify whether it applies to a fixed deterministic encoding, an encoder with private noise, or a protocol with shared randomness.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-21 · Among all encoders on the 16 actual erasure signs and all decoders with the side record, a best 15-symbol encoding gives 51935/1075648 for a seedless writer and 657035/13599264 with a writer coin or shared lottery. Both full rates require all 16 symbols. If only the eraser has private noise, the full randomized rate requires exactly 17. These are fixed-length, one-launch message costs, including distinguishable refusals. [Sibling announcements and paid-value keepers](../research/SIBLING-CHANGE-ANNOUNCEMENTS-20261121-001/decision.md)

### A196 · Finite intrinsic readings for recognizing four recorded constructions

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Give each recorded construction and each selected root equal weight. Estimate dimension using the number of distinctions within a given number of meeting steps. Read a local three-order pattern from how neighboring knots meet, and test the two candidate limiting shapes using four-point comparisons and the error of a two-dimensional distance fit over finite samples. Record a bounded bond only on a pair with a sound witness.

### A197 · Count newly reproduced later behaviors under a charged search

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** Compare independent lawful starts with a search that retains distinct early readings and edits its starts. Judge both by later changes in births and parent counts that were absent from an independent reference sample and recur on different starting holdings. ### A198 · Give a moving reader rations, energy and a local food bit

**Assumed** · 2026-11-21 · scaffold

- **Statement.** The game draws labelled founder trees of three through seven holders, with the private supply and structural separation specified in its design. **Assumed** · 2026-11-22 · scaffold

### A223 · A fold of emergence is a made pattern that reads another and grows its own

**Assumed** · 2026-11-23 · interpretation

- **Statement.** The owner's picture, in the owner's words (23 November 2026): "I'd like to record DNA as something like 'intelligence' as an emergent structure/pattern. So things like DNA are kind of like patterns that observe a different pattern but also have their own growing patterns. This is generalized, so maybe DNA has some access to things like the golden ratios, and can output structures based on that." And: "structures like DNA or intelligence should maybe be exceptional, almost considered folds of emergence." Read in the program's terms, a "fold of emergence" is a pattern with three properties: the rules make it and nobody places it; what it makes depends on another pattern; and it makes a pattern of its own. Such patterns would be a kind apart from growth that only repeats or multiplies. No claim is made about DNA, cells or minds. Stated in the Intuitions guide, lesson 8.7 ([lesson](../INTUITIONS.md#87-generators-and-folds-of-emergence)).
- **Why.** Recorded at the owner's request of 23 November 2026, after the pictures of the golden-ratio eddy in the guide's Chapter 8 introduction.
- **Rests on it.** No result. It frames lesson 8.7 and the leads filed with it.
- **Challenge.** It would gain its first instance from one growth in which a made pattern takes one course or another according to what a second made pattern is, with nothing read or chosen from outside. It would lose its footing if ordinary growth and every law compared with it were shown to allow no such dependence. What the record has: packets that the growth makes and that release packets of their own, and a knot that receives 18 fresh distinctions where two packets are joined and 15 where they are kept apart, each found by comparing two prepared starts ([generator study](../research/GENERATORS-20261120-001/decision.md)); a stored value that steers no ordinary birth ([stored-bit study](../research/STORED-BIT-AND-NEXT-ACTION-20261122-001/decision.md), referee pending); and readers, learners and agents whose sensors and rules were all supplied. The cheapest first test: hang the same part on two rings that differ only in how many of their knots keep a leftover distinction, and ask whether anything the part makes, beyond its count of knots, tells the two rings apart.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-25 · The owner asked whether time is itself such a fold: "Is time itself a fold like intelligence/life through DNA?" Read in the program's terms, a pattern that counts another pattern's returns would take something from another pattern and make something of its own, and at each return it needs only whether the other has returned; whether that is enough for the fuller sense of this entry's test is not settled. No made pattern counts another in the record. Filed as sub-goal B329 and recorded under A228. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-25 · `PARTS-MEET-AGAIN-20261124-001` ([decision](../research/PARTS-MEET-AGAIN-20261124-001/decision.md), referee pending) gives the first cases in the record in which what one made part builds depends on whether another made part is there, under a stated law with nothing placed by hand after the start: 216 confirmed cases under laws in which a birth takes a distinction none of its parents holds (A245). They fall short of this entry's picture in two ways: what crosses is whether the other part is there and never what it is (16 pairs asked, none), and no part makes a pattern of its own from what it meets. Under the recorded laws in which a birth takes only what its parents hold there is no such dependence at all, by an argument. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-24 · `PARTS-MEET-AGAIN-20261124-001` opened at the owner's request of this date to look for a first instance: a law under which
    parts of the live roster that have come apart can meet again, tested by growing a start with and without a second part.
    It starts from the laws in which a birth takes a distinction that is already shared (A225, A230, A235). No result yet;
    status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · `RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001` ([decision](../research/RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001/decision.md), referee pending) marks where an instance cannot be: under ordinary growth and under the added rules of A224, once past the start the parts of the live roster act alone and never become joinable again, so nothing one part makes can depend on another. A first instance needs a law under which parts can meet again. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · Recorded, in the owner's words, as the owner's interpretation; framed by Intuitions lesson 8.7.

### A224 · Three added rules give a ring that returns and gives off parts

**Assumed** · 2026-11-23 · scaffold

- **Statement.** A pilot of 23 November 2026 changed ordinary growth by three rules added by hand. Cap: a new knot receives at most three fresh distinctions. Wait: a knot that owes more loose distinctions than it holds makes no join, every join that needs it is cancelled, and it stays live with what it holds, where the ordinary law stalls the whole round; the other knots make an offshoot from each distinction a cancelled join would have taken. Unused fillers: a fresh distinction that a knot receives only to make up three places or the least of one, and that no join of the next round asks for, is never used. Under the first two rules a ring of an odd number of knots, five or more, each sharing a distinction with the next and holding three loose distinctions, returns to its own live roster every two rounds while as many parts of two knots come apart from it as the ring has knots; a ring of four or of six does not return. Under all three each such part makes one more knot and then nothing, and the knots that can still make something repeat every two rounds; the knots that stop still hold a loose distinction that is never used. Stated in the Intuitions guide, lesson 8.7 ([lesson](../INTUITIONS.md#87-generators-and-folds-of-emergence)).
- **Why.** The owner asked for a way past the proved limit that a finite start cannot keep releasing one fixed finite packet under ordinary growth ([copies study](../research/COPIES-AND-MEETINGS-20261120-001/bounded.md)): "We need to break this limit. I'm imagining a system of balance. Similar to life and death cycles." Under ordinary growth no part of the live roster ends while another goes on, and its parts never come apart ([generator study](../research/GENERATORS-20261120-001/components.md)), so a rule had to be added.
- **Rests on it.** The pilot's result alone: computed by two independent programs, with an argument for every ring of four or more knots, not refereed. Nothing about ordinary growth rests on it, and the limit it was meant to pass still stands as a theorem about ordinary growth.
- **Challenge.** Swap each rule. In the pilot's search of 56 combinations of eight ways to ration fresh distinctions with seven ways to treat a knot that cannot pay, the returning ring appeared only where the short knot waits and the ration is the cap of three, one fewer than the ordinary count, or a flat three, and in none of the 56 did the parts that came apart end; the third rule was added for that, and it also ends every endless chain. Still to test: whether every knot made under these rules passes the knot test in the whole structure, the same starts under not-all-equal, other choices for what a cancelled join's distinctions do, and starts taken under a named weighting. The model would replace this scaffold if a law with a reason of its own gave the same return and release.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-24 · `GUARD-CAP-AND-HOLD-20261124-001` ([decision](../research/GUARD-CAP-AND-HOLD-20261124-001/decision.md), referee pending): under the two added rules as recorded (a cap of three, waiting, release), with and without the fillers, births made one at a time under the guard of A242 and A243 build what the complete rounds build in every order searched. With the release swapped for holding the guard fails. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · `COPYING-PARTS-20261123-001` ([decision](../research/COPYING-PARTS-20261123-001/decision.md), referee pending): under the rations FLOOR0, FLOOR1 and ONE-FEWER of the same family of added rules, with a short knot's joins cancelled, a part of two knots becomes two of the same every two rounds, at a rate set by the treatment; this needs neither waiting nor the release rule, and the fillers stop it. Every knot passes the knot test under both knot laws on the small starts, and under FLOOR1 in a census of 3,344 starts. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · `RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001` ([decision](../research/RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001/decision.md), referee pending) swapped each rule on real knots. The ring's return survives every cap from three to seven and fixed rations of three and four. It does not survive the loss of waiting, of offshoots, or of the release of what cancelled joins would have taken, which that study names as part of what was added. Every knot made passes the knot test under parity and not-all-equal. The fillers were swapped only against their absence. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · Recorded with the pilot. The owner's words are quoted in lesson 8.7 and in the sub-goal filed with it.

One birth of this kind placed by hand between two such parts does join them; it is labelled supplied. Status unchanged.

### A228 · The logic comes before time, and time would be a pattern like the others

**Assumed** · 2026-11-23 · interpretation

- **Statement.** The owner's interpretation, in the owner's words (23 November 2026): "Also, what if 'time' is a device or pattern just like the other ones? Are we able to simulate things 'over time'? So logic is prior to time as well as space." It continues his "Time doesn't have ticks" of 18 November 2026 (Pacific time; 19 November in UTC, the date of the first record that quotes it). Read in the program's terms: no clock is given. A history is one allowed state of one fixed system of rules, with a separate distinction for each thing at each step ([lesson 1.3](../INTUITIONS.md#13-emergence-in-the-game-of-life)). The only before and after the structure itself holds is that a knot comes after its parents. A clock is then a pattern among the others: one that returns, or one whose members carry a count, and time is what one pattern counts of another. Every round, tick and order of changes in the program's simulations is supplied by the one who runs them, so it is a scaffold until a pattern replaces it. No claim is made about physical time: an order of formation alone is not physical time. Stated beside the rounds reading of the Intuitions guide ([lesson 8.1](../INTUITIONS.md#81-growing-in-rounds), "Rounds are not a clock").
- **Why.** Recorded from the owner's question of 23 November 2026, asked after the ring that returns under added rules ([lesson 8.7](../INTUITIONS.md#87-generators-and-folds-of-emergence)). It is the sibling of A112, which says the same of space.
- **Rests on it.** No result. It frames lesson 8.1's reading of the rounds (A110's history of 19 November 2026), the clock studies read through A150, and the lead filed with this entry.
- **Challenge.** What the record has. Inside the proved class a knot's round is its cluster's size less one, so the rounds count size and nothing has to change over time ([round-free study](../research/ROUND-FREE-20261119-001/round_free.md), proved, refereed). Outside it the supplied order still matters: laws that make one join at a time and read only what is built made different things in different orders in 12 of 14 cases, and a law that reads only what is built and needs no tick is not known (same study). Recurring releases of a generator have been counted as ticks, two clocks in one structure of 21 knots ticking every 16 and every 24 parent steps, with the reader that identifies a tick supplied ([clock study](../research/FREEDOM-CLOCKS-AND-BIRTHS-20261120-001/decision.md), referee pending; A150); and between the same two receipts a prepared receiver counted three of its own ticks, and one when the start knots it could return to were exchanged ([receiver study](../research/TICK-BIRTHS-AND-SCARCITY-20261121-001/decision.md), referee pending). The interpretation would gain its first instance from a clock that no one supplies: a pattern that returns under every fair order of local changes, with no round given, and whose returns a second pattern made by the same growth counts. It would lose its footing if every law that needs no supplied order were shown to build nothing that returns or counts. The cheapest first test: run the ring that returns under the two added rules of A224 one knot at a time, in every fair order, and ask whether it still returns and gives off the same parts.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-25 · [TIME-AS-FOLD-20261125-001](../research/TIME-AS-FOLD-20261125-001/decision.md) (computed, referee pending): the one test made of "time is a pattern" in the sense of one made pattern counting another's returns found no such pattern. In 88,299 tests of a made clock and a second part, under a family of laws fixed first, no second part makes a fixed number of its own knots for each tick; a second part's course differs at each of three returns in 78 tests, each lost under some renumbering of its start. The study does not show that no law can hold a made count. Status unchanged: it remains the owner's interpretation.
  - 2026-11-25 · The owner asked: "Is time itself a fold like intelligence/life through DNA?" Read against this entry: an order (a knot after its parents) is not a pattern, and a clock (a pattern that returns) takes nothing in, so neither is a fold; a made count of returns, one pattern counting another's returns, has the form of his fold of emergence (A223), and it asks the least of any fold, since whether another part is there is what the study of parts that meet again found can cross between parts. Whatever counts in the record is supplied, and no law yet holds both a pattern that returns and a pattern that meets it at each return. Filed as sub-goal B329; the guide gathers the subject in its chapter on time. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-25 · `LEAST-RECORD-20261124-001` ([decision](../research/LEAST-RECORD-20261124-001/decision.md), referee pending): the before and after that births made one at a time need can be carried by a count of three values handed from parent to child. It cannot be read from what is built alone (the study before it), and here it is written by hand. Whether the structure can hold such a count itself is filed as sub-goal B328. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-24 · `LOCAL-ROUND-20261123-001` ([decision](../research/LOCAL-ROUND-20261123-001/decision.md), referee pending): the round inside a part need not be supplied either. With each birth recording its parents (A242), a knot that waits until the knots made alongside it have parents that have finished giving builds, one birth at a time and in every order searched, what the complete rounds build. The before and after that the structure holds, a knot after its parents, is then enough to stand in for the scheduler; the round is still what such a law replays. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · `RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001`, ticket C ([decision](../research/RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001/decision.md), referee pending): with the parts of the live roster taking their rounds one at a time in any order the same structure is built, and a knot's count of its own part's rounds is its round, so a count of rounds can belong to a part (726 runs and an argument). With one birth at a time and every knot reading again after each birth, the ring jams in every order. The round inside a part is still supplied (A237). Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · The owner refined the picture the same evening: time is "not really like a sequencer, but more like a priority system", with how often a part is updated set by a property of it. Recorded as A229, with its lead. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · Recorded, in the owner's words, as the owner's interpretation. Computed the same day, not refereed: in the golden-ratio eddy a knot made in round r with p parents and f fresh distinctions has r = f + 2p - 3 - 2e with e equal to 0, 1 or 2, so what one knot holds gives its round to within three values; and under the two added rules of A224 the ring that returns is made each time of the knots that waited.

### A229 · Time is a priority among updates, and what absorbs information samples the whole

**Assumed** · 2026-11-23 · interpretation

- **Statement.** The owner's picture, in the owner's words (23 November 2026): "Also, for the time thing, I feel like it's not really like a sequencer, but more like a priority system, if that makes sense. How often updates happen given some property (maybe mass impacts that). In fact, it's kind of like black holes are the limit of the compute, or the place that absorbs information, like a reader. It's almost like the distribution of black holes is a statistical way of getting a sample of the universe. Please record this idea. Then places with nothing going on need less simulation." Read in the program's terms, it says four things. Nothing steps every part at once: each part is updated as often as some property of it sets, so how often takes the place of when. A part with nothing going on is not updated at all. A reader is a part that takes in and gives nothing back, and the parts that take in the most are where the updates are densest, his "limit of the compute". And where the readers are is a weighting the structure supplies for itself, so a sample taken at the readers would be a sample of the whole. It refines A228. The literature has counterparts, named here once so that they can be found: event-driven simulation, which keeps the next changes in a queue by priority and spends nothing on quiet regions (for Conway's Life, Gosper's hashlife, 1984); clocks that run slower near mass in general relativity; and black holes as the limit on the information a region can hold (the literature's Bekenstein bound, 1981) and as the limiting case in Lloyd's "Ultimate physical limits to computation" (2000). No claim is made about physics, mass or black holes.
- **Why.** Recorded at the owner's request of 23 November 2026, after the question of time as a pattern (A228) and the ring that returns under added rules ([lesson 8.7](../INTUITIONS.md#87-generators-and-folds-of-emergence)).
- **Rests on it.** No result. It frames the lead filed with this entry.
- **Challenge.** What the record has. Growth touches only live knots, so a part with nothing loose costs nothing; under the three added rules of A224 the knots that can still make something stay between five and fifteen while the structure goes on growing. With the rounds taken away, the order of births matters and each order so far was supplied ([lesson 8.1](../INTUITIONS.md#81-growing-in-rounds), "Rounds are not a clock"; A168). Under one supplied priority, finishing the oldest funded births first, one tick of a clock cost 81 births alone, 253 with a second part attached that spends from the tick's own parents and 141 with one attached elsewhere that spends nothing there, while all three ticked once in every four parent steps ([receiver study](../research/TICK-BIRTHS-AND-SCARCITY-20261121-001/decision.md), referee pending): counted in births, the loaded clock is slower, and that study left a reading in terms of gravity open. Every weighting used to sample a growth was chosen by the study that used it ([lesson 8.3](../INTUITIONS.md#83-reading-a-blow-up-by-sampling)). The picture would gain its first instance from a priority read from the structure alone, neither an age nor an order we choose, under which what is built does not depend on how ties are broken and a part's own returns per birth fall as more is attached to it. It would gain a second from a sample taken at the parts that take in and make nothing, which gives the same counts as a sample of the whole under a named weighting. It would lose its footing if every priority read from the structure either builds what the rounds build or builds different things for different ways of breaking ties.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-24 · `LOCAL-ROUND-20261123-001` ([decision](../research/LOCAL-ROUND-20261123-001/decision.md), referee pending): with births made one at a time under a guard read from the structure, no order of updates has to be supplied inside a part, so any priority among the allowed births builds the same thing. What a knot must read to know it may act is four holder steps on the cycles tried, and more for each time a knot has to wait. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · `RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001`, ticket C ([decision](../research/RING-IN-BALANCE-20261123-001/decision.md), referee pending) counted the ring's returns in births under seven orders of the parts: slower and slower under even-handed orders while the parts it gave off keep growing, every 4n births with the largest part first (the parts given off are then never served), and steady under the three added rules. The orders are supplied (A237); nothing in the growth sets one or counts births. The sample at the readers was not attempted. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · Recorded, in the owner's words, as the owner's interpretation.

### A230 · A short knot pays the rest with shared distinctions (rule R)

**Assumed** · 2026-11-23 · scaffold

- **Statement.** As ordinary growth, except that a live knot that owes more loose distinctions than it holds pays the rest with distinctions it shares, each of which gains a holder; under parity a birth gets no fresh distinction only when the parity of what it takes is implied by the old knots.
- **Why.** It is the least wording of the move the third-holder epic found: a birth that takes a distinction already held twice.
- **Rests on it.** Every result of REREAD-BIRTHS-AND-MIXED-LAWS-20261123-001A and arm 1 of REREAD-BIRTHS-AND-MIXED-LAWS-20261123-001C.
- **Challenge.** Re-read where nothing is short: a one-parent birth that takes a shared distinction of its parent in place of a fresh one, or a spent knot that joins a neighbour's birth.
- **History.**
  - 2026-11-25 · `PARTS-MEET-AGAIN-20261124-001` ([decision](../research/PARTS-MEET-AGAIN-20261124-001/decision.md), referee pending) ran the rule as a treatment of a short knot under the rations of the ring that returns and of the parts that copy themselves, where knots are short every other round. It never makes parts that share nothing one part again (an argument: everything a birth takes is held by one of its parents; no exception in 504 runs and 1.94 million histories so far, every choice tried in 268 enumerations holding 239,866 of them and 100,000 taken at random under a fixed seed in each of 17 others), and from round 1 it undoes both objects: the ring never returns and nothing copies itself. Status unchanged.
  - 2026-11-23 · Recorded at the freeze. The rule forbids no old word, acts at round 1 only and never in a recorded source; no part with at most two holders gains a hidden excess under it. [Result](../research/REREAD-BIRTHS-AND-MIXED-LAWS-20261123-001A/result.md)

- **Why.** The rule does not say which; two fixed conventions and an enumeration bracket the choice.
- **Rests on it.** The counts of REREAD-BIRTHS-AND-MIXED-LAWS-20261123-001A that differ by convention, and the seven gains of REREAD-BIRTHS-AND-MIXED-LAWS-20261123-001C, the smallest of which appears under one convention only.
- **Challenge.** Find what in the structure could fix the choice, or read the choice as a setting.
